Tests: `tests/unit/domain/model/test_people_round2.py`, `tests/integration/graph_store/test_person_round2.py`. Existing person tests use valid 64-character hashes in place of the earlier placeholder strings.

6. **Staff names and free text (round 3, owner decision; verifier finding 2 of round 3).** `display_name` is a staff name, not patient data, so a plain name such as `Dr A Example` is accepted. The owner decided this. `Practitioner.__post_init__` refuses a postcode shape in `display_name`, using the pattern of DEC-047 item 1 (copied into `domain/model/people.py`, which does not import the messages module). A refusal is `ValidationFailed` naming `display_name` only; the value is never echoed. **Round 5 (verifier finding of round 4):** the NHS-number pattern of DEC-047 item 2 is removed from `check_staff_text`, because the digit-run rule of item 8 already refuses both NHS forms, `943 476 5919` and `9434765919`. Those two inputs stay in the tests as cases of the digit-run rule (`test_people_round4.py`, `contiguous-10` and `grouped-10`, and `test_people_round3.py`), so the NHS forms are still refused. The DEC-047 titled-name pattern is not applied: a staff name carries titles, and the owner's instruction names only the postcode and NHS-number shapes. Practitioner has no other free-text field. A run of ten or more digits is refused in `display_name` (items 8 and 9), so an eleven-digit phone number, contiguous or grouped, is refused too. **Superseded by item 11 (round 9):** the separator list is withdrawn. The separators the round-8 rule looked through were a space, a hyphen, a full stop, a round bracket and a plus sign (item 9). Item 11 counts digits in total instead. The earlier statement that such a number is accepted was wrong and is withdrawn. The phone hash is the only phone field, and the number itself never reaches the repository (DEC-065 item 3). Tests: `tests/unit/domain/model/test_people_round3.py`. Test ids are neutral (`raw-phone`, `full-email`, `postcode-shape`, `nhs-number-shape-spaced`) because a pytest id is built from its parameter, so a probe value must not be used as a parameter id (verifier finding 1 of round 3).

7. **Ids that hold a name (round 4, verifier finding 2 of round 4).** A pytest id is built from its parameter, so a staff name used as a parameter is echoed in the test id. The three staff names in `test_people_round3.py` now carry neutral ids (`staff-title-initial`, `staff-plain-name`, `staff-role-title`). Other test files under `backend/tests` use name-like values as auto-generated ids (see the round-4 evidence section). They belong to delivered tasks (D17, D25, D26) and are not changed on this branch; the owner should decide.

8. **A run of ten or more digits is refused in staff text (round 4, verifier finding 1 of round 4; owner's instruction).** **Superseded by item 11 and item 12 (history kept):** as first written in round 4, the rule allowed one space or hyphen between digits. The rule in force is item 11's, as normalised by item 12. The forms this item named (contiguous 11-digit, grouped 5-3-3, 3-3-5 and 3-4-4, hyphen-grouped, and the 10-digit NHS shape) are all still refused by item 11's count. A refusal is `ValidationFailed` naming `display_name` only, and the value is never echoed. A short number such as `Ward 4B` or `Bed 4 of 12` is a name and is accepted. This closes the residual gap recorded in item 6: an eleven-digit number inside a name is now refused. Tests: `tests/unit/domain/model/test_people_round4.py`.

9. **Separators in the digit run (round 8, verifier finding of round 7). Superseded by item 11 (round 9), and by item 12 as to normalisation (round 11).** History kept. The rule as written in round 8 was `(?<![0-9])[0-9](?:[ .()+-]*[0-9]){9,}(?![0-9])`: a run of ten or more digits is refused when its digits are separated by any run of these characters: a space, a hyphen, a full stop, a round bracket, or a plus sign. Forms refused include `(07700) 900 123`, `07700.900.123`, `+44 7700 900123` and `(0770) 090 0123`, as well as contiguous and grouped forms. A run of nine digits is accepted, and so are `Dr A Example`, `Ward 4B` and `Bed 4 of 12`. Any other character breaks the run. The digits are counted after the separators are skipped, so the separators do not count towards the ten. Tests: `tests/unit/domain/model/test_people_round8.py`, and `test_people_round4.py`.

10. **`now` is checked before any query (round 8, verifier finding of round 7).** `services_for_phone_hash` and `reporter_roles_for_service` call `check_aware("now", now)` before they run a query. A naive `now` raises `ValidationFailed` naming the field `now`, even on an empty graph. This is the correct choice over correcting DEC-064 item 15, because the rule should hold for every input, not only for data that happens to exist. Test: `tests/integration/graph_store/test_person_round8.py`. `check_aware` is the public name of the helper that was `_aware` in `domain/model/people.py`.

11. **A staff name holds at most nine digits in total (round 9, owner decision).** This replaces the separator rules of items 8 and 9, which are superseded by it. `check_staff_text` counts the Unicode decimal digits (category Nd, tested with `str.isdecimal()`) in the name, in any script, and refuses the name if ten or more remain. Every other character is ignored in the count. **Counting sentence superseded in part by item 12 (round 11):** the count now runs on normalised text, after format and combining characters are removed. **Round 10 (verifier finding of round 9):** the count was ASCII-only (`[0-9]`) before; it now covers every Nd digit, so eleven fullwidth or Arabic-Indic digits are refused. The postcode check runs on `unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", value)`, so a fullwidth postcode such as `ＳＷ１Ａ １ＡＡ` is refused. There is no separator list, so a phone number is refused in any punctuation or spacing, including a slash, an underscore, a tab, round brackets, a full stop, a plus sign and digits split across words (`Shift 2026 10 26 ref 12` has ten digits and is refused). A name with nine or fewer digits in total is accepted (`943476591`, `Ward 4B`, `Room 12B`, `Bed 4 of 12`, `Dr A Example`, `Sharon Smith`). A refusal is `ValidationFailed` naming `display_name` only, and the value is never echoed. The postcode refusal of item 6 is unchanged. Tests: `tests/unit/domain/model/test_people_round9.py` and `test_people_round10.py`. Residual, by the owner's rule and recorded for the owner: a name with nine digits in total is accepted, so a phone number with one digit missing, or a nine-digit reference, is accepted as a staff name.


12. **Staff text is normalised before any check (round 11, owner decision; verifier finding of round 10).** Order: `unicodedata.normalize("NFKD")`, then remove every character whose category is `Cf` (format, such as a zero-width space) or `Mn` (combining mark), then `unicodedata.normalize("NFKC")`. Only then are the Unicode decimal digits counted (`str.isdecimal()`, item 11) and the postcode pattern run. This stops superscripts, circled digits, combining marks and zero-width spaces from hiding digits or a postcode. **Deviation from the owner's stated order, recorded for the owner:** the owner wrote NFKC first, then removal of `Cf` and `Mn`. That order leaves `SW1Á 1AA` (written with a combining acute accent) unrefused, because NFKC has already composed `A` plus the combining acute into the single character `Á`, which is no longer a combining mark. Decomposing first (NFKD) keeps the combining mark visible to the removal, and the final NFKC restores composed text for the checks. The refusal the owner asked for holds. The postcode check runs on NFKC-normalised text, as item 11 says. Tests: `tests/unit/domain/model/test_people_round11.py`, and the round-10 tests.

13. **Phone hash and email domain are ASCII by design and are not normalised (round 11).** `check_phone_hash` (`[0-9a-f]{64}`) and `check_email_domain` (`[a-z0-9-]+` with dots) are not passed through item 12's normalisation. A fullwidth or Arabic-Indic digit in a hash, or a fullwidth full stop in a domain, is refused, not folded into an ASCII character and then accepted. Normalising the hash would make a non-ASCII value look like a valid hash, which is the opposite of what the check is for. Tests: `tests/unit/domain/model/test_people_round11.py` (fullwidth and Arabic-Indic hashes refused, 64 ASCII lowercase hex accepted, fullwidth-dot domain refused).

## DEC-066 Case repository on Neo4j: stub, request and scores (P21)
Status: accepted (builder, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: P21 (`adapters/outbound/graph_store/case_repository.py`, `application/ports/outbound/repositories.py`, `adapters/outbound/graph_store/extensions.py`), U11 to U14 (case use cases), U12 (`CRITERIA_SEEN`), U35 (date-range reads, not built here), R07, D18 `audit_summary`.

Numbered DEC-066: DEC-065 is the highest number on main and on every task/* branch when this was written (checked with `git show <branch>:docs/delivery/decisions.md` for each branch, lesson L43).

The P21 prompt leaves these points open, or cannot be built as written on the store and mapper:

1. **The postcode is a Patient property, not a FHIR element.** `Patient.address` is not a mapped element (DEC-053 item 13), so `postalCode` is set on the Patient node after the put (`Q-CASE-SET-POSTCODE`), with null when the case is closed. Every put replaces the node's properties (DEC-053), so a closed case loses the postcode on save. `case_to_fhir` still produces `address[0].postalCode` and `case_from_fhir` reads it, so the FHIR form is complete. The repository moves the value between the two forms.
2. **The stub's `meta` is not stored.** The store refuses `meta` on a put (DEC-053 item 10). `case_to_fhir` keeps `meta.tag` (`pseudonymised-case-stub`) and `meta.security` (`R`) in its output; the repository drops them before the put, so they are not in the graph and do not come back on a read. They are fixed for every stub. Owner decision: store them (for example as Concept relationships) or keep them as an output rule only.
3. **Urgency is stored as an extension, as well as priority.** `priority` cannot be reversed: `within-2-hrs` gives `urgent`, and `today` and `planned` both give `routine`. The code is kept in the extension `urgency` so that a read returns the exact case. `case_to_fhir` always sets `priority` from urgency; no urgency means no priority.
4. **Closing time, outcome and best-fit reason are extensions** (`closedAt`, `outcome`, `bestFitNotUsed`), and the age band is the extension `ageBand` on the Patient stub. `fhir-data-model.md` gives the age band as a concept from the age-band system; the extension registry holds primitive values only (no CodeableConcept), so the code is stored as a string. Owner to confirm. Five extensions are registered in `extensions.py`, and the registry test pins twenty-two names (lesson L66).
5. **Needs.** Each need is a `reasonCode` (a Concept node in the need-category system), with `REASON_CODE` to the request. Closing clears the postcode and the needs, so the request's `REASON_CODE` relationships are gone after close (the store clears a root's outgoing relationships on every put, DEC-053 item 6). The Concept nodes are shared vocabulary (DEC-053 item 5) and are not deleted; they carry no link to a patient once the relationship is gone. The prompt's "no needs anywhere in the graph" is read as "no link from a stub, request or score to a need". Owner to confirm.
6. **The reference.** The case reference is an identifier (system `https://diucs.example/case-reference`) and a copy in `ServiceRequest.reference`, set after the put (the DEC-063 item 2 pattern). The shared Identifier node is not deleted at close (DEC-053 item 17c). It is a case reference, not a postcode or a need, so it is kept. Owner to confirm.
7. **Scores are Observations, written once.** Ids are `sc-<case id>-<n>` in recorded order. News2, clinical frailty and ambulance category are `valueInteger`. Manchester triage and Pathways Dx are `valueCodeableConcept`, in the local systems `https://diucs.example/CodeSystem/manchester-triage` and `https://diucs.example/CodeSystem/pathways-dx`. `contracts.md` names no code system for either, so the owner should add them or replace them with the NHS systems. The store refuses to put an existing Observation (DEC-053 item 3), so `add` writes the scores and `save` never rewrites them.
8. **Immutability on save.** `save` refuses a change to the scores (`ValidationFailed`), a change to the reference (`ValidationFailed`), and any change to a closed case (`InvalidTransition`). All three are checked before the first write, so a caller that catches the error and commits writes nothing. Tests: one for each, and each was seen to fail when its check was removed (mutation check in the evidence file).
9. **Add.** The Patient is written first. The store refuses an existing Patient with `Conflict` before it writes anything (expected version None), so a second add stops on its first write. Sixteen racing adds give one winner (the store's lock, DEC-053 item 4). Lesson L77: the add-twice test and the racing test each pin a different thing, and neither is a substitute for the other.
10. **References are not unique in the graph.** `get_by_reference` returns the first case by id. Nothing in this task stops two cases holding one reference, and `domain-rules.md` section 4 is silent on it. The use case that opens a case (U11) must allocate a reference that no other case holds, as `new_reference(rng, taken)` does for bookings (lesson L29). Residual, not fixed here.
11. **Contract names.** `contracts.md` lists `CaseRepository` under P02, but P02 merged without it, and P21 owns the port and the adapter. This task does not edit `docs/spec/`, so `contracts.md` is not updated for `CaseRepository` or for the five extension names. The owner should add them.
12. **No migration and no index on `ServiceRequest.reference`.** A lookup by reference scans the request nodes, the same residual as DEC-053 item 14a. Acceptable at pilot size.
13. **occurrenceDateTime is a spec gap (corrected in DEC-068).** The prompt does not mention it (checked by grep of `docs/plan` and `docs/spec`). It appears only in `docs/spec/fhir-data-model.md` line 218, in the ServiceRequest property list. `Case` has no field for it, so it is not written. This is for the owner to reconcile (add a field to `Case`, D18, or drop the property from the spec). It is not a prompt requirement and is not added to the code.
14. **Lesson L78 does not apply.** P21 writes no local relationship: every relationship it writes is mapped and carries a `fhirPath`, so the loader does not skip any of them.

Tests: `backend/tests/unit/adapters/outbound/graph_store/test_case_mapping.py` (the pure mapping, the identity and guard refusals), `backend/tests/integration/graph_store/test_case_repository.py` (the stored case: round trips, closing, reference lookup, add and save guards, sixteen racing adds), and `test_extensions.py` (the registry, now twenty-two names).

## DEC-067 Owner decisions on the P21 verification failure: district only, reference format, refusals and a unique reference (P21, round 2)
Status: accepted (owner decisions, recorded by build-P21) · Affects: P21 (`adapters/outbound/graph_store/case_repository.py`, `adapters/outbound/graph_store/migrations.py`, the case tests), DEC-066 items 1, 3, 9, 10 and 13, U11 (the case use case must pass a district and a valid reference), DEC-041 (reference format).

Numbered DEC-067: DEC-065 is the highest on main and on every task/* branch, DEC-066 is P21's first entry on its branch, and no branch claims DEC-067 (checked with `git show <branch>:docs/delivery/decisions.md` for every branch, lesson L43). The migration id 0022 is the next free one: 0021 is the highest on every branch (lesson L68).

1. **Only the postcode district is stored (owner decision).** The Patient holds the district from D19 (`postcode_district`), not the full postcode. A full postcode, or any value that is not a canonical district (`normalise_district(value) == value`), is refused with `ValidationFailed` naming `postcode` only, before any write. This overrides the prompt's "address.postalCode" wording and DEC-066 item 1's full-postcode reading. The reason is that matching and criteria use districts (DEC-022), so a district is all the graph needs. U11 must derive the district before it builds the Case. Tests: `test_a_full_postcode_is_refused_and_the_value_is_not_echoed`, `test_a_postcode_that_is_not_a_district_is_refused` (three values), `test_a_full_postcode_is_refused_and_writes_nothing`.
2. **Reference format (owner decision).** A case reference is four digits with no leading zero, the DEC-041 format used by `events.py`. It is refused in `case_to_fhir`, which runs before any write. A reference in NHS-number shape is refused. Tests: `test_a_reference_outside_the_four_digit_format_is_refused` (five values, one of them NHS-shaped) and `test_a_reference_outside_the_four_digit_format_is_refused_and_writes_nothing`.
3. **Unknown elements are refused, not dropped (owner decision, DEC-049).** `case_from_fhir` refuses any key outside an allowlist on the Patient, the ServiceRequest and each score. The identity elements (`name`, `identifier`, `birthDate`, `telecom`) are refused by the same allowlist, so the separate identity check from round 1 was removed as unreachable. Tests: `test_an_element_the_mapping_cannot_represent_is_refused_not_dropped` (Patient, request and score). The identity test is kept as a behaviour test.
4. **Priority and status are checked against the urgency and the close time (owner decision).** `priority` must be `urgent` for within-2-hrs and `routine` for today or planned. A priority without an urgency is refused. The status must be `completed` exactly when `closedAt` is present. Tests: `test_a_priority_that_does_not_match_the_urgency_is_refused` (three cases), `test_a_priority_without_an_urgency_is_refused`, `test_an_open_case_whose_status_is_completed_is_refused`, `test_a_closed_case_whose_status_is_active_is_refused`.
5. **An outcome without a close time is refused (owner decision).** Test: `test_an_outcome_without_a_close_time_is_refused`.
6. **Closed-case residue is refused, not cleared (owner decision, choosing refusal).** `case_to_fhir` refuses a case that has a close time and still holds a postcode or needs. A save of such a case therefore writes nothing, and the stored open case keeps its postcode and reason codes until a proper `Case.close`. `Case.close` clears both, so the close path is unchanged. Tests: `test_a_closed_case_saved_with_postcode_or_needs_left_is_refused` (two cases: postcode left, needs left).
7. **A reference is unique in the graph (owner decision; overrides DEC-066 item 10).** Migration `0022_case_reference_id` adds `CREATE CONSTRAINT case_reference_id IF NOT EXISTS FOR (n:ServiceRequest) REQUIRE n.reference IS UNIQUE`. Three layers guard it, each with its own test:
   - the pre-check in `add` (`Q-CASE-BY-REF`) raises `Conflict` before any write. Test: `test_a_second_case_with_the_same_reference_is_refused_and_writes_nothing` (the refusal is swallowed and committed; one Patient remains);
   - the constraint itself. Test: `test_the_case_reference_is_unique_in_the_graph` (a raw duplicate `CREATE` raises `ClientError`);
   - the mapping of a constraint error raised by a racing write to `Conflict`. The pre-check is bypassed in the test, so the constraint is the only guard that can fire. Test: `test_a_duplicate_reference_that_passes_the_check_is_a_conflict`.
   The migration count in `test_driver.py` rises from 17 to 18, and `test_migration_ids.py` pins the new id after 0021 (lesson L75).
   **A racing test was written and removed.** Concurrent adds of one reference all share the same Identifier node and Concept nodes, so the store's existing constraints decide that race, not the reference constraint. The test passed on the red run for that reason, so it did not prove the guard (lesson L77).
8. **A need coded in another system is refused.** Test: `test_a_need_coded_in_another_system_is_refused`. The check is in `service_repository.codeable_from_fhir`, which is shared with P01, and was not changed.
9. **DEC-066 item 13 is corrected** (superseded in wording by DEC-068): `occurrenceDateTime` is a spec gap in `fhir-data-model.md` line 218, not a prompt requirement. The earlier text said the prompt listed it; that was wrong.
10. **Residuals.** The `ClientError` to `Conflict` mapping in item 7 is a backstop; it has its own test. The identity check is replaced by the allowlist (item 3). The district check does not run when a case is read back, because nothing that fails it can be written.

Tests: `backend/tests/unit/adapters/outbound/graph_store/test_case_mapping.py`, `backend/tests/unit/adapters/outbound/graph_store/test_migration_ids.py`, `backend/tests/integration/graph_store/test_case_repository.py`, `backend/tests/integration/graph_store/test_driver.py`.

## DEC-068 Write-path checks for every P21 refusal, and the occurrenceDateTime correction (P21, round 3)
Status: accepted (builder, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: P21 (`adapters/outbound/graph_store/case_repository.py`, the case tests), DEC-066 item 13, DEC-067 item 9, and any use case that writes a case (U11 to U14).

Numbered DEC-068: DEC-067 is the highest on main and every task/* branch when checked. No branch claims DEC-068.

1. **Outcome without a close time is refused on the write path (verify-P21-r2 blocking finding).** `case_to_fhir` now refuses it, so `add` and `save` both refuse before any write. The read-side check in `case_from_fhir` stays. Tests: `test_an_outcome_without_a_close_time_is_refused_on_add_and_writes_nothing` (zero nodes after a swallowed and committed refusal) and `test_an_outcome_without_a_close_time_is_refused_on_save_and_the_stored_case_is_kept`.
2. **Every round-2 refusal now has a write-path test, or a reason it cannot be written.**
   - Full postcode: refused on `add` (round 2) and on `save` (`test_a_full_postcode_is_refused_on_save_and_the_stored_case_is_kept`, new). This test passed on the red run because the check already ran in `case_to_fhir`, which `save` calls. It was confirmed by removing the check (mutant killed).
   - Reference format: refused on `add` with zero nodes (round 2). On `save` the reference cannot change, so the immutability guard refuses a changed reference first, and a stored reference is always well formed.
   - Closed case with residue: refused on `add` (`test_a_closed_case_with_residue_is_refused_on_add_and_writes_nothing`, new, passed on the red run for the same reason as the postcode) and on `save` (round 2).
   - Duplicate reference: refused on `add` by the pre-check and the constraint (round 2). `save` never changes the reference.
   - Outcome without close time: item 1.
   - Priority/urgency, status/close time, unknown elements, need coding system: these are read-side checks in `case_from_fhir`. The write path cannot produce a mismatch, because `case_to_fhir` derives `priority` from urgency, derives `status` from the close time, emits only the elements it knows, and checks the code system when it builds the code. The read-side tests therefore stand for these. Owner note: if a second writer is ever added that bypasses `case_to_fhir`, these would need write-path tests.
3. **occurrenceDateTime (DEC-066 item 13, DEC-067 item 9).** The prompt does not mention it. It appears only in `docs/spec/fhir-data-model.md` line 218. The correction in DEC-066 item 13 and DEC-067 item 9 says the prompt listed it; that was wrong. It is a spec gap for the owner to reconcile (add a field to `Case`, D18, or drop the property from the spec). It is not added to the code.

Tests: `backend/tests/integration/graph_store/test_case_repository.py` (the four round-3 tests above).


## DEC-069 U08 needs P06 and P14 before it can be built; the ledger's dependency list is incomplete (U08)
Status: accepted (orchestrator) · Affects: U08 (`application/use_cases/query_board.py`), P06, P14, `docs/plan/tasks.json` (not edited).

Numbered DEC-069: DEC-068 is the highest on main and on every task/* branch checked.

1. **Dependencies.** The U08 prompt uses `QueryBoard(uow_factory, clock)` and tests 5 needs `FlagRepository.services_with_flag("suspended")`. Those come from P06 (UnitOfWork) and P14 (flags), which the ledger does not list. The ledger dependency list (P02, D07, D08) is therefore incomplete. The source plan is not edited; this decision is the record.
2. **Decision: wait, do not reduce scope.** Dropping the UoW or the flag test would change the `QueryBoard` constructor named in `docs/spec/contracts.md`, which is a shared contract. U08 stays `blocked` until P06 and P14 are delivered.
3. **Sequencing.** P14 touches `application/ports/outbound/repositories.py`, which P04 is also editing. P14 starts after P04 is delivered. P06 has a long dependency chain (P21, P13, G14 and others); it is not started by this decision.
4. **Lesson.** L80 (published by build-U08) records the same gap for the other sessions.

Tests: none. This is a planning decision.

## DEC-070 FastAPI and httpx for the FHIR REST app; the critical path to P06 (R01, U08)
Status: accepted (orchestrator) · Affects: R01 (`adapters/inbound/fhir_rest/`), `backend/pyproject.toml`, `backend/uv.lock`, P06, P13, P14, U08.

Numbered DEC-070: DEC-069 is the highest on main and on every task/* branch checked.

1. **FastAPI and httpx are approved.** The spec names FastAPI (`docs/spec/architecture.md`). DEC-003 says it is a default that may be used or replaced. R01 uses it, so it is the default and is kept. httpx is the test client for it. Both are added to `backend/pyproject.toml` and `backend/uv.lock` on the R01 branch, when R01 resumes, so the lock change is reviewed with R01 and not on main. They are not added to main now.
2. **Lock-file sequencing.** Only one branch may change `backend/uv.lock` at a time. Whoever adds a dependency first (R01 by default) rebases the others on it. Other R-tasks that need the same packages reuse this change.
3. **Critical path to P06.** P06 needs P04 (in progress, build-P04), P13 (not started) and P14 (not started). P13 and P14 both edit `application/ports/outbound/repositories.py`, as P04 does, so they build one after another after P04 is delivered. U08 and R01 both wait for P06. Nothing on this path can be built in parallel with another edit of `repositories.py`.
4. **R01 test clock.** The prompt's test clock is 13:12Z, while F08's TEST_NOW is 13:00Z (build-R01 noted this). R01 passes the clock explicitly. It does not change F08.

Tests: none. This is a planning decision; the dependency change is tested with R01.
## DEC-071 Storage shape of contacts, PIN credentials and devices (P16)
Status: accepted (builder, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: P16 (`adapters/outbound/graph_store/contact_store.py`, `credential_store.py`, `adapters/testing/memory_code_store.py`, `application/ports/outbound/identity.py`, migrations 0023 and 0024); P06 (registration of the three stores); I10, I13, U16, U25, U32, U37, and any task that reads a phone, a PIN state or a device.

Numbered DEC-071: DEC-070 is on main (FastAPI and httpx, lesson L83), so the next free number is 071. Checked against every branch with `git show <branch>:docs/delivery/decisions.md`; no branch claims 071 or 072.

The P16 prompt leaves these points open, or cannot be built as written:

1. **[Item 1 superseded in part by DEC-073 item 1: a unique `key` now prevents the duplicate.]** Contacts are keyed by role id as a property, and are not linked to the role.** `(:ProtectedContact {roleId, kind, value, updatedAt})` has one node per role and kind (`phone` or `email`), written by MERGE. DEC-001 would make `roleId` a relationship. It stays a property on purpose: architecture.md keeps the number out of the readable graph, and a relationship from a contact to a PractitionerRole would let a traversal of the FHIR graph reach it. The prompt names this shape and its index (migration 0024), so it is followed. Residual: Community Neo4j has no composite uniqueness, so two first puts for one role and kind in racing transactions can create two nodes, and `get` then returns one of them. Contacts are written by registration, one actor at a time; this is accepted and recorded here.
2. **The phone is stored as given, trimmed.** `value` holds the number the caller supplies. The protected store is the only place a number may be stored in plain text (P10 prompt: "Phone numbers are never stored in plain text outside the protected contact store"). Lookups use the `PhoneHasher` hash (P10), not this value. Refused before the write: a blank phone, and an email without exactly one `@` and a dotted domain. A refusal names the field only and never echoes the value. Encryption at rest is not built. Whether the protected store needs it, and its retention, are information-governance decisions (human-required, see DEC-072 item 4).
3. **The PIN lock has no end time, so `lockedUntil` is not stored.** DEC-044 item 2 says a locked PIN stays locked until `reset_pin_state` after a verified text code. `PinState` has no lock time, so the credential stores `failed` (the count), `locked` (a boolean) and `lastOutcome`. The prompt's `lockedUntil` would hold a value the domain never produces. The test "save_state with five failures and a lock persists the lock" asserts `locked`.
4. **A device is linked to its practitioner by a relationship, not by a `practitionerId` property.** `(:Practitioner)-[:HAS_DEVICE]->(:Device {id, status, registeredAt, revokedAt})`. The prompt's `practitionerId` property would be a second copy of a reference, which DEC-001 forbids. `Device.id` is unique in the graph (migration 0023). A device id that already belongs to another practitioner is `Conflict`. Registering a device again for the same practitioner changes nothing, so a revoked device stays revoked: `known` stays false after `revoke_all`, and a later `register` does not bring it back. A device is re-enrolled only under a new id.
5. **A credential is linked to its practitioner by `HAS_CREDENTIAL`, keyed by `deviceId` under that practitioner.** `set_pin` and `register` refuse an unknown practitioner with `NotFound` before any write. `set_pin` on an existing device replaces the hash and starts the state fresh (no failures, not locked). Who may call it (a verified text code) is decided by U32.
6. **Times written by the store are written by the database in UTC.** The store ports take no `now` (the prompt's signatures), so `updatedAt` and `revokedAt` use `datetime({timezone: 'UTC'})` in Cypher. `registeredAt` is the caller's `at`, converted to UTC. A naive `at` is `ValidationFailed`. Tests assert these are Neo4j `DateTime` values, not text (DEC-001).
7. **Text-code expiry in the fake.** `InMemoryCodeStore.get` returns the code while `issued_at <= now < issued_at + CODE_VALID_FOR` (ten minutes). That matches `code_attempt` in `identity_policy.py`, which expires the code at the same instant. A code whose issue time is in the future is dropped (fail closed, DEC-044 item 4). A naive issue time is `ValidationFailed`. The fake stands in for Redis (I13), which is not a record store, so DEC-004 does not forbid it.
8. **The ports follow the P16 prompt, not `contracts.md` line 123.** The prompt gives `CodeSender.send_code(phone, code)`, `PinStore` with `set_pin`, `get`, `save_state` and `clear_all`, and `DeviceRegistry.register(practitioner_id, device_id, at)`. `contracts.md` line 123 lists `send_code(phone)`, and a `PinStore` with `set_pin`, `verify_pin`, `attempts` and `lock`. The prompt is followed because it is the task's own instruction and gives full signatures. `docs/spec/` is not edited here. The owner should reconcile `contracts.md` line 123 with these names, or supersede this item.
9. **Not done: registering the three stores in `DEFAULT_FACTORIES`, and a `uow_factory` fixture.** The prompt asks to register `contacts`, `pins` and `devices` in `DEFAULT_FACTORIES` (P06). P06 is not delivered and is not a dependency of P16 (its deps are P10 and P03). No unit of work or factory registry exists on main, so there is nothing to register in. P06 must add the three stores when it lands. The tests use the store classes directly inside `write_tx`, as the P10 and P03 tests do.

Tests: `backend/tests/integration/graph_store/test_contact_store.py`, `backend/tests/integration/graph_store/test_credential_store.py`, `backend/tests/unit/adapters/testing/test_memory_code_store.py`, `backend/tests/unit/adapters/outbound/graph_store/test_migration_ids.py`, `backend/tests/integration/graph_store/test_driver.py` (migration count 20). Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/P16.md`.

## DEC-072 PIN attempts read under the write lock; scope of the protected store (P16)
Status: accepted (builder, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: P16 (`credential_store.py`), U32 (PIN use cases), U25 (text-code use cases), I13 (Redis code and PIN counters), and any use case that reads a credential and saves it again.

Numbered DEC-072, the second entry of P16.

1. **`get` on a credential takes the credential's write lock.** Each PIN attempt is a read, a judgement and a save. If the read takes no lock, two concurrent attempts can read the same failure count, and both are allowed. Measured with 16 concurrent wrong PINs, each in its own unit of work: without the lock all 16 answered `wrong`; with the lock, 4 answered `wrong`, the fifth and the rest answered `locked`, and the stored state is 5 failures and locked. `Q-CRED-GET` therefore runs `SET c.deviceId = c.deviceId` before it reads (lesson L73). It is registered in write mode, and the tests read a credential with `write_tx`.
2. **U32 must run the read, the judgement and the save in one unit of work.** The lock is held until the transaction commits. A caller that reads in one unit of work and saves in another gets no protection. `save_state` raises `NotFound` if the credential was cleared between the read and the save.
3. **The raw PIN never reaches the store.** `set_pin` takes the keyed hash. Hashing, keying and the six-digit check stay in the use case and `validate_pin_format` (DEC-044 item 6). The test "the raw PIN is in no stored property" scans every node.
4. **The done-when scan and the protected store.** The prompt says "no phone number or raw PIN appears in any stored node property". Read literally it contradicts the protected store, whose one job is to hold the number (P10 prompt; the `value` property of `ProtectedContact`). The scan in `test_contact_store.py` therefore checks that the number appears in exactly one node, a `ProtectedContact`, and in no other label. The PIN scan covers every node. Encryption at rest, retention and who may read the protected store are not decided here. They are human-required items for information governance and the owner, and they stay pending.

Tests: `backend/tests/integration/graph_store/test_credential_store.py::test_sixteen_concurrent_wrong_pins_lock_the_device_at_five_failures`, and `backend/tests/integration/graph_store/test_contact_store.py::test_the_number_is_held_only_by_the_protected_contact_node`.

## DEC-073 Racing writes on contacts, credentials and devices (P16, verifier round)
Status: accepted (builder, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: P16 (`contact_store.py`, `credential_store.py`, migration 0025), DEC-071 items 1 and 4 (item 1 is superseded in part, item 4 is extended), U25, U32, U37 and any task that writes a contact, a credential or a device.

Numbered DEC-073: DEC-070 is the highest number on main and every branch checked, and DEC-071 and DEC-072 are this branch's. Checked with `git show <branch>:docs/delivery/decisions.md` for every branch.

The verifier (verify-P16) raised three racing-write findings. Each was measured before a decision was taken. Each test is in the suite and runs against the code described here.

1. **Protected contacts: a unique key (superseding DEC-071 item 1 in part).** Measured: sixteen racing first writes of one role's phone produced sixteen `ProtectedContact` nodes (red run, `test_racing_first_puts_for_one_role_leave_one_protected_contact`). DEC-071 item 1 accepted this, and the verifier rightly said that acceptance does not hold: a second number for a role is a wrong recipient for a clinical message (H-09, H-13 family). Fix: each node has `key = roleId + "|" + kind`, unique in the graph (migration 0025, a single-property constraint that Community Neo4j supports). The prompt's properties `roleId`, `kind`, `value`, `updatedAt` and the index on `roleId` are kept; `key` is one added property derived from them, and the key is unambiguous because `kind` is always `phone` or `email`. A losing racing write is `Conflict`, and it stores nothing. Tests: `test_contact_store.py::test_racing_first_puts_for_one_role_leave_one_protected_contact` (16 racers: one node, every outcome is `stored` or `conflict`), `tests/unit/adapters/outbound/graph_store/test_migration_ids.py::test_the_protected_contact_key_migration_is_0025_after_the_contact_index`.

2. **Same practitioner, racing registrations of one device: a no-op, not a refusal (extends DEC-071 item 4).** The prompt is silent on re-registration. Measured before the fix: sixteen racing registrations of one device by one practitioner raised `ConstraintError` for fifteen of them, because each passed the owner check before the first commit. Decision: the same practitioner's racing registrations are one registration and none is refused. The register path locks the practitioner node first (`Q-DEVICE-LOCK`, which is also the existence check), so the second racer waits, then sees the committed device, and the owner check makes it a no-op. A different practitioner racing for the same device id still gets `Conflict` from the unique constraint on `Device.id`. Tests: `test_credential_store.py::test_racing_register_by_the_same_practitioner_succeeds_and_leaves_one_device` and `::test_racing_registrations_of_one_device_leave_one_device_node` (two practitioners: one registered, the other `Conflict`).

3. **[Superseded by DEC-074.] Credentials: the race was not reproduced, so no code change; accepted with the test as proof.** The verifier's conjecture was that two racing `set_pin` calls can create two `Credential` nodes. Measured on the harness Neo4j, sixteen racing `set_pin` calls for one practitioner and device left exactly one node in three runs out of three (`test_racing_set_pin_for_one_device_leaves_one_credential`). The reason is that `Q-CRED-SET` creates the `HAS_CREDENTIAL` relationship from the practitioner node, and the engine locks that node while it creates the relationship, so the racing writes serialise. The residual is that this depends on the engine's locking, not on a uniqueness constraint, which Community Neo4j cannot give for a (practitioner, deviceId) pair. If a duplicate ever appears, the failure is a refusal and not silent: `save_state` matches two nodes and raises `NotFound` (it does not pick one), and `clear_all` removes both. The test is kept as a regression guard and runs on every full pass. Re-run it on any Neo4j version change. Not changed: no explicit lock was added, because the test shows the behaviour without it and an unmeasured change would be a guess.

4. **The PIN read lock is unchanged (DEC-072 item 1).** Its test still fails without the lock (see evidence, mutation check).

Tests for this entry: `backend/tests/integration/graph_store/test_contact_store.py`, `backend/tests/integration/graph_store/test_credential_store.py`, `backend/tests/unit/adapters/outbound/graph_store/test_migration_ids.py`, `backend/tests/integration/graph_store/test_driver.py` (migration count 21). Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/P16.md`.

## DEC-074 One PIN credential per device, enforced by the schema; reads fail closed (P16, round 3)
Status: accepted (builder, for the owner to confirm; decision of the orchestrator on the round-2 verification) · Affects: P16 (`credential_store.py`, migration 0026), DEC-073 item 3 (superseded), DEC-044 items 1 and 2 (lockout), U32 (PIN use cases), I13, and any task that writes a credential.

Numbered DEC-074: DEC-073 is the highest on main and on every branch checked (`git show <branch>:docs/delivery/decisions.md`), so 074 is free.

1. **DEC-073 item 3 is superseded.** That item accepted the Credential race because sixteen racers left one node in each of nine runs. A duplicate Credential defeats the PIN lockout, which is a patient-access control. Absence of an observed failure in test runs proves nothing, so the risk is now prevented by the schema, not by a count of clean runs.
2. **Schema: `deviceId` is unique on `Credential`** (migration 0026, `CREATE CONSTRAINT credential_device_id ... REQUIRE n.deviceId IS UNIQUE`). The choice of `deviceId` alone, not a practitioner and device key: a device id is already unique in the graph (`Device.id`, DEC-071 item 4); the prompt binds a PIN to a device; and a single-property constraint is what Community Neo4j supports. The prompt's Credential properties are unchanged, so no key property is added.
3. **Consequence: one PIN per device, whoever sets it.** A second practitioner's `set_pin` for a device that already has a PIN is `Conflict` and writes nothing. Which practitioner may use a device is registration's decision (U10), not this store's.
4. **`get` fails closed.** A read that returns more than one credential for a device raises `Conflict` and does not pick a row. Before this, `rows[0]` was read in unspecified order, so a PIN could be checked against the wrong hash, and `save_state` then refused, so the lock could not engage.
5. **A constraint error on `set_pin` is `Conflict`.** The failing write stores nothing, so the caller can retry or refuse. Constraint errors on contacts were already mapped this way (DEC-073 item 1).
6. **Proof is deterministic, not a race run.** Tests: `test_credential_store.py::test_a_second_credential_for_one_device_is_refused_by_the_schema` (a raw second CREATE raises `ConstraintError`), `::test_a_pin_for_a_device_of_another_practitioner_is_a_conflict`; `tests/unit/adapters/outbound/graph_store/test_credential_store_errors.py::test_a_read_that_finds_two_credentials_fails_closed` and `::test_a_constraint_error_on_set_pin_is_a_conflict`. Each applies the schema itself first (`apply_migrations`), so the result does not depend on test order (lesson L92). The racing `set_pin` test was deleted: it could not fail with the constraint in place and had shown nothing without it.

Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/P16.md`.

## DEC-075 Contract suites for the cache and event bus: harness shape, scope and timing (P12)
Status: accepted (builder decision, owner to reconcile) · Affects: P12; I01 (Redis cache contract factory); I02 (Service Bus contract factory); I05 and U08 (live-update consumers of the cache). Numbered DEC-075: DEC-074 is the highest number on main and on every `task/*` branch (checked with `git show <branch>:docs/delivery/decisions.md`).

The P12 prompt fixes the suites' names and their main cases but leaves these choices open. The builder decided them as follows.

1. **Files.** The prompt's Build section lists `tests/contract/harness.py` and `tests/contract/factories.py`, but the "Files this task owns" line names only `conftest.py` and the two suites. Both files are added, with `tests/contract/__init__.py` (the other test packages have one). They hold no behaviour beyond the harness shape and the factory lists.
2. **Harness has a `now` field.** The prompt lists `subject`, `advance`, `receive` and `close`. A real adapter's clock is real time and cannot be set, so a test that schedules a message must take its enqueue time from the adapter's own `now()`. The field is the fifth member of `Harness`; `receive` stays last and optional, as the prompt has it.
3. **`receive(name, timeout)` semantics.** A queue name returns the next message that is due by now. A topic name returns the next message published to that topic since the harness was made, so a real adapter must hold its subscription from creation. A fake answers at once and ignores `timeout`. A real adapter waits up to `timeout`. `next_message` gives the same meaning for cache subscriptions: `None` when nothing arrives in time.
4. **Durations are seconds, never minutes or hours.** A real adapter runs the same test in real time, so the longest wait in either suite is about 3 seconds. A cache test that checks "still there much later" advances by one TTL plus one second, not an hour.
5. **Cache expiry is judged at `set time + ttl`, inclusive (DEC-058 item 3).** The TTL test asserts the key is present one second before the TTL and missing once the TTL has passed. Lock tests assert a lock is held one second into its TTL and free after the TTL.
6. **Fencing tokens start at 1 for each lock name and only go up (DEC-058 item 2).** The suite asserts both, and that a stale holder's release does nothing to a newer holder.
7. **Subscription is live from the call (DEC-058 item 1).** The suite publishes between `subscribe` and the first read, and checks that a publish before `subscribe` is not replayed. This is the case lesson L69 names.
8. **London clock change is not in the contract suites.** A real adapter's clock cannot be set to 2026-10-25, so the change is tested on the fakes only, in `tests/unit/adapters/testing/test_memory_cache.py` and `test_memory_event_bus.py`, where those tests already exist. The contract suites use UTC instants only.
9. **Only port-level behaviour is asserted.** The contract does not assert the order of messages across queues, a session check on `capacity.events` (a fake-only rule), or what a fake stores beyond its port. The refusals asserted are the ones the port docstrings state: an unknown topic, an unknown queue or a topic used as a queue, and a naive enqueue time, each raising `ValidationFailed`.
10. **Red commit holds stubs (DEC-010).** `make_memory_cache` and `make_memory_event_bus` raise `NotImplementedError` in the red commit, so the red run fails in the factory fixture, not on an import. The green commit builds the two harnesses over the existing fakes (`InMemoryCache`, `ManualEventBus`) with a `FixedClock`.
11. **Factories are the only place an adapter is named.** A later task adds its adapter as one `pytest.param(factory, id=..., marks=pytest.mark.integration)` line. Real factories must clean up what the test wrote on `close()`, because the contract suites share no state between tests.

Tests: `backend/tests/contract/test_cache_contract.py`, `backend/tests/contract/test_event_bus_contract.py`.

## DEC-077 Exit code of `pytest -m integration` on a contract suite with no integration case (P12, verifier round)
Status: accepted (builder decision, verifier ruling applied) · Affects: P12; I01 and I02, which add the first integration factories; the CI step that runs `-m integration` on a contract suite. Numbered DEC-077: DEC-076 is claimed by task/P04-criteria-port (unmerged), so this is the next free number.

1. **Reading of the criterion.** The P12 done-when says `uv run pytest -m integration tests/contract` "does not error". Pytest exits 5 when no test is collected, so the literal reading fails while the suite has no integration factory. The reading recorded here, from the verifier's ruling: the criterion is met when the `integration` marker is registered and the run does not fail because of the marker itself. Exit 5 with "33 deselected" is the expected outcome for a suite with no integration factory.
2. **No test is marked integration to get exit 0.** Marking a memory case integration would make the normal run deselect it and would hide a fake from the default run.
3. **The marker check.** `tests/contract/test_suite_markers.py::test_the_integration_marker_is_registered` asserts that the `integration` marker is registered in `backend/pyproject.toml`. That check passes on the current configuration, so it is a check, not a guard, and it was not seen red.
4. **Consequence for I01 and I02.** When the first integration factory is added, its test run exits 0 only when it collects at least one case. A CI step that runs `-m integration` must treat exit 5 as "no integration cases" until then.

## DEC-078 Correction to DEC-075 item 9: which refusals the port docstrings state (P12, verifier round 3)
Status: accepted (builder decision) · Affects: P12; I01 (Redis cache) and I02 (Service Bus) must match the same refusals and cancel rule. Corrects DEC-075 item 9 only; DEC-075 stays as written. Numbered DEC-078: DEC-076 is task/P04-criteria-port's, DEC-077 is this task's marker reading, and DEC-078 is free on main and every task/* branch (checked with `git show <branch>:docs/delivery/decisions.md`).

1. **What item 9 got wrong.** DEC-075 item 9 says the refusals asserted "are the ones the port docstrings state". The `event_bus.py` docstrings state two of the three. `publish` refuses an unknown topic. `schedule` refuses an unknown queue, and a topic is not in `QUEUES`, so a topic used as a queue is an unknown queue. The naive-enqueue-time refusal is not in any port docstring.
2. **Where the naive-time refusal comes from.** It comes from DEC-057 item 7 and `ManualEventBus`. The contract keeps the assertion because a naive datetime cannot be compared as a UTC instant (lesson L52), so every real adapter must refuse it too. The assertion is a decision of this entry, not a docstring. It is to be confirmed when I02 is built.
3. **Cache refusals.** No cache port docstring states a refusal. Every cache contract case asserts behaviour that a docstring states: get, set and TTL, publish and subscribe, and lock tokens.
4. **Cancel is identified by queue and number.** The port says cancelling an unknown or delivered number does nothing. The contract also asserts that a cancel on a different queue leaves the message on its own queue. This is a reading of the port's `cancel(queue, sequence_number)` signature, recorded here, and the case is `test_cancelling_a_number_on_another_queue_leaves_the_message`.
5. **Async iteration is required.** `Cache.subscribe` is typed `AsyncIterator[str]`, so `async for` is part of the port. The case `test_a_subscription_can_be_read_with_async_for` pins it, and mutant M17 (no `__aiter__`) fails it.

## DEC-079 The naive-time refusal is a fake rule, not a port promise: moved to the fake's unit tests (P12, verifier round 4)
Status: accepted (builder decision, verifier finding applied) · Affects: P12; I02 (Service Bus adapter, which need not refuse naive times if the port does not say so). Corrects DEC-078 item 2 and DEC-075 item 9 on this point only; both stay as written. Numbered DEC-079: free on main and every task/* branch (checked with `git show <branch>:docs/delivery/decisions.md`).

1. **The port does not promise it.** `EventBus.schedule` documents an unknown queue as refused, and nothing else about its input. The naive-time refusal is `ManualEventBus`'s own rule (DEC-057 item 7). DEC-078 item 2 was wrong to put it in the contract suite.
2. **Choice (a): move it to the fake's unit tests.** The case is removed from `tests/contract/test_event_bus_contract.py`. The unit test `tests/unit/adapters/testing/test_memory_event_bus.py::test_a_naive_enqueue_time_is_refused` already exists and still guards the fake. Removing a test is not a new behaviour, so no new red run is needed. The evidence records this.
3. **Consequence for I02.** The Service Bus adapter is not held to a naive-time refusal by the contract. Any real adapter must still refuse a naive time at its own boundary if it can receive one, but that is I02's design choice, not a port promise.
4. **Unchanged.** The unknown-topic and unknown-queue refusals stay in the contract (DEC-078 items 1 and 3), because `publish` and `schedule` document them.
## DEC-076 Criteria repository on Neo4j: storage, the service link, locks and the local-relationship rule (P04)
Status: accepted (builder build-P04b, taking over from build-P04; for the owner to confirm) · Affects: P04 (`adapters/outbound/graph_store/criteria_repository.py`, `application/ports/outbound/repositories.py`), `resource_store.py` (`LOCAL_RELATIONSHIP_TYPES`), U28, U30, U31, G11, P13, P14 and any task that reads criteria or saves a service.

Numbered DEC-076: DEC-074 is the highest on `main`; DEC-075 is written on `task/P12-contract-tests` and is not merged, so 076 is the next free number above every branch (checked with `git show <branch>:docs/delivery/decisions.md`).

1. **Storage.** Each version is a FHIR `Basic` written through the G05 store, with no json and no meta (DEC-001, DEC-053 item 10). The generic mapper gives the elements: `subject` (SUBJECT), `author` (AUTHOR, the owner's PractitionerRole), `supersedes` (SUPERSEDES), `acceptsNeed` and `excludesNeed` (ACCEPTS_NEED and EXCLUDES_NEED to shared Concept nodes, DEC-053 item 5), and the scalars `version`, `status`, `ageMin`, `ageMax`, `reviewBy` (a Neo4j date) and `changeReason`. `postcodeDistricts` is a sorted list property. No extension is registered, so the registry test is unchanged. The `ServiceCriteria` profile is the label, set after the put, because the store refuses `meta.profile`.
2. **CURRENT_CRITERIA is a local relationship.** It has no `fhirPath`, so the loader skips it only if its type is listed in `LOCAL_RELATIONSHIP_TYPES` (DEC-065 item 5). Without the listing a service with a current version cannot be read (`KeyError: 'fhirPath'`, the red test `test_a_service_with_a_current_version_still_reads_and_saves`). The type is added in this task.
3. **Reads do not depend on the link.** `current_for_service` finds the version whose status is `current` on a `ServiceCriteria` that is a subject of the service. A service put clears the service's outgoing relationships (DEC-053 item 6), so any `ServiceRepository.save` removes `CURRENT_CRITERIA`. **Residual, not fixed here:** after a service save the link is absent until the criteria repository writes it again, and nothing in this task does so. The owner or P01 should either re-point the link in `ServiceRepository.save`, or G11 should read the status as this repository does. The test shows the status read still finds the current version after a service save.
4. **Writes take the service lock first.** Every `add` and `save` sets the service's id to itself (lesson L73), then checks, then writes. The checks, all before the first change: the id is not used (Conflict); no other version of the service is current (Conflict); no other version of the service has the same version number (Conflict); a superseded version exists (NotFound) and belongs to the same service (ValidationFailed). Two racing writers serialise on the service lock, so the held-transaction tests (lesson L94) leave one current version and one version number.
5. **Unknown service is NotFound and writes nothing.** The lock finds no service and the call stops there, so no Basic or placeholder is written. A `save` that changes the service, the version number or the superseded version raises ValidationFailed (`criteria`) before the lock. A `save` of an unknown id is NotFound.
6. **Only criteria are returned.** `get` returns None for a `Basic` that is not a `ServiceCriteria` (a service flag, P14). `history` returns every version of the service, newest version number first. Old versions are never deleted.
7. **Empty values are left out.** The mapper refuses an empty list (`UnsupportedElement`, DEC-064 item 9 pattern), so an empty district list, need list or change reason is absent from the graph, and the rebuild returns the empty set or the empty text (lesson L74).
8. **`createdAt` is not stored.** The prompt lists it, but `ServiceCriteria` (D20) has no creation time, and adding one is a domain change outside this task. Owner to decide whether D20 gets a `created` field.
9. **The owner is a reference, not checked.** `author` points at a PractitionerRole. If the role is not yet written the store makes a placeholder (DEC-053 item 8). The repository does not check the role exists. U30 must check it before it approves a version.
10. **Test defects found on the first green run.** The racing version-number test superseded a version that it never stored, so under the implementation it failed with NotFound. Its input was corrected in `1d066a6` (red, stub), with the assertion unchanged. The racing current test's count of two stored versions was correct from its first red.
11. **Mutation checks (lesson L92).** With the service lock removed in `add`, both race tests fail. With the second-current check removed, the current race fails. With the version-number check removed, the version race fails. With the link write removed, three tests fail (the link, the supersede history and the race). Each guard is red when its fix is removed.
12. **Residuals.** (a) A racing `ServiceRepository.save` can clear the link after a criteria write (item 3). (b) `ServiceCriteria` has no `created` field (item 8). (c) The role is not checked (item 9). (d) No migration was added: the one-current rule and the version rule are enforced in the repository under the service lock, not by a constraint, because Neo4j Community has no composite uniqueness (lesson L90).

13. **Racing version tests (round 5).** The original racing-version test keeps its assertion (one stored version). Its input is corrected in `1d066a6` (red, stub) so that the first version has no predecessor. A new test, `test_two_racing_writers_with_a_seeded_version_leave_two_versions`, seeds the first version and asserts two stored versions. It is committed red in `80d4353` (stub, NotImplementedError) and passed by `1dc2b8c`. No existing assertion changes in a green commit.
14. **Racing-save guard: red, then green.** `5b2f284` commits `test_two_racing_saves_that_make_a_version_current_leave_one_current` red, with the save lock absent. It fails at `pytest.raises(Conflict)` with `DID NOT RAISE Conflict`. `bda0855` adds the save lock, which makes it pass. The test is committed before its guard, as lesson L88 requires.
15. **Deviation: an edit outside this task's files (verifier note).** The one-line change to `LOCAL_RELATIONSHIP_TYPES` in `resource_store.py` was made without stopping to report, which the brief asks for. It is kept: the test `test_a_service_with_a_current_version_still_reads_and_saves` shows it is needed, and item 2 records it. Future edits outside the file list will stop and be reported.
16. **Hazard H-07 now records the link clearing (item 3).** A service save clears `CURRENT_CRITERIA`. Reads by status are unaffected. The owner or P01 must re-point the link, so the H-07 row carries the open item.
Tests: `backend/tests/unit/adapters/outbound/graph_store/test_criteria_mapping.py` (pure mapping, refusals by field) and `backend/tests/integration/graph_store/test_criteria_repository.py` (round trip, the link, the supersede history, the shared needs, the guards, the two held-transaction races, and the service read with a current version).

## DEC-080 Registration draft and derived Task copies: no JSON draft, typed rows (P13)
Status: accepted (builder build-P13, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: P13 (`domain/model/registration.py`, `adapters/outbound/graph_store/registration_repository.py`), U26, U27 (`write_bundle`), U28, U29, every task that stores a registration; DEC-001.

Numbered DEC-080: DEC-079 is the highest number on `main` and on every `task/*` branch when this was written (checked with `git show <branch>:docs/delivery/decisions.md`).

The P13 prompt says the wizard draft is kept as an extension `ext_draftJson` holding JSON [ASSUMED]. DEC-001 forbids a JSON string on a node, so that is not built. The choices are:

1. **The draft is typed rows.** Each step and field of the draft is one `RegistrationDraftField` node `{step, field, value}`, joined to the Task by `DRAFT_OF`. `value` keeps its type (text, whole number or flag). The draft is a mapping of steps, each a non-empty mapping of fields. Any other shape is refused with `ValidationFailed` naming the draft only (no value is echoed): a step that is not text, an empty step, a field that is not text, a value that is null, a float, a list or a mapping. Rows read back with a field stored twice are refused (`draft_from_rows`).
2. **The draft is replaced as a whole on every save.** `save` puts the Task first, which takes the store's root write lock and checks the version (DEC-053 item 4), and only then deletes the old rows and writes the new ones. A refused save therefore writes no rows. Test: `test_a_save_waits_for_a_held_save_and_a_refused_save_leaves_its_draft`.
3. **Derived copies on the Task**, written by the same put and the same transaction, as DEC-063 does for bookings: `businessStatus` (the registration status code; the store does not hold it as a concept) and `submittedAt` (a Neo4j UTC datetime, so submissions can be ordered in the graph). The FHIR form holds the same submission time as `executionPeriod.start`. The copy exists only for ordering and is never read back.
4. **`note` is `Task.description`.** Empty notes are left out.
5. **`add` for an unknown service is `NotFound` and writes nothing.** The store would otherwise create a placeholder HealthcareService (DEC-053 item 8), which is not a service.
6. **The repository does not check which status may follow which.** The use cases do that with `transition_registration` (D22). `save` checks only that the stored Task is a registration.
7. **Pure mapping signatures**: `registration_to_fhir(r)`, `registration_from_fhir(resource, draft)`, `draft_to_rows(draft)`, `draft_from_rows(rows)`. The draft is outside the Task, so the FHIR round trip is `registration_from_fhir(registration_to_fhir(r), draft_from_rows(draft_to_rows(r.draft))) == r`.

Tests: `backend/tests/unit/adapters/outbound/graph_store/test_registration_mapping.py`, `backend/tests/unit/domain/model/test_registration.py`, `backend/tests/integration/graph_store/test_registration_repository.py`. Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/P13.md`.

## DEC-081 Registration Task status, its code and the four approval checks (P13)
Status: accepted (builder build-P13, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: P13, U23 to U29 (the use cases that move a registration), `extensions.py` (the registry), the Task mapping of every registration.

Numbered DEC-081, after DEC-080.

1. **Task status per registration state.** The FHIR Task status follows the state, with the registration status code as the business status (DEC-080 item 3): invited and draft give `draft`; submitted, in-review and needs-changes give `requested`; approved gives `accepted`; live and reverify give `in-progress`; paused gives `on-hold`; not-approved gives `rejected`. The prompt says only "status follows Task states". The table is a builder choice; the owner may change it, and the change is one mapping.
2. **Task code.** The Task carries `code` = `register-service` in CodeSystem `task-code` (`code_system_url("task-code")`). The registration query matches that concept. A Task with another code is refused as a registration (`ValidationFailed("code", ...)`).
3. **Four checks as extensions.** `checkAutomatic`, `checkCallback` and `checkCriteria` are boolean; `checkScope` holds the scope flag as text and is left out until the scope is decided (`None`). Each is registered in `extensions.py` (DEC-054 item 3 pattern).
4. **Deviation: `extensions.py` and its pin test.** Both are outside P13's file list. They are needed because the mapper refuses unregistered extension URLs (`UnknownExtension`, DEC-049). The four names are added to the registry. The pin in `test_extensions.py` was changed in the first red commit (evidence P13.md), so the registry change is tested by `test_registry_holds_the_twenty_six_named_extensions` and by the parametrised tests for each name.
5. **Approval gate is not decided here.** The approver-differs check and the two-person rule are DEC-032 (use cases). The four booleans are stored as they are given.

## DEC-082 The scope list: one List node, ENTRY relationships, a minimal service, and the write lock (P13)
Status: accepted (builder build-P13, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: P13 (`adapters/outbound/graph_store/scope_list_repository.py`), U23 (`AddScopeEntry`), U24 (invitations, which need an `in-pilot` entry), U25, U27 and every task that reads a service created from the scope list; migration 0001 (the `Resource.id` constraint).

Numbered DEC-082, after DEC-080 and DEC-081.

1. **The list root.** One node `(:Resource:List {id: 'scope-list', status: 'current', title: 'Scope list'})`. The label `Resource` puts it under the `Resource.id` uniqueness constraint (migration 0001), so two racing first writes cannot make two lists (lesson L90). The list is not written through the mapper: G04 does not map `List`, and its entries are relationship properties.
2. **Not built, from the spec's List row:** the `code` (scope-list) as a Concept, the `date`, and `SOURCE -> PractitionerRole` (the owner of the list). No P13 test needs them. The owner decides whether the list records its owner and whether the root carries a date. Until then the list is identified by its fixed id.
3. **Entries.** `(:List)-[:ENTRY {flag, date, reason}]->(:HealthcareService)`. `flag` is the ScopeFlag value, `date` a Neo4j date, and `reason` is left out when empty. `entries()` is sorted by the service name, then id. The name and category come from the service, not from the entry.
4. **A new service is minimal.** `save_entry` for a service that does not exist creates `HealthcareService {id, active: false, name, category}` through the resource store. It has no profile extensions. **Trap:** `Neo4jServiceRepository.get` (P01) refuses such a service with `ValidationFailed` until a full profile is saved (`usualRangeMin` and the other profile extensions are required). Anything that loads a service from the scope list must read it through the scope list, or wait until the registration writes its profile. `save_entry` never changes an existing service.
5. **Every save takes the list's write lock first** (`SET l.id = l.id`, lesson L73), then reads the service, then writes the entry. Without the lock, two saves that create the same new service meet the uniqueness constraint after the first commits, and the second is refused with `Conflict`. Tests that show this: `test_a_save_for_a_service_that_a_held_save_is_creating_waits_and_succeeds` and `test_sixteen_racing_saves_for_a_new_service_all_succeed_and_leave_one_service` (committed red before the lock was restored; see evidence P13.md). The held-transaction test for an existing service (`test_a_second_save_for_a_service_waits_for_a_held_save_and_leaves_one_entry`) passes without the lock, because Neo4j's MERGE on the relationship already serialises that case. It is kept as a regression test and is not a guard.
6. **A racing first creation of the list** can lose on the constraint. The loser gets `Conflict` (`test_sixteen_racing_creations_of_the_list_leave_one_list`), and nothing is stored twice.

## DEC-083 Report and service flags: the lock query, the resolution rule and the flag id (P14)
Status: accepted (builder build-P14, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: P14 (`domain/model/flags.py`, `adapters/outbound/graph_store/flag_repository.py`), U07, U36, U39, U31, G11 and any task that sets or resolves a flag.

Numbered DEC-083: DEC-082 is the highest number on `main` and on every `task/*` branch when this was written.

1. **Lock on an immutable property.** Every write locks its parent with `SET x.id = x.id`, then checks, then writes (lesson L73, L99). The report-flag resolution lock is `SET f.id = f.id`. An earlier draft used `SET f.status = f.status`: under a held transaction the statement reads `status` before it waits, and after the wait it writes the old value back. A second resolution then saw `open` with a resolution time. The held-resolution test `test_a_second_resolution_waits_for_a_held_resolution_and_is_refused` caught it. Rule: lock on a property that no write changes.
2. **A resolved flag is not resolved again.** `save_report_flag` raises `InvalidTransition` when the stored flag is not open. The prompt does not say this. It keeps two shift leads from recording two different outcomes for one flag.
3. **Fixed fields.** A save that changes the report, service, reason or creation time raises `ValidationFailed`. The prompt does not say this either.
4. **Service flag id.** One Basic per service and code, id `<serviceId>.<code>`. The codes are a fixed set with no dots, so the id is unambiguous. A Basic already at that id that is not a ServiceFlag raises `Conflict`, and is not overwritten.
5. **No json (DEC-001).** The prompt's test 7 (json equals the canonical resource) is replaced by: the stored resource, rebuilt from the graph, equals `service_flag_to_fhir` apart from `meta`. Fields are scalars (`code`, `status`, `reason`, `actor`, `at`), as the criteria mapping does.
6. **Input correction (test defect).** The P14 red commit's test `test_saving_a_flag_cannot_change_its_report_or_its_reason` built its flag with status checked and no resolution time, which the ReportFlag rule refuses at construction. The correction gives it a resolution time (`d813182`). The assertion is unchanged. That commit is red: the corrected input fails on the stub.
7. **Unique key on the flag id (migration 0027).** `add_report_flag` locks the Observation, so two adds of one id on different reports did not serialise and both stored. A single-property unique constraint on `ReportFlag.id` (lesson L90) refuses the second write, and `add_report_flag` maps the `ConstraintError` to `Conflict`. The race test is `test_two_racing_adds_of_one_flag_id_on_different_reports_leave_one_flag`.
8. **Equivalent guards, kept.** With the unique key in place, the report-add lock (M1) and the application duplicate check (M4) are no longer what refuses a same-report duplicate: the database does. Both mutants survive their named tests on the final head. They are kept as defence in depth. The owner may remove them.
9. **Open dependency on P06: the rollback on Conflict is general.** The unit of work must roll the transaction back when a write raises `Conflict`, and must never commit it. This rule is general: it protects every write path, not only the flag add. It is not a condition of M4's equivalence. No data is written before `Conflict`, but the add takes a write-mode lock first (a self-SET of the Observation's id), so it does write to the graph's lock state, so M4's equivalence holds for the add alone. P06 is not built, so the rule is an open dependency on P06, not a P14 check. Owner to confirm when P06 is specified.

## DEC-084 Outbox and unit of work on Neo4j: payload storage, the write guard, wiring and deviations (P06)
Status: accepted (builder build-P06, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: P06 (`application/ports/outbound/outbox.py`, `unit_of_work.py`, `adapters/outbound/audit_neo4j/outbox.py`, `adapters/outbound/graph_store/unit_of_work.py`, `tests/support/uow.py`), `bootstrap/container.py`, `graph_store/migrations.py`, `.importlinter`, and every use case and repository that writes (U-series, P15, I03).

Numbered DEC-084: DEC-083 is the highest number on `main` and on every `task/*` branch when this was written (checked with `git show <branch>:docs/delivery/decisions.md`).

1. **The payload is typed properties, not JSON (DEC-001).** The prompt's `payloadJson` is not built. Each payload field is a property `payload_<field>` on the `OutboxMessage` node, set in one `SET m += $props`. A field that is null is listed in `payloadNulls`, because Neo4j stores no null property (lesson L74). Text that is a canonical UTC instant (the form `to_payload()` writes, for example `2026-10-23T13:00:00Z`) is stored as a datetime and read back as the same text. Other text stays text, and is never altered on the way out. Values that are not text, whole numbers, flags or null are refused with `ValidationFailed("payload", ...)`, which names the field only.
2. **Field names** must match `[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_]{0,63}`, or the add is refused.
3. **The write guard (DEC-083 item 9, now built).** Every repository the unit of work hands out is wrapped. A `Conflict` from any of its async methods marks the unit of work refused, and a refused unit of work never commits: `commit()` rolls back and raises `Conflict`. The `Conflict` still reaches the caller, who may catch it. This covers every write path, as item 9 requires. Test: `tests/integration/graph_store/test_unit_of_work.py::test_a_conflict_that_the_caller_swallows_cannot_be_committed` (red in 063df25, the guard in the green commit).
   **Residual:** the guard sees only `Conflict`. A Neo4j error that a repository does not map to `Conflict` is not marked. Repositories must map every refused write to `Conflict`, as P13 and P14 do.
4. **Session and transaction.** The unit of work takes its session from the source it is given: the composition root passes `Neo4jConnection` (DEC-012, lesson L12), and tests may pass the driver. The session is closed on every path, tested by counting opens and closes. The explicit transaction (`session.begin_transaction()`) is committed once, or rolled back on exit, and re-raises the exception. After commit or rollback, using the unit of work raises `RuntimeError`. The explicit transaction is cast to `AsyncManagedTransaction` for the repositories, which only call `run()`.
5. **Registry.** `DEFAULT_FACTORIES` builds 17 repositories: the 14 names of the port, plus `contacts`, `pins` and `devices` for the P16 stores (DEC-071 item 9, lesson L86). A later task adds one entry. An attribute with no factory raises `AttributeError("unit of work has no '<name>' repository registered")`. The class annotates the port's attributes for the type checker, so nothing else changes per repository.
6. **Clock of the audit factory.** The factories take `(tx, store)` only, so the audit log's provenance `recorded` time uses the system clock, not the unit of work's clock. No write path records provenance yet (DEC-055 item 18). The first task that records provenance must pass the clock.
7. **The outbox** (`Neo4jOutbox(tx)`, named queries `Q-OUTBOX-ADD`, `Q-OUTBOX-PENDING`, `Q-OUTBOX-LOCK`, `Q-OUTBOX-PUBLISHED`, `Q-OUTBOX-FAILED`, registered in G01's registry). Status is `pending`, `published` or `dead`. `pending()` is oldest first by `createdAt`, then `id`, with the limit. `mark_published`: pending becomes published, published is a no-op (a relay retry), dead is `Conflict`. `mark_failed` adds one attempt and keeps the error text; at `MAX_ATTEMPTS` (10) a pending message becomes dead. An unknown id in either is `NotFound`. A repeated id is `Conflict`, from the unique key (migration 0028). The status is set under a self-SET lock first (lesson L73).
8. **Deviations outside the task's file list.** Each one is needed for the task's tests, and each is proven by a test:
   a. `bootstrap/container.py`: the `uow_factory` field the prompt asks for, wired on the container's connection. Test: `tests/integration/graph_store/test_unit_of_work.py::test_the_container_hands_out_units_of_work_on_its_own_connection` (red in 85678f2).
   b. `graph_store/migrations.py`: migration `0028_outbox_message_id` (unique `OutboxMessage.id`, the prompt's requirement). Tests: `tests/integration/graph_store/test_driver.py` (migration count 23 to 24, red in 063df25) and `tests/unit/adapters/outbound/graph_store/test_migration_ids.py::test_the_outbox_message_id_migration_is_0028_after_the_report_flag_migration`.
   c. `.importlinter`: four `ignore_imports` entries. Two let `audit_neo4j.outbox` use the G01 driver and query registry, as DEC-055 item 15 does for the audit log. Two let `graph_store.unit_of_work` import the audit log and the outbox, because it is the one place that wires them. The architecture probe in `tests/architecture/test_import_rules.py` gets the matching modules, so the ignores have targets. Proof: `lint-imports` reports "Adapters are independent KEPT (6 ignored imports)", and `tests/architecture` passes (22 tests).
   d. `docs/spec/contracts.md`, the Additions table: `UnitOfWork.contacts`, `pins` and `devices`. This names the attributes the code uses. It is an addition, not an edit to a source-of-truth artifact.
9. **Not built here.** The unit of work has no `processed` (P15) and no `alert_subscriptions` (U33) attribute. Those tasks add their factories and attributes. The idempotency store is P15.

## DEC-085 QueryBoard read model: reads, deviations from the prompt, and the sample clock (U08)
Status: accepted (builder build-U08b, taking over from build-U08; for the owner to confirm) · Affects: U08 (`application/use_cases/query_board.py`), U09 (board cache and live push), R05 (`$board`), W05 (tile wording), P02 (`BookingRepository.confirmed_since`).

Numbered DEC-085: DEC-084 is the highest number on `main` and on every `task/*` branch when this was written (checked with `git show <branch>:docs/delivery/decisions.md`).

1. **Confirmations are read per service.** The prompt and the Rules section ask for one query per repository call across all services. `BookingRepository.confirmed_since` takes one service id (contracts.md, P02), so `QueryBoard` calls it once for each service that has a latest report, with that report's effective time as `since`. Every other read is one call for all services: `list_active`, `services_with_flag("suspended")`, `latest_for_services`, `open_for_services` (pending counts). The per-service calls are bounded by the number of services, not by patients or bookings. A batch form (`confirmed_since_for_services`) belongs in P02's port and adapter, not in this task's file list. It is a follow-up for U09 or the owner; until then the board does one confirmation query per service with a report.
2. **Pending.** `pending` is the number of requested bookings for the service (`open_for_services`). It never reduces `effective_available`, as the Rules section says.
3. **Acceptance test 4 is read at 14:25.** The prompt reads the board at Friday 14:12 and then has "a booking confirmed 14:19". A confirmation after the clock is not a fact the board can use, so the test reads the confirmed step at 14:25 (13:25 UTC). The confirmation itself is stored at 14:19 BST, which is 13:19 UTC, and the test asserts the result of the confirmation (available 3, pending 0).
4. **Order.** Entries sort by the `ServiceCategory` enum order, then by name, then by service id as a last tie-break. The tie-break is added so the order is total when two services share a name. It is not in the prompt.
5. **Anonymous link.** Only `Role.ANONYMOUS_LINK` is refused, with `NotAllowed`. Every other role may read the board, as the prompt says.
6. **Suspended.** `ServiceProfile.suspended` is not stored. The use case sets it with `dataclasses.replace` for each service in `services_with_flag("suspended")`, before `compute_status`.
7. **Pressure.** `system_pressure` and `all_limited_or_worse` take the status of every entry. D08 leaves out out-of-hours and does not count not-updated, so the board needs no extra filter. A board with no in-hours service has `all_limited_or_worse` false.
8. **Due and was-due instants.** `next_due_at` is `next_due(profile, now)`. `was_due_at` is `Freshness.was_due`, which is set only when the service is overdue (DEC-029 and DEC-023 apply). Both are UTC instants.
9. **Read-only.** The unit of work is entered and left without `commit()`, so it rolls back and writes nothing. The node count before and after a run is compared in a test.
10. **Fields.** `BoardEntry` has the fourteen fields of contracts.md and no other. A test fixes the field names, so a later field that could hold patient data fails the build.
