Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/U08.md`. Tests: `backend/tests/integration/application/test_query_board.py`.

## DEC-088 Batch confirmation read for the board; supersedes DEC-085 item 1 (U08)
Status: accepted (builder build-U08b, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: U08 (`application/use_cases/query_board.py`), P02 (`BookingRepository` port and `adapters/outbound/graph_store/booking_repository.py`, one added method), U09, R05, and `docs/spec/contracts.md` "Additions".

Numbered DEC-088: DEC-087 is the highest number on `main` and on every `task/*` branch when this was written (R01 holds DEC-086 and DEC-087 unmerged).

1. **Supersedes DEC-085 item 1 for the board's reads.** The board no longer calls `confirmed_since` once per service. It calls the new `confirmed_since_for_services(service_ids, since)` once for all services with a latest report. `since` is the earliest of those report times. Each service's confirmations are then kept only at or after its own report time, by the availability rule (domain-rules 1.6). DEC-085 item 1 is otherwise unchanged; the per-service `confirmed_since` port method is kept for other callers.
2. **Port change to a delivered task.** The new method is added to P02's `BookingRepository` protocol, and listed in contracts.md "Additions". It returns every requested service id as a key, with `[]` where there are no confirmations.
3. **Query count: one query, no per-booking load.** The adapter issues exactly one named query, `Q-BOOK-CONFIRMED-FOR`, for all the listed services, whatever the number of bookings. It matches each confirmed Task to its `Task_Output` with `confirmed-at` (`HAS_OUTPUT`, then `TYPE` to the output-type Concept) and returns the service id and the stored datetime per row. Nothing is loaded per booking. This meets the prompt's bulk rule. An earlier version loaded each confirmed booking after the query (one query plus one load per confirmed booking). Round 3 folded the load into the query, and a test counts the queries run through the transaction, store reads included: three confirmed bookings across two services run one query. The read is one query per call, and the board makes one such call (item 1). An empty list of services runs no query and returns `{}` (a test).
4. **Pending is per service.** A test pins each service's pending count and availability, so a count taken from one service for every service fails the build.

Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/U08.md` (round 2 and round 3 sections).
## DEC-086 FHIR REST error bodies, body types and unexpected errors (R01)
Status: accepted (builder build-R01b, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: R01 (`adapters/inbound/fhir_rest/errors.py`), every R-task that adds a route (R02 to R18), hazard H-09.

Numbered DEC-086: DEC-084 is the highest on `main` and on every `task/*` branch when this was written (checked with `git show <branch>:docs/delivery/decisions.md`). DEC-085 was then taken by `task/U08-query-board` (QueryBoard), so this entry and the next are DEC-086 and DEC-087. Checked against every branch before the commit.

1. **415 and the body types.** A POST or PUT whose body is not `application/fhir+json` or `application/json` (parameters after `;` are ignored, the match is case-blind) gets 415 with code `not-supported`. A POST or PUT with no Content-Type is refused the same way. The prompt's list of error codes has no 415 code, so `not-supported` (the FHIR issue type) is the choice. The check runs before routing, so it also covers paths that do not exist.
2. **Other framework errors use the same shape.** An unknown path under `/fhir` is 404 `not-found`, a wrong method is 405 `not-supported`, other 4xx are `invalid` and 5xx are `exception`. Diagnostics are fixed text for each status. Starlette's own `detail` text is not used, because it can echo the request.
3. **Request validation** (a body that does not fit its model, a bad query value) is 400 `invalid`. `expression` is the location of the first error with `body`, `query` and `path` removed (for example `count`). Diagnostics are fixed text. Neither the input value nor the framework's message is returned, because a value can be a postcode.
4. **Unexpected errors** are 500 `exception` with diagnostics `Internal error`, and no stack trace. The catch-all is an outer ASGI middleware, not a Starlette exception handler. Starlette's handler re-raises after it responds, and the server would then log the exception message and traceback, which can carry a postcode. The middleware logs the exception class name only. If a response has already started, the error is not turned into a body; the connection is left to the server.
5. **Domain mapping.** `ValidationFailed` is 400 `invalid`, `expression` = its field, diagnostics = its message (the domain must keep values out of its messages). `NotFound` and `NotAllowed` are both 404 `not-found` with the same fixed text, so the two bodies are byte-identical (a forbidden record is indistinguishable from a missing one). `Conflict` is 409 `conflict`. `InvalidTransition` is 409 `business-rule`. Any other `DomainError` falls through to item 4.

Tests: `tests/integration/adapters/inbound/fhir_rest/test_app.py` (red in b6d5dc2 as stubs; the body and log tests guard items 1 to 5). Mutation runs on the green build: removing the 415 check, removing the catch-all, logging the message, and changing the NotAllowed body each fail a test.

## DEC-087 FHIR REST app wiring, metadata registry and API test fixtures (R01)
Status: accepted (builder build-R01b, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: R01 (`app.py`, `capability.py`, `tests/support/api.py`), every R-task that adds a route or a resource, `docs/spec/contracts.md` (Additions).

1. **The container is a Protocol.** `create_app` takes an `ApiContainer` (read-only `clock` and `uow_factory`), not `bootstrap.Container`. The layers contract forbids adapters from importing bootstrap. `Container` satisfies the Protocol structurally, so the composition root is unchanged.
2. **Lifecycle is not owned by the app.** `create_app` does not call `container.startup()` or `shutdown()`. DEC-012 puts that in the process that serves the app, which is a later task. Tests never run the lifespan, so the test fixture owns its connection.
3. **OpenAPI pages are off** (`docs_url`, `redoc_url`, `openapi_url` set to None). Every response under `/fhir` is FHIR, and the surface is the FHIR API only.
4. **The metadata date comes from the container clock.** `build_capability_statement(now)` takes the instant as an argument, and the route passes `container.clock.now()`. The R01 test clock is 2026-10-23T13:12Z (DEC-070 item 4). F08's `TEST_NOW` is unchanged.
5. **Registry.** `register_resource(type, interactions, operations)` is module-level. Interaction codes are checked against the R4B `TypeRestfulInteraction` set, and a bad code, an empty type or a second registration of the same type raises `ValueError` (a programming error, refused at registration, not per request). Each operation is a canonical OperationDefinition URL; its `name` is the last path segment. The exact operation shape is for R14 to confirm.
6. **Test fixtures.** `test_container` is `build_for_tests(clock=FixedClock(2026-10-23T13:12Z), neo4j=<a Neo4jConnection on the harness container>)`. It is opened and closed per test, and depends on `clean_graph`. `api_app` is `create_app(test_container)`, a separate fixture so a route test can add its own test-only routes. `api_client` is an `httpx.AsyncClient` on `ASGITransport(app=api_app)`. The `tests/integration/adapters/inbound/fhir_rest/conftest.py` file imports them, so later route tasks reuse them.
7. **Dependencies.** FastAPI 0.162.0 and httpx 0.28.1 were added on `task/R01-fhir-rest-skeleton` (commit dbbb935) under DEC-070 item 1. The lock change is additions only (104 lines in `backend/uv.lock`, 2 in `pyproject.toml`).
8. **Shared names, recorded here and not in `contracts.md`.** The orchestrator's brief for R01 says not to edit `docs/spec` source files, so the Additions row is not written. The names this task introduces are `ApiContainer`, `create_app`, `get_container` and `operation_outcome` in `adapters/inbound/fhir_rest/app.py` and `errors.py`; `FhirJSONResponse`, `register_resource` and the `GET /fhir/metadata` route in `capability.py`; and the fixtures `test_container`, `api_app` and `api_client` in `tests/support/api.py`. The owner should add them to the Additions section when reconciling (lesson published with this entry).
9. **httpx moved to the dev group (deviation from DEC-070 item 1).** DEC-070 approved FastAPI and httpx and put both on the R01 branch. httpx is used only as the test client (`ASGITransport` in `tests/support/api.py`), and no code under `src/` imports it. It is therefore in `[dependency-groups] dev`, not in the runtime `dependencies`. The version is unchanged (httpx 0.28.1). FastAPI stays a runtime dependency. This changes only where httpx is declared, not the approval. The lock change is re-generated, and `uv lock --check` passes.

## DEC-090 Idempotency store: result storage, result types and marker keys (P15)
Status: accepted (builder build-P15). Owner ruling 2026-10-27: items 1, 2 and 5 accepted by the orchestrator, because each follows DEC-001 or the Community constraint · Affects: P15 (`application/idempotency.py`, `application/ports/outbound/idempotency.py`, `adapters/outbound/graph_store/idempotency_store.py`, migrations 0029 and 0030), P06 (`DEFAULT_FACTORIES` and the unit of work attributes `idempotency` and `processed`), every command use case (U-series) and every message consumer (I04 and later), DEC-001, DEC-012.

Numbered DEC-090: DEC-089 is claimed by task/I01-redis-cache (unmerged), and DEC-088 is the highest number on main. DEC-090 is the next free number on main and on every `task/*` branch (checked with `git show <branch>:docs/delivery/decisions.md`, lesson L43).

1. **No JSON text is stored (DEC-001 overrides the prompt).** The prompt's `resultJson` property is not built. `encode_result` still produces a canonical JSON text, but that text is an intermediate form only. The store writes it as a tree of `(:IdempotencyValue {seq, kind, text, num, flag, at})` nodes, one per JSON value, linked by `[:MEMBER {position, name}]` and reached from the record by `[:HAS_VALUE]`. Strings, numbers and flags keep their own typed property. A UTC instant is a Neo4j `datetime` in `at`, and reads back as the same text. No node holds a map, a list of maps or a JSON-looking string (test `test_no_stored_node_holds_a_map_a_list_of_maps_or_a_json_string`).
2. **Result types come from diucs.application and diucs.domain (deviation, the prompt says application only).** Command results carry domain enums: `AuditEntry` holds an `Actor` whose `role` is `diucs.domain.codes.Role`. Under the prompt rule such results could not be replayed. Decoding imports only modules under these two packages. The packages are ours and pure, so the security intent of the rule (no import of an arbitrary module named in stored text) still holds. `encode_result` refuses any type outside them, so the refusal happens at the first write and never at a replay. Tests: `test_a_domain_enum_nested_in_a_result_decodes_as_itself`, `test_a_dataclass_outside_the_allowed_packages_is_refused_on_decode_and_encode`, `test_decoding_a_type_outside_the_allowed_packages_raises_value_error`.
3. **Results are dataclasses or enum members, not None or plain containers.** Nested values: dataclasses (init fields only), enum members, tuples, frozensets, lists, strings, booleans, finite numbers in 64-bit range, and aware datetimes (stored in UTC). A naive datetime, a dict and any other type are refused on encode with ValueError.
4. **Replay semantics.** `put` of an equal result for a stored key changes nothing. `put` of a different result for a stored key raises `Conflict`, and the first result is kept. A racing first write meets the unique key on `IdempotencyRecord.key` (migration 0029); its CREATE fails with `ConstraintError`, which is mapped to `Conflict`, and the unit of work is then refused. Test `test_a_raw_second_create_of_a_record_key_is_refused_by_the_database` shows the database refuses the duplicate.
5. **Processed markers: the key is one property, by choice; the composite form also works.** A probe on Neo4j 5.26.32 Community (run in the harness during this fix, and reported by verify-P15) shows that a composite unique constraint on `(n.a, n.b)` refuses a duplicate. So lesson L90, which says composite uniqueness is unavailable on Community, is wrong for this version, and the prompt's composite constraint was available. The builder still chose one unique property, `key = "<len(consumer)>:<consumer>:<messageId>"` (migration 0030), with `messageId` and `consumer` also stored. The length prefix makes the key injective. The single-key form is valid and tested, and it was not required. Tests: `test_pairs_whose_unprefixed_text_is_equal_keep_separate_markers` (four pairs, each equal under one unprefixed order), `test_markers_do_not_collide_when_the_separator_appears_in_the_values`, `test_a_colon_in_either_value_cannot_make_two_pairs_share_a_marker`, `test_a_raw_second_create_of_a_processed_key_is_refused_by_the_database`.
6. **Racing marks.** A second MERGE on the same key waits for the held first mark. After the first commits it matches the committed marker and returns False: the message was already recorded and the second writes nothing. If the first rolls back, the second creates the marker and returns True. Across sixteen racing units of work the outcome is one `created` and fifteen `already`, with no `Conflict`; `test_sixteen_racing_marks_for_one_message_let_exactly_one_win` asserts both counts, and it passed five times in a row. A CREATE that meets the key raises ConstraintError, which the store maps to `Conflict`, and the caller must not commit. Test `test_a_racing_mark_waits_for_a_held_mark_and_leaves_one_marker`.
7. **Times are written by the database in UTC.** The ports take no clock argument (the prompt's signatures), so `createdAt` and `processedAt` are `datetime({timezone: 'UTC'})`, as DEC-084 item 7 and the P16 store do. Tests: `test_a_processed_marker_records_the_time_as_a_datetime`, `test_an_instant_in_a_result_is_stored_as_a_neo4j_datetime`.
8. **Wiring.** `DEFAULT_FACTORIES` gains `idempotency` and `processed`. The `UnitOfWork` port and `Neo4jUnitOfWork` gain the attributes `idempotency: IdempotencyStore` and `processed: ProcessedMessages`. `DriverIdempotencyStore(driver)` runs each call in its own transaction and always closes its session (DEC-012).
9. **Red commits and the first expectations.** The task's red commits are `3edad11` (registry, migration count and migration order expectations) and `97edd58` (every new codec and store test, with the expectations as they stand now), then `e7d3057` (the London check split so that its astimezone assertion is its own failing line; the isinstance check follows it). The London round trip expects `astimezone(UTC)`, and the held racing mark expects False. An earlier build of this branch wrote the first version of these two expectations differently and corrected them before any implementation existed; that earlier history is kept on the branch `backup/P15-1d913cb`, not on the task branch. The red stubs return constant values (no `NotImplementedError`), so each new test fails on its own assertion line (DEC-010): `assert False is True`, `DID NOT RAISE ValueError`, `assert 0 == 1`, and similar.
10. **Community uniqueness, and the migrations header.** The header of `graph_store/migrations.py` is correct: UNIQUE constraints on Community work, including composite ones (item 5). Lesson L90 says otherwise and is wrong for Neo4j 5.26.32 Community; the coordinator is publishing a correction. Item 5 records the single-key choice; it is not a workaround for a missing feature.
11. **Query names.** The prompt names `Q-IDEM-GET` and `Q-IDEM-PUT`. They are not built under those names. `Q-IDEM-GET` is three reads, `Q-IDEM-HEAD` (the record), `Q-IDEM-VALUES` (its value nodes) and `Q-IDEM-EDGES` (its member relationships), because the result is a tree. `Q-IDEM-PUT` is two writes, `Q-IDEM-CREATE` (record and value nodes in one statement) and `Q-IDEM-LINK` (member relationships, run after the nodes exist in the same transaction), because one statement would need the value nodes and the edges in one UNWIND over the tree. `Q-PROCESSED-MARK` keeps the prompt's name. All five are registered in `REGISTRY`.

## DEC-089 Redis cache adapter: kept as the default, subscription and fencing limits, test instance (I01)
Status: accepted (builder decision, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: I01 (`adapters/outbound/cache_redis/redis_cache.py`), P12 (cache contract factories), I03 (outbox relay lock), I05 and U08 and R06 (live-update subscribers of `board.updates`), any service that relies on a fencing token. Numbered DEC-089: DEC-088 is the highest number on `main` and on every `task/*` branch when this was written.

1. **Redis stays, and nothing new is added.** DEC-003 allows Redis as a default and asks that a replacement keep the same properties. I01 keeps Redis, so no replacement is recorded. It uses `redis.asyncio` (the `redis>=8.1.0` already in `backend/pyproject.toml`, no lock change), with the URL from `Settings.redis_url`. TLS and the password come in the URL (`rediss://`), so Azure Cache for Redis and Azure Managed Redis both work. The open choice between them stays with the owner and needs no code change.
2. **A subscription is live from the call, as far as this process can tell (DEC-058 item 1).** `subscribe()` is a plain method, so it cannot await the SUBSCRIBE. It starts the SUBSCRIBE in a task and returns at once. The task waits for the server's subscribe reply. `RedisCache.publish` waits for every subscription this cache has made before it sends, so a publish from the same cache after `subscribe()` is never missed. **Residual, not met by I01:** a publish from another process (another API worker, the relay) can still be missed during the round trip between `subscribe()` and the server's reply. The contract suite cannot see this. Live consumers must refetch on connect and after a reconnect; the board already refetches (architecture, pages refetch on a missed message).
3. **Closing a subscription is confirmed by the server.** `aclose()` sends UNSUBSCRIBE, waits for its reply and then releases the connection, so `PUBSUB NUMSUB` is 0 when it returns. A reader waiting on a closed subscription ends at once, and so does any later read. `RedisCache.aclose()` closes every subscription the cache made, then the client. Each subscription has one reader task, because two readers on one PubSub take each other's replies.
4. **Fencing tokens are monotonic only while Redis keeps its data (DEC-058 item 2, limit).** The token is `INCR diucs:fence:<name>`, which never expires, so within one Redis data set tokens strictly increase. The compose Redis has no volume (infra/README.md), and a managed Redis can restart or lose data. After such a loss the counter starts again at 1 and a token can repeat. Redis alone cannot give the rule "a later token is always greater" across a data loss. **Consequence, for I03 and I05:** a writer that a fencing token protects must also refuse any token lower than the highest one it has already committed, and that highest value must be stored in Neo4j (a fact of record, DEC-001), not in Redis. The lock itself is only single-flight; the fence is what protects the write. Lesson L118 says the same.
5. **Values are text, and replies are text.** The Protocol takes `str`, so the adapter stores the string as it is and does not add JSON encoding. `from_url` sets `decode_responses=True`. A caller that passes its own client must do the same, because the adapter does not decode bytes. `get` has a `cast` for that reason.
6. **Locks and releases.** `acquire_lock` is `INCR` then `SET key token NX PX ttl`; a failed `SET` returns `None`. `release_lock` is a Lua compare-and-delete on the key, so a stale holder cannot remove a newer holder's lock (DEC-058 item 4). TTL is converted to whole milliseconds.
7. **Key families.** Every name the adapter sends is under `diucs:` with one of four families, `cache`, `lock`, `fence` and `chan` (architecture.md). The unit test records every name passed to the client and checks the families.
8. **The Redis contract runs on a shared instance, not a throwaway one.** The contract factory and the two Redis-only cases use the Redis of `infra/docker-compose.yml` (`Settings().redis_url`), which is not a per-session container as Neo4j is (DEC-008). Each case starts and ends by deleting only `diucs:*` keys (`tests/support/redis.py`). Two sessions that run the cache suite at the same moment share that keyspace. The Redis-only cases are under `tests/integration/` and so also start the session's Neo4j harness, because the conftest hook adds `clean_graph` to every integration case. This is accepted for now.
9. **Contract result.** With the redis factory marked `integration`, `pytest -m integration tests/contract` collects 23 cache cases and exits 0. This is the first integration factory that DEC-077 item 4 anticipated.
10. **Not done.** No test kills or restarts the Redis container during a lock or subscription (DEC-077 and lesson L18 show how the harness could do it, but I01 did not add it). No test covers the cross-process window in item 2 or the data loss in item 4. Both are recorded here as residual risks, not as passing guarantees.

Tests: `backend/tests/contract/test_cache_contract.py` (redis id), `backend/tests/unit/adapters/outbound/cache_redis/test_redis_cache.py`, `backend/tests/integration/adapters/outbound/cache_redis/test_redis_cache.py`. Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/I01.md`.

## DEC-091 Fencing tokens after a Redis data loss, and append-only persistence for the local Redis (I01)
Status: accepted (coordinator ruling, builder implementation) · Affects: I01 (`acquire_lock`), I03 (outbox relay lock), I05 and every later holder of a fencing token, `infra/docker-compose.yml`, `infra/README.md`, hazard H-26. Supersedes DEC-089 item 4. Numbered DEC-091: DEC-090 is claimed by `task/P15-idempotency-store` (unmerged), so DEC-091 is the next free number above every branch.

1. **The token is `max(INCR counter, host clock in microseconds)`, and the counter is set to it.** `acquire_lock` runs one Lua script on `diucs:fence:<name>`: it takes the larger of the next INCR value and `time.time_ns() // 1000`, stores that value as the counter, and returns it. Redis runs the script atomically. Tokens strictly increase per name while the counter survives. If Redis loses its data, the counter restarts at 1, but the clock term is the current UTC epoch in microseconds, so a new token is greater than any token issued before the loss, provided the host clock has not stepped backwards.
2. **Residual, recorded and not closed.** (a) A backward step of the host clock, or a host clock reset, can make a new token smaller than an old one. (b) The clock is the host's, not Neo4j's or a single authority's, so two hosts with skewed clocks can issue tokens in an order that differs from real time, though each still increases on its own host. Consequence for consumers: **I03 and I05 must not trust a lock whose token is below the last token they recorded in Neo4j.** The token recorded in Neo4j with the committed write (a fact of record, DEC-001) is the authority. The lock itself stays single-flight.
3. **Hazard H-26 records this risk.** Severity stays `TBD` for the clinical safety officer.
4. **Conflict with the contract suite: open, not resolved here.** DEC-058 item 2 and DEC-075 item 6 say a lock name's first token is 1. The contract cases `test_a_lock_name_starts_its_own_token_count_at_one[redis]` and `test_releasing_a_lock_nobody_holds_does_nothing[redis]` assert `fencing_token == 1` after a fresh acquire. Item 1 cannot give 1 on a fresh counter, because a fresh counter and a lost counter look the same to Redis. The builder has not edited either case (the brief forbids weakening a contract case). Both cases fail on `RedisCache` until the owner or coordinator rules. The memory fake still issues 1, so the fake and the adapter now differ on the value of the first token. Options for the owner: amend DEC-058 item 2 and the two cases to "positive and strictly increasing per name", and change the fake to match; or keep 1 and accept that the fencing guarantee holds only while Redis keeps its data.
5. **Local Redis persistence, a deviation outside I01's file list.** `infra/docker-compose.yml` now starts Redis with `--appendonly yes` and mounts the named volume `redis-data` at `/data`. `infra/README.md` row for Redis is updated to match. Proof: `backend/tests/unit/adapters/outbound/cache_redis/test_compose_redis_persistence.py` reads the compose file and asserts the flag and the volume. The compose file was validated with `docker compose config`. The stack was not restarted, so the flag was not observed on a running container. `down -v` still clears the data, as before.
6. **Tests.** `backend/tests/integration/adapters/outbound/cache_redis/test_redis_cache.py::test_a_token_after_the_counter_is_lost_is_greater_than_every_token_before_it` deletes the counter key after three acquisitions and asserts the next token is greater.

Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/I01.md`.

## DEC-092 Subscribe is async and returns only after the server confirms the subscription (I01, amends DEC-058 item 1 and DEC-089 items 2 and 3)
Status: accepted (coordinator ruling, builder implementation) · Affects: the `Cache` port (`application/ports/outbound/cache.py`, P09), `InMemoryCache` (P09 fake), the contract suite and unit tests of the cache (P12), I01, and every later subscriber of `board.updates` (I05, U08, R06). Numbered DEC-092: highest on `main` and every branch is DEC-091 now.

1. **`subscribe` is `async def`.** Its return value is unchanged: an `AsyncIterator[str]`. Callers write `await cache.subscribe(channel)`. The change is a shared-name change to the port's signature. `docs/spec/contracts.md` is not edited (source artifact rule); the owner must record it in "Additions".
2. **The subscription is active at the server when `subscribe` returns.** The Redis adapter sends SUBSCRIBE, then waits for the server's subscribe reply before it returns. So a publish sent after `subscribe` returns, from any connection, is not lost. DEC-089 item 2's "in-process barrier" is removed: `publish` no longer waits, because `subscribe` already did. DEC-089 item 2's residual (another process missing a publish in the window after subscribe) is closed by this item.
3. **The fake does the same by construction.** `InMemoryCache.subscribe` registers the queue when it is called, so the fake already satisfied the rule; it is now `async` to match the port.
4. **Tests.** `tests/unit/adapters/outbound/cache_redis/test_redis_cache.py::test_subscribe_returns_only_after_the_server_confirms_the_subscription` holds back the server's reply and asserts that `subscribe` is still pending. It was red when the wait was removed (`assert not True`, the task finished at once). The cross-process case `tests/integration/adapters/outbound/cache_redis/test_redis_cache.py::test_a_publish_from_another_connection_right_after_subscribe_is_delivered` (400 rounds, publisher on a second connection) was measured against the adapter without the wait and passed in two runs, and in a separate probe lost 2 of 400 publishes. So that case is a real but probabilistic guard, not a deterministic red. The deterministic test is the held-reply unit test.

## DEC-093 Revision of two P12 contract cases: a lock name's first token is positive, not 1 (I01, revises DEC-075 item 6)
Status: accepted (coordinator ruling, builder implementation) · Affects: `backend/tests/contract/test_cache_contract.py` (two cases), P12, I01, I03, I05. Numbered DEC-093: DEC-090 is claimed by `task/P15-idempotency-store`, DEC-091 and DEC-092 are I01's, so DEC-093 is the next free number.

1. **Which cases.** `test_a_lock_name_starts_its_own_token_count_at_one` and `test_releasing_a_lock_nobody_holds_does_nothing`. Both asserted that the first fencing token for a name is exactly 1.
2. **What changed.** The first case is renamed `test_a_lock_name_gets_a_positive_first_token_of_its_own` and asserts `other.fencing_token > 0` where it asserted `== 1`. The second keeps its name and asserts `lock.fencing_token > 0` where it asserted `== 1`. No other assertion in either case changed, and no other case changed.
3. **Why.** The port promise (DEC-058) is that tokens for one lock name strictly increase. It does not promise that the first token is 1. DEC-091 makes the Redis token `max(INCR, host clock in microseconds)`, so the first token is large by design, and the two cases were over-specified against the port. The in-memory fake still starts at 1, which satisfies the revised assertion.
4. **Red first.** The commit recording this entry was made while both Redis cases still asserted 1. Their failing lines (`assert 1793091110110967 == 1`, `assert 1793091119556943 == 1`) are in the evidence file. The assertion change is the next commit.

## DEC-094 BFF bearer tokens: HS256 checked by a stdlib codec in the domain, no new dependency (R02)
Status: accepted (builder build-R02, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: R02 (`domain/rules/bff_token.py`, `adapters/inbound/fhir_rest/auth.py`, `adapters/testing/tokens.py`), every R-task that routes under `/fhir`, and W02 and W09 (the BFF mints the token).

Numbered DEC-094: DEC-093 is the highest number on main and on every task/* branch when this was written (checked with `git show <branch>:docs/delivery/decisions.md`, lesson L43).

1. **Format and codec.** The FHIR API accepts the BFF's bearer JWT as the prompt sets it: HS256, claims `sub`, `role`, `services`, optional `device`, `exp`. The codec uses only the standard library (hmac, hashlib, base64, json) and lives in `domain/rules/bff_token.py`. PyJWT is not used. A JWT library would be a new dependency and a change to `uv.lock`, which other task branches also edit (the lock change would conflict with other branches' lock edits), and HS256 is HMAC-SHA256. DEC-003 makes this a free choice; the choice stays behind `decode_bff_token`, so it can be swapped.
2. **Checks, in order.** The token is at most `BFF_TOKEN_MAX_LENGTH` (8192) characters, so an oversized token is refused before any parsing. It is ASCII with exactly three parts and no `=` padding. JSON nested too deeply to parse is refused as `TokenRejected`, not left to raise (a `RecursionError` would be a 500). The header's `alg` must be exactly `HS256`, so `none` and every other algorithm are refused. The signature is compared in constant time before the payload is parsed. `exp` is an integer, not a boolean, and the token is refused at or after `exp`, judged on the `now` argument and never on the system clock. `sub` is non-empty text, `role` is a `Role` value, `services` is a list of text (empty is allowed), and `device`, when present, is non-empty text. Any failure raises `TokenRejected`, whose message is fixed.
3. **Secret and clock.** A secret shorter than `BFF_SECRET_MIN_BYTES` (32, the HMAC-SHA256 output size) is refused with `ValueError` on encode and decode. A naive `now` or a naive expiry is `ValidationFailed`, as in DEC-044 item 5.
4. **Not checked, and the residual.** No `iss` or `aud` claim: the prompt names none. No revocation: a token is valid until `exp`. The lifetime is the issuer's choice (W02, W09), not this module's. A token replayed within its lifetime is accepted.
5. **Human-only.** The signing secret's storage is a credential item (DEC-096). Nothing here needs a real secret.

## DEC-095 A 401 is an OperationOutcome with code `login` and `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer` (R02, amends DEC-086 item 2 for 401 only)
Status: accepted (builder build-R02, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: `adapters/inbound/fhir_rest/errors.py` (R01), `auth.py`, every route under `/fhir`.

1. The prompt asks for a 401 with code `login` and `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer`. DEC-086 item 2 maps every other 4xx to `invalid`, which would have made a 401 `invalid`. For 401 only, `_http_error` now returns code `login`, the fixed text "The bearer token is missing, expired or not valid.", and the `WWW-Authenticate` header from the exception. `operation_outcome` takes an optional `headers`. Every other status is unchanged.
2. **Deviation from file ownership.** `errors.py` is R01's file, outside R02's list. The edit is needed for the 401 tests to pass, since they assert the code `login`. It is in the R02 commits with those tests (`tests/integration/adapters/inbound/fhir_rest/test_auth.py`, every case that asserts `login`). This is recorded so the owner sees that R01's module changed.
3. This task produces no 403. A refusal is `NotAllowed`, which R01 answers with a 404 (DEC-086 item 5, DEC-098 item 3). No 403 shape is needed; the refusal bytes are compared to a missing record in `tests/integration/adapters/inbound/fhir_rest/test_auth.py::test_a_refused_role_gets_the_same_body_as_a_missing_record`.

## DEC-096 The BFF secret is read from `request.app.state.secrets` (R02)
Status: accepted (builder build-R02, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: `auth.py`, the later task that serves the FHIR app in a process (it must set the store), the test fixture in `test_auth.py`.

1. The prompt says `SecretStore.get("bff-jwt")`. The composition root has no secret store (`bootstrap/container.py` has none; P10 gives only the port and `FixedSecretStore`), and `ApiContainer` (DEC-087 item 1) provides only `clock` and `uow_factory`. So the store is set on `app.state.secrets` by the process that serves the app. `create_app` is unchanged.
2. **Fail closed.** If the store is absent, or it has no `bff-jwt` (`NotFound`), `current_actor` raises `RuntimeError`. That is a 500 `exception`, and only the class name is logged. A request is never passed on a missing secret.
3. **Human-only.** A real secret store (a vault, with credentials and spend) is not built here. Tests use `TEST_BFF_SECRET`, a synthetic value, and nothing else.

## DEC-097 Staff identity provider: not chosen by R02; the authentication design awaits sign-off (R02)
Status: accepted for the build; the design sign-off is human-only and pending · Affects: R02, W02, W09, the BFF.

1. `architecture.md` (Security, IG and operations) and `observability.md` name Microsoft Entra ID for staff sign-in, and `domain-rules.md` section 247 says the Trust staff account. Entra ID is a default, not a mandate (DEC-003). R02 calls no identity provider: the FHIR API checks only the BFF token (DEC-094). Which provider signs staff in is for the BFF (W02) and needs the Trust tenant, which is a human-only item.
2. **Sign-off is pending.** The authentication design (token format, the role list, the scope rules, DEC-094 to DEC-098) is built here but has not been signed off by the owner or by information governance. The build does not claim that sign-off, and the evidence says it is pending. This does not block R02, because the design is what R02 delivers and the sign-off is a separate human gate.
3. Roles are the ten members of `domain/codes.py` `Role`, exactly. A `wall` token takes its device from `sub`.

## DEC-098 Projections, the reporter rule, and refusals (R02)
Status: accepted (builder build-R02, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: `auth.py` (`ProjectionRegistry`, `PROJECTIONS`), every route from R04 on that returns a Task.

1. **Projection key and label.** A projection is keyed by (resource type, role). When a projection changes a resource, the result gets `meta.security` with code `REDACTED` in system `http://terminology.hl7.org/CodeSystem/v3-ObservationValue`, added to any codes already there. A projection that changes nothing adds no label. The resource given in is not changed.
2. **The reporter rule applies to every Task.** For `reporter`, a Task loses `focus` and `for`. The prompt says "a booking Task", but P02 (DEC-063) writes no `Task.code`, so a booking cannot be told apart by code. Applying the rule to every Task fails closed: a reporter never gets a case reference from any Task. The test is `test_a_reporter_task_with_nothing_to_remove_gets_no_label` and the Task without a code in `test_a_reporter_sees_a_booking_task_without_its_case_and_with_the_label`.
3. **Refusals.** A wrong role, a service out of scope, and a non-patient role each raise `NotAllowed`. R01 answers them with the same 404 body as a missing record, and the integration tests compare the bytes (DEC-086 item 5).
4. **Scope reading.** `ensure_can_act_for` passes for lead, advisor, clinical and audit, anywhere, as the prompt lists. Owner and exec are not in that list, so an owner acts only for services in the token's `services`. This is a reading of the prompt; the owner may change it.
## DEC-099 Board projector: cache key, rebuild and publish order, failure handling, and two deviations (U09)
Status: accepted (builder build-U09, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: U09 (`application/use_cases/board_projector.py`), U08 (`QueryBoard`, read only), I04 and R06 and R05 (consumers of `board.updates` and `board:v1`), H-27 (new hazard), `docs/spec/contracts.md` "Additions". Numbered DEC-099: DEC-098 is the highest number on `main` and on every `task/*` branch when this was written.

1. **Key and time to live.** The board is one JSON value under `board:v1`, with a 60 s time to live (`BOARD_CACHE_TTL`, [ASSUMED] in the prompt). Every rebuild, from an event or from a read, sets the same key and time to live. The time to live is a backstop: a rebuild on each listed event does the real work.
2. **Rebuilding from events.** `handle` rebuilds for the seven names in `REBUILD_EVENTS` and returns False for every other name without touching the cache or the bus. The projector matches names as strings, so `PhoneTaskRaised`, which has no event class yet (U06), is matched by name only.
3. **Store before publish.** `handle` stores the rebuilt board and only then publishes `board-changed`, so a page that refetches on the message finds the new board. Test: `test_the_board_is_stored_before_the_update_is_published`.
4. **Failures in `handle` are raised.** If the store or the publish fails, `handle` raises. The consumer can then retry, and a rebuild is idempotent because it is computed from Neo4j. A silent failure would leave the wall board on the old figure until the time to live ends. Tests: `test_a_failed_publish_is_raised_after_the_board_is_stored`, `test_a_failed_publish_is_raised_so_the_consumer_can_retry`.
5. **Failures in `get_board` are not raised.** A cache read that fails, or a value that does not decode as a board, is a miss: the board is computed through `QueryBoard`. A cache write that fails after a computation is logged by exception class name and the board is still returned. Logs never carry the exception message, because a database or cache error text can hold a value (lesson L111). Tests: the two cache-failure tests and `test_an_unreadable_cached_board_is_replaced_by_a_computed_one`.
6. **The anonymous link is refused before the cache is read.** Otherwise a cached board would reach a role that `QueryBoard` refuses. Test: `test_the_anonymous_link_is_refused_even_when_the_board_is_cached`.
7. **One key for every role.** `QueryBoard` today refuses only the anonymous link, so every other role sees the same board and one key serves them all. Any role-specific filter added later needs its own key (recorded in the module docstring).
8. **The message.** `{"type": "board-changed", "service_id": <id or null>, "at": <ISO 8601, UTC>}`. `service_id` is taken from the event payload when it is a string. No figure, name or patient value is in the message (architecture.md, live updates). The clock gives `at`.
9. **Confirmations come from the batch read.** The board is computed by `QueryBoard`, which uses the batch confirmation read of DEC-088, so the projector adds no per-service query.
10. **Deviation: ReportCapacity is not on `main`.** The prompt's test 5 writes the report "through ReportCapacity", which is U01 and is not yet delivered. It is not in this task's dependencies. The test writes the report through `uow.capacity.add`, the same store path that U08's tests use, and then calls `handle("CapacityReported", ...)` as the report flow would. The test that proves the rebuild does not depend on who wrote the report. When U01 lands, its integration test should also check the board after a real report.
11. **Staleness bound.** If an event is missed or a rebuild fails, the board can be behind by up to 60 s. That bound is the hazard H-27. It is not a guarantee that the board is current; the advisor screen shows the data age (H-01).

Tests: `backend/tests/unit/application/use_cases/test_board_projector.py`, `backend/tests/integration/application/test_board_projector.py`. Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/U09.md`.

## DEC-105 ETag, If-Match and Idempotency-Key: refusal order, codes, replay cache and the lock convention (R03)
Status: accepted (builder build-R03, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: R03 (`adapters/inbound/fhir_rest/concurrency.py`, `errors.py`), every write route from R04 on, `tests/support/things.py` (test-only `:TestThing` store), H-28.

Numbered DEC-105: DEC-104 is the highest number on any branch when this was written. Checked with `git show <branch>:docs/delivery/decisions.md | grep '^## DEC-'` on every branch: DEC-100, DEC-101 and DEC-104 are on `task/U01-report-capacity`, DEC-102 on `task/I02-service-bus`, DEC-103 on `task/I06-twilio-sms`. An earlier R03 draft used DEC-101 and collided with these; it was renumbered here (lesson L43).

1. **Order of checks on a write.** Dependencies run in route-signature order. Authentication is R02's (401, DEC-095), and R03 routes put `current_actor` first. The header rule (If-Match on PUT, Idempotency-Key on POST) runs next. The request body is then read and hashed, then the replay cache, then the use case. A repeat answered from the cache never reaches the use case.
2. **If-Match.** Only the weak form `W/"<n>"`, with n a positive integer without leading zeros, is a version. No If-Match is 428 `required`. Any other value (the strong form `"3"`, `*`, an empty value) cannot match a stored version, so it is 412 `conflict`, not 400. Owner review requested for this 412 choice.
3. **Idempotency-Key.** One to 200 visible ASCII characters (0x21 to 0x7E). A missing or empty header is 400 `required`; a value outside the rule is 400 `invalid`. Neither echoes the value.
4. **Replay.** The first successful (2xx) response is cached for 24 hours under `idem:<sub>:<key>`, where `sub` is the token's subject (the practitioner, or the device for a wall screen). The cache entry holds the SHA-256 of the request body, the status, the headers ETag, Location and Content-Type, and the response body. The request body is never stored. A repeat with the same key and digest gets the stored response with `Idempotent-Replay: true`, and the use case does not run. The same key with a different digest is 422 `conflict` ("This key was used for a different request."). Failed responses (a raised error or a non-2xx return) are not cached, so a refused request can be retried. An unreadable entry is a miss; the durable store still refuses a repeat. A subject that contains a colon is refused, because `idem:<sub>:<key>` could then name two pairs.
5. **Argument names (coordinator ruling 4).** `ReplayCache.lookup(actor, key, body_hash)` and `save(actor, key, body_hash, response)` take the `Actor`, as the prompt gives, and derive the subject with `subject_of`. `replay_or_run(request, actor, key, run)` takes the actor too. The cache is an adapter class, not a port, so no port contract changes.
6. **Residual of item 4.** A successful create's response body is held in Redis for 24 hours. Redis is transient (DEC-001): losing it only removes the fast guard. The body is whatever the route returns, so every create route from R04 must keep patient detail out of its response body. Recorded in H-28.
7. **The lock-before-read ordering is a convention, not an enforced guard.** The version check in `check_version` is only correct if the caller has taken the record's write lock first and then read the version (lesson L63). R03's production code does not take that lock: `check_version` compares two integers. The lock, and the held-write test that proves the ordering, live in the test-only store `tests/support/things.py`. That store takes the lock with a fresh `lockStamp` and reads `versionId` in a second statement. A self-SET of `versionId` was tried first and failed the held-write test, because it wrote back a value read before the lock was granted (lesson L142). R04 and later write routes must follow the same order in their own repositories, and must add a held-write test of their own. Until a real repository enforces it, this is a convention.
8. **Who holds the durable guard.** The use case passes `scoped_key(sub, key)` into its own `IdempotencyStore` (P15) inside its unit of work, and stores its result there. The cache is never the only guard. R03 proves this in the test route only; R04 is the first route that must use it for a real write.
9. **Fail closed.** With no `app.state.cache` set (the process that serves the app sets it, the same pattern as the BFF secret, DEC-096), a write is a 500 `exception`, and the use case does not run.
10. **Exceptions and handlers.** `PreconditionFailed` (412, `conflict`), `PreconditionRequired` (428, `required`) and `HeaderRefused` (the 400 and 422 cases) are adapter exceptions in `errors.py`, with fixed texts. They are not domain errors, because they are about HTTP headers. The 428 text is the prompt's "A write without a version is refused".
11. **Result type of the durable guard.** The test route stores the acting `Actor` (a dataclass in `diucs.application`, so DEC-090 item 2 lets it replay). A real route stores its own command result.
12. **Not built here.** No production store changes. `versioned_response` only writes the ETag and `meta.versionId`. Racing creates on the durable store are proven by P15, not repeated here. R02's no-token 401 test is not an R03 guard and is not repeated here.

Tests: `backend/tests/unit/adapters/inbound/fhir_rest/test_concurrency.py`, `backend/tests/integration/adapters/inbound/fhir_rest/test_concurrency.py`. Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/R03.md`.

## DEC-107 Twilio SMS adapter: sending, status callbacks, errors and settings (I06)
Status: accepted (builder build-I06, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: I06 (`adapters/outbound/sms_twilio/twilio_sms.py`), `application/ports/outbound/messaging.py` (`DeliveryUpdate`, `TransientSendError`, `SendFailedError`), `bootstrap/settings.py`, I07 and I10 (consumers of the status callback and of the send errors), `docs/spec/contracts.md` "Additions". Numbered DEC-107: when this was written, DEC-105 was the highest number on `main`, and DEC-106 the highest on any `task/*` branch (`task/U01-report-capacity`, which also holds DEC-100, DEC-101 and DEC-104; `task/I02-service-bus` holds DEC-102). DEC-103 is on no branch, and DEC-107 is the next free number.

1. **Twilio is the provider, so the prompt's open choice is closed.** The I06 prompt says "Open decision: Twilio or GOV.UK Notify". DEC-002 settles it: Twilio is fixed and I16 is cancelled. The `sms_provider` setting stays, but no code selects between providers. The UK sender number, the data region and the sub-processors stay human-only (DEC-002, `architecture.md`).
2. **httpx becomes a runtime dependency.** The adapter takes an injected `httpx.AsyncClient`. httpx 0.28.1 is already in `uv.lock` in the dev group, so `backend/pyproject.toml` moves it from the dev group to `[project].dependencies`. The lock file changes only its metadata; no package is added. R01 (DEC-070) adds httpx on its own branch, so the two branches will both touch `pyproject.toml`; the merge keeps one entry.
3. **Sending follows the prompt.** The form carries `To` (E.164), `From`, `Body` and `StatusCallback`, with HTTP Basic auth (account SID and auth token). The message id is not sent to Twilio; it appears only in logs. The number is normalised and the text checked before any request, so an invalid number, or empty or whitespace-only text, raises `ValueError` and makes no request.
4. **Responses.** 201 with a `sid` gives `SendReceipt(sid, True)`. A 4xx whose JSON `code` is 21211, 21614 or 21610 gives `SendReceipt("", False)`: Twilio issued no sid, so the provider id is the empty string. 429, 5xx, timeouts and connection errors raise `TransientSendError`. Any other status (another 4xx, or an unexpected 2xx or 3xx) raises `SendFailedError`, which is not retried. This is a choice: a wrong token or a withdrawn sender then stops sending loudly, rather than marking every message as refused.
5. **Residual risk: a retry after a timeout can send a second text.** The Messages API takes no idempotency key on this path, so a request that timed out may have been sent. The consumer (I10) retries with the same message id. A duplicate text is possible; it repeats a request and does not leak data. Recorded for the owner and the clinical safety officer; not mitigated in this task.
6. **UK numbers.** `to_e164_uk` reuses `normalise_uk_mobile` from the identity port, so there is one rule for UK mobiles. It adds one form the identity rule refuses: a bare `44` followed by ten digits starting with 7 (the prompt's test 4). Only mobiles are accepted (national `07`, `+44`, `0044`, bare `44`); a landline is `ValueError`. The error text never includes the number.
7. **Status mapping.** `queued` and `sending` map to `queued`; `sent` to `sent`; `delivered` to `delivered`; `undelivered` and `failed` to `failed`. An unknown status, or a missing `MessageSid` or `MessageStatus`, is `ValueError`. A blank or absent `ErrorCode` is `None`; otherwise the code is kept as text.
8. **Logging (H-09).** Logs carry the message id, the Twilio sid and, for a refusal, the provider's error code. They never carry the number, the body, the token or an exception's text. Tests read the log at DEBUG and assert on each of these. httpx logs each request URL at INFO, and the Twilio URL carries the account SID. The adapter module sets the `httpx` and `httpcore` loggers to WARNING on import, so no INFO line carries the SID. Test: `test_a_send_logs_no_info_line_that_carries_the_account_sid`. Follow-up for O01: the logging configuration must hold `httpx` and `httpcore` at WARNING too, because the level is process-wide and this module-level setting is only a guard for I06. httpx and httpcore are set to WARNING on import, because httpx logs each request URL at INFO and the URL carries the account SID; the O01 logging config must keep the same setting (follow-up).
9. **Shared types live in the port.** `DeliveryUpdate`, `TransientSendError` and `SendFailedError` are in `messaging.py`, so GOV.UK Notify, if it is ever built, can share them. No Twilio name is in the port or in the domain.
10. **Settings.** `twilio_account_sid`, `twilio_auth_token` (a `SecretStr`) and `twilio_from` are added and unset by default. The prompt also asks for `public_base_url`; the existing `base_url` is the public base instead, because two settings for one fact can disagree (principle 5). The composition root, when it is built, forms the status callback from `base_url` and the status path. Decision of build-I06-r2 for this item: `public_base_url` is not added and `base_url` is used. Owner confirmation is not needed for this item.
11. **Test boundary.** Every test goes through an `httpx.MockTransport`. No network, no Twilio account, no SDK. Test values are placeholders: a SID of `AC` and 32 zeros, the token `placeholder-auth-token`, a sender `DIUCS-TEST`. A live send, and a check of the sender number, data region and sub-processors, are human-only.
12. **The two settings tests are characterisation tests, not red tests.** `test_twilio_settings_are_unset_by_default` and `test_twilio_token_is_read_from_environment_and_hidden_from_repr` check defaults and secret handling (the token is a `SecretStr` and does not appear in `repr`). They guard no new behaviour. They passed at the first red commit on this branch, 783f957, as the verifier noted, because the settings fields were added in that same commit. Their red status does not count toward the red evidence for I06; the red tests are the sms_twilio tests. The 2xx/3xx test, `test_a_2xx_or_3xx_other_than_201_is_a_failure_not_a_receipt` (commit b6ec0bc), is a characterisation test too: it passed when added, because the adapter already raises `SendFailedError` for those statuses (item 4).
13. **A 201 with no non-empty `sid` is a malformed reply, not a receipt.** `_accepted_sid` raises `SendFailedError` when the body has no `sid`, or an empty one. It does not return `SendReceipt` with an empty provider id, because a consumer must not store an empty provider id (lesson L141). The error is not retried; the consumer (I10) records the message as failed for staff. A 201 with a non-empty `sid` is unchanged. This refines item 4's 201 rule. The guard was already in the adapter when this item was recorded (`f0b50d2`), so no code changed for it. Test: `test_an_accepted_send_with_an_empty_sid_is_a_failure_not_a_receipt` (commit `02feddd`), a characterisation test; a temporary weakening of the guard made it fail with `DID NOT RAISE SendFailedError`, and the weakening was not committed.

Tests: `backend/tests/unit/adapters/outbound/sms_twilio/test_twilio_sms.py`, `backend/tests/unit/bootstrap/test_settings.py`. Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/I06.md`.

## DEC-108 Reaper for stale diucs-test containers: cutoff, scope and limits (F10)
Status: accepted (builder build-F10-reaper, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: F10 (`tests/support/neo4j.py`, `tests/conftest.py`), every pytest session on a developer machine or in CI that runs Docker, DEC-008, DEC-013, lesson L67. Numbered DEC-108: DEC-107 is the highest number on `main` and on every `task/*` branch, and no branch uses DEC-108.

Context: a pytest run that is killed or times out never reaches the teardown in `throwaway_driver` or `stop_throwaway_neo4j`, so its `diucs-test-neo4j-*` container keeps running. Dozens of these exhausted Docker memory and stopped the Service Bus emulator.

Decision:
1. **Cutoff: 90 minutes from the container's `Created` time** (`docker inspect`). Test runs take under 20 minutes, so a container older than 90 minutes cannot belong to a live run. `REAPER_MAX_AGE` holds the value.
2. **Scope.** A container is removed only if both hold: its name starts with `diucs-test-neo4j-` (`NAME_PREFIX`), and it carries the label `diucs-test=true`. The reaper lists by label, then checks the name prefix and the label again on each inspected container before it removes anything. A labelled container with any other name is kept, and so is an unlabelled one. The shared stack (`diucs-local-neo4j`, port 7687, lesson L67) has neither the label nor the prefix, so the reaper cannot reach it.
3. **When it runs.** `reap_stale_test_containers()` runs once per pytest session, from `pytest_sessionstart` in `tests/conftest.py`. This includes unit-only runs, so a unit run also removes stale containers.
4. **Tolerance.** The reaper never raises. If Docker is missing, or a docker call fails or times out (60 seconds per call), it logs a warning and returns what it has removed so far. A `Created` value that does not parse skips only that container: the reaper logs it and goes on with the rest. A test session is never stopped by the reaper.
5. **Tests.** `tests/unit/support/test_reaper.py` injects a fake Docker runner, so the cases run without Docker: old labelled removed, recent labelled kept, unlabelled kept, a labelled container with another name kept, one bad timestamp skipped while a stale good container is still removed, Docker missing, Docker refusing.
6. **Why this is safe for a live run.** A live run's container is younger than its run, and its run is younger than 20 minutes. The reaper removes only what is older than 90 minutes, so it cannot remove a container from a run that is within its normal length.

What this does not cover:
- **A run that lasts more than 90 minutes.** Another session's reaper removes its container, and the run then fails at its next Neo4j call. No run is expected to last that long, and the harness has no guard against it. If a legitimately long run appears, raise `REAPER_MAX_AGE` in one place.
- **Leftovers accumulate between sessions.** A killed run's container stays until a session starts after its 90-minute mark. If no session starts, it stays longer. The reaper does not run on a timer.
- **Other machines.** The reaper sees only the local Docker daemon. A container on another developer's machine or a CI runner is not affected.
- **Load from live runs.** Several runs at once can still exhaust Docker memory. The reaper does not limit concurrency.
- **Restarted containers.** F11 stops and starts a container. The cutoff uses the original `Created` time, so a container restarted late in a run can look older than it is. A run that restarts Neo4j after 90 minutes would lose its container, the same case as the first limit above.

Tests: `backend/tests/unit/support/test_reaper.py`. Red commit `743f73b`, green commit `f34786a`. Name-prefix and bad-timestamp caveats from the verifier: red commit `de03d7a`, fix in the commit that follows it on the branch.

## DEC-109 Front-end FHIR client: MSW version, fixed BFF error text, and a non-JSON success body (W01)
Status: accepted (builder build-W01, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: W01 (`frontend/lib/fhir/client.ts`, `frontend/lib/bff/errors.ts`, `frontend/test/msw/`, `frontend/package.json`), every route handler that calls the back end (W02 onward).

Numbered DEC-109: DEC-108 is the highest number on `main` and on every `task/*` branch (checked with `git show <branch>:docs/delivery/decisions.md` on each local branch).

1. **MSW 2.15.0, pinned exactly.** The front-end conventions name MSW, and the prompt does not pin a version. 2.15.0 was published on 2026-07-08, more than two weeks before this build. 3.0.3 is newer, but it needs Node 22.12 or later and vite 6 or later as peers, and it falls outside the `msw: ^2.4.9` peer range that `@vitest/mocker` declares, so pnpm would report an unmet peer. Moving to 3.x later is one version change followed by a run of the suite.
2. **BFF error text is a fixed table** (`lib/bff/errors.ts`). The browser receives a code and one message chosen by status. The wording is placeholder API text, not screen wording from `ui-screens.md`, and the owner can replace it in one place. An issue code that is not lower-case letters and hyphens is not echoed; the code falls back to `exception` for 5xx and `invalid` for other statuses.
3. **A 2xx body that is not JSON is a 502 `exception` FhirError.** It is not returned as text in `data`. A non-2xx body that is not an OperationOutcome is replaced by the synthesised outcome `HTTP <status>`, as the prompt says.
4. **Request defaults.** `init` is optional. `method` defaults to GET, or POST when a body is given. This extends the signature in `contracts.md` (Front end) and does not change it.
5. **A network failure keeps no error text.** A rejected `fetch`, or a response body that cannot be read, becomes FhirError 503 with code `transient` and fixed diagnostics, because the underlying error can carry the request URL.
6. **Server-only by convention.** The base URL is read from `process.env` in `lib/fhir/client.ts`, which only route handlers and Server Components should import. No `server-only` package is added. A client component importing the module would not fail the build; W02 may add the package if the owner wants the build to enforce it.

7. **Empty and no-content successes.** A 204, or any 2xx whose body is empty, returns `data: undefined` and does not fail. `etag` is the `ETag` header when the response has one, and `undefined` when it has none.
8. **Request headers.** `Authorization` is sent only when `token` is given. `traceparent` is sent on every request, GET and POST alike, and is generated when the caller gives none. `Idempotency-Key` and `If-Match` are sent only when given. `Content-Type` is sent only with a body. The method defaults as item 4 says.
9. **Issue code in BFF errors.** `toBffError` uses the first issue's code when it is lower-case letters and hyphens, at most 40 characters. Otherwise it uses `invalid` for a 4xx and `exception` for a 5xx. A code that does not match is never echoed.
10. **A public spec example stays in commit history.** The W3C Trace Context sample parent id, which held a 10-digit run, was used in `client.test.ts` in commit 512807c and was replaced by a synthetic value in 2f66d2b. The value is absent from every file on the branch and on `main`, and remains in commit history only. The owner decided, on the round-5 verification, not to rewrite history, because rewriting would invalidate every verification so far. The value is a public W3C specification example, not personal data, so it is accepted in history. The evidence file does not reproduce it.

Tests: `frontend/lib/fhir/client.test.ts`, `frontend/lib/fhir/client.guards.test.ts`, `frontend/lib/fhir/traceparent.test.ts`, `frontend/lib/bff/errors.test.ts`, `frontend/lib/bff/errors.guards.test.ts`, `frontend/test/msw/server.test.ts`. Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/W01.md`.

## DEC-111 The live-update stream: route, message shapes, roles, subscription close and wiring (R06)
Status: accepted (builder build-R06, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: R06 (`adapters/inbound/fhir_rest/routes_events.py`), `app.py` (one router include), the `Cache` port docstring, the in-memory cache fake (P09), W06 (the BFF relay and the wall board), U09 (the message it publishes).

Numbered DEC-111: DEC-109 is taken on `task/W01-bff-foundations` and DEC-110 on `task/I09-email-smtp` (both unmerged), and DEC-108 is the highest on `main`. DEC-111 is the next free number on every `task/*` branch (checked with `git show <branch>:docs/delivery/decisions.md`, lesson L43).

1. **Route.** `GET /events` at the root, not under `/fhir`, as `contracts.md` (non-FHIR routes) gives it. The response is `text/event-stream` with `Cache-Control: no-store`. The stream stores nothing.
2. **Two message shapes, one event.** The R06 prompt names `{serviceId, kind, changedAt}`. The board projector (U09, DEC-099 item 8) publishes `{"type": "board-changed", "service_id", "at"}` and no kind. Following the prompt alone would drop every real message, so the stream accepts both. A projector message becomes `event: board` with `serviceId` and `changedAt`, and no `kind`. The builder does not invent a kind for it. A message with a `kind` must have one of `capacity`, `status` or `booking`, or it is dropped. A message with no usable service id or time is dropped. A projector message with `service_id` null is dropped, because it names no service. Both fields are format-checked: a service id is a FHIR id (`[A-Za-z0-9.-]`, 1 to 64 characters, DEC-053 item 11), and a time is a UTC instant with `Z` that is a real date. A value that fails either check is dropped and never echoed. A whole-board change with no service id therefore does not push; open pages still refetch on the wall's one-minute poll (architecture.md, live updates). Residual, owner to decide: whether W06 needs a whole-board event.
3. **Roles.** `wall`, `advisor`, `clinical`, `lead` and `exec` may open the stream. Every other role gets the R01 not-found body (DEC-086 item 5), and a missing or bad token gets 401 (DEC-095). The stream is not limited by service scope: an event carries only a service id, an optional kind and a time, with no figure, name or patient value.
4. **Subscribe before the response.** The handler awaits `cache.subscribe` before it returns the response, so a message published after the request is answered is not missed (DEC-092). The handler fails closed with a 500 when no cache is set on `app.state.cache`, the same pattern as R03 (DEC-105 item 9).
5. **Keep-alive.** `: keep-alive` is sent after `KEEPALIVE_INTERVAL_SECONDS` (15 s) with nothing published. The read of the next message stays pending across ticks, so a tick never cancels a read.
6. **Closing.** The generator closes its subscription when it ends, by calling `aclose()` on the iterator when it has one. The `Cache` port docstring now says so. The in-memory fake implements it: `aclose` removes the subscriber and wakes a waiting reader. Redis (I01) has its own subscription close (DEC-089 item 3). Residual: Starlette ends the body by cancelling it, and the generator's `finally` runs when the task unwinds. The test waits up to two seconds for the count to fall to zero. A stream that is never iterated keeps its subscription until the process ends. Neither case is a leak in normal use.
7. **Deviations outside the R06 file list.** (a) `app.py` includes the router. The import is local to `create_app`, because `routes_events` imports `auth`, which imports `app`. (b) `memory_cache.py` (P09 fake) gained `aclose` and a `subscriber_count(channel)` helper for tests. The port has no such call. (c) The `Cache.subscribe` docstring in `cache.py` names `aclose`. The signature is unchanged.
8. **Prompt test 7 is partly met, not met.** Test 7 asks for a capacity Observation posted through R04's route, with the U09 projector wired in. R04 is not on `main`, so there is no real trigger path to exercise. The test `test_a_capacity_report_written_through_the_board_projector_streams_one_event` writes the report with `uow.capacity.add`, hand-builds the `CapacityReported` payload, and calls `BoardProjector.handle`. It proves the projector-to-stream leg only. It does not prove the R04 route, its validation, or that the route calls the projector. It also checks the event, not the rebuilt board (the board contents are checked by U09's own tests). What closes test 7: R04 on `main`, then a test that posts through its route and reads the stream. The route the BFF calls is unchanged by that.
9. **Test client.** httpx's ASGI transport waits for a whole body, and a stream never ends. The stream tests drive the ASGI app directly and read the body while the response is open (`_Stream` in the integration test).

Tests: `backend/tests/unit/adapters/inbound/fhir_rest/test_routes_events.py` (the message rules), `backend/tests/integration/adapters/inbound/fhir_rest/test_routes_events.py` (the HTTP stream, roles, keep-alive, close and the projector path), `backend/tests/unit/adapters/testing/test_memory_cache.py` (the fake's close). Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/R06.md`.
## DEC-100 ReportCapacity choices the prompt leaves open (U01)
Status: accepted (builder build-U01, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: U01 (`application/use_cases/report_capacity.py`), U02 (reuses `record_report`), U07 (report flags), U03 (prompts after a report), and every echo that C3 or C4 renders.

Numbered DEC-100: first written as DEC-100, renumbered because DEC-094 to DEC-098 are on task/R02-auth-roles and DEC-099 on task/U09-board-projector. DEC-099 is the highest number on `main` or any task branch (checked with `git show <branch>:docs/delivery/decisions.md` on each branch).

The U01 prompt leaves these open or contradicts itself, and this decision settles them.

1. **The C3 echo gives the next due instant strictly after the report, not "14:00".** The prompt's test 2 records a report at 13:12Z (14:12 BST) and expects "Next prompt 14:00". 14:00 is before the report, so it cannot be the next prompt. The prompt's own sample ("as of 08:06. Next prompt 10:00") also gives the next due after the report. The echo therefore uses `schedule.next_due(profile, now)` (domain-rules 1.4), which gives 16:00 for this sample. Test 2 asserts "Next prompt 16:00". The 14:00 expectation is not kept.
2. **Refusals happen before any write.** A value outside 0 to 999 raises `ValidationFailed("value")`. An effective time after `now` raises `ValidationFailed("effective_at")`, because a figure from the future is a clock error and must not be shown as current. A `replaced_value` outside 0 to 999 raises `ValidationFailed("replaced_value")`. A report flag in the command raises `ValidationFailed("flag")` until U07 records report flags: accepting it here would drop the flag without a trace.
3. **Who may report.** The service is loaded first: an unknown or inactive service raises `NotFound`. Then the permission check. A `REPORTER` may report for a service in `actor.service_ids`, from any source except `advisor-phoned`, because only an advisor or lead may name that source (a reporter cannot claim it). An `ADVISOR` or `LEAD` may report any active service, and only with source `advisor-phoned`. Every other combination raises `NotAllowed`, and nothing is written.
4. **Replay (P15).** `ReportCapacity` takes the idempotency store in its constructor for an early read. The authoritative `get` and `put` run inside the unit of work (`uow.idempotency`), so the first result commits with the change. A replay returns the stored result with `replayed=True` and writes nothing.
5. **Echo recipient and message id.** `record_report(send_echo=...)` takes the performer role id, or None for no echo. The echo is sent only when the source is `text` and a performer is given. The `Communication` and the `SendMessage` payload carry the catalogue id as `message_id` ("C3" or "C4"), because `Communication.message_id` is a catalogue code (contracts.md). The communication is `queued`, and its time is `now`.
6. **Suspended services.** A service with an active `suspended` flag is read as suspended, for the status before and after the report (domain-rules 1.2). A report to a suspended service therefore emits no `StatusChanged`. Its `CapacityReported` is still emitted.
7. **Audit snapshot.** The snapshot holds `value`, `source`, `status_before` and `status_after`, as the prompt says. The audit log canonicalises enums to their text.

Tests: `backend/tests/integration/application/test_report_capacity.py`. Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/U01.md`.

## DEC-101 Prompt test 2 expects Next prompt 14:00: the echo keeps 16:00 (U01)
Status: accepted (builder decision, build-U01, for the owner to confirm; recorded after the verifier's FAIL at 9bcbc7e) · Collision: DEC-101 is also on task/I06-twilio-sms and task/R03-etag-idempotency; whichever of the two merges second renumbers in the same commit (lesson L43) · Affects: U01 test 2 (`docs/plan/prompts/U01.md`), `backend/tests/integration/application/test_report_capacity.py`, every C3 echo, and U02 (reuses `record_report`).

1. The prompt's test 2 says a report recorded at 13:12Z (14:12 BST) gives "Next prompt 14:00". 14:00 BST is 13:00Z, which is before that report, so it cannot be the next prompt after it. The next due after the report is 16:00 BST by the two-hourly rule (domain-rules 1.4). The code and the test give 16:00. The prompt's expectation is wrong; the code is right.
2. The decision is 16:00. The prompt snapshot `docs/plan/prompts/U01.md` is not edited, because it is a snapshot. The test asserts 16:00 with a comment that cites this entry.
3. A prompt expectation of a next prompt earlier than the report that produces it cannot be met by any correct implementation. Check such expectations against the clock before building to them.

## DEC-104 A reused command key with a different body is refused with Conflict (U01)
Status: accepted (builder decision, build-U01, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: U01 (`application/use_cases/report_capacity.py`), U02 (reuses `record_report` and the same replay path), and every command that replays through `IdempotencyStore` (P15).

1. A replay is the same command. The key is matched to a fingerprint: a SHA-256 of the command body and of the actor who sent it (service, value, source, effective time, performer role, replaced value, report flag, the actor's role and practitioner id). An identical replay returns the stored result with `replayed=True`. A replay whose fingerprint differs raises `Conflict` and writes nothing.
2. The stored record is `_StoredReport(fingerprint, result)`, written through `IdempotencyStore` in the same unit of work as the change (DEC-090 item 4).
3. The actor is part of the fingerprint. Without it, a second person holding a key could read the first person's result, and the early replay read runs before the permission check.
4. The check runs on the early read and again inside the unit of work (the authoritative check). Tests: `test_a_reused_key_with_a_different_body_is_refused`, `test_the_same_key_twice_replays_the_first_result`, `test_a_replay_is_decided_inside_the_unit_of_work`.
5. Known limit, for the owner: a key used again after a different actor's report is refused rather than replayed, so an honest retry by a different person needs a new key.

## DEC-106 A stored command key is decided before any other check, and again inside the unit of work (U01, amends DEC-104 item 4)
Status: accepted (builder decision, build-U01, for the owner to confirm) · Number: DEC-105 is on main and task/R03-etag-idempotency; DEC-106 is unused on main and on every task/* branch (checked with `git show <branch>:docs/delivery/decisions.md`) · Affects: U01 (`application/use_cases/report_capacity.py`), U02 (reuses the same path), every command that replays through `IdempotencyStore` (P15).

1. A command key that already has a stored result is decided before the value, flag, effective-time and permission checks. An identical command (same body and same actor, DEC-104 item 1) returns the stored result with `replayed=True`. A different command raises `Conflict`. Neither writes anything. A different command is refused even when it would be refused on its own terms, so a reused key never reports a different outcome.
2. Two requests with the same key can both miss the early read, so the same decision is taken again inside the unit of work, before any write. A stored result found there is returned with `replayed=True` and the unit of work commits nothing new.
3. Item 4 of DEC-104 described this authoritative check, but the code at 5a66ca5 did not run it. This decision closes that gap. The test is `test_a_replay_is_decided_inside_the_unit_of_work`.
4. The known limit in DEC-104 item 5 stands: a key used by a different actor is refused with `Conflict`, not replayed.

## DEC-102 Service Bus adapter: cancel no-op rule, emulator timing limits, session receive window (I02)
Status: accepted (orchestrator decision, build-I02; corrected after independent verification of 3dff98a) · Affects: I02; I03 (outbox relay calls cancel), I05 (consumer of scheduled.work and capacity topic). Numbered DEC-102: DEC-101 is taken on task/U01-report-capacity, task/I06-twilio-sms and verify-U01-scratch-r2 (checked with `git show <branch>:docs/delivery/decisions.md | grep '^## DEC-'` on every task branch).

1. **The port contract stands.** Cancel of an unknown or delivered number, or of a number held by another queue, does nothing. The contract cases are not skipped and their assertions are not weakened. The emulator refusal is recorded on the case itself with the `emulator_limit` marker (`backend/tests/contract/test_event_bus_contract.py`, `test_cancelling_an_unknown_or_delivered_number_does_nothing`), which names the case, the emulator version and the error.
2. **The guard.** `is_unscheduled_cancel(error, sequence_number)` is true only when the error is exactly `ServiceBusError` (a subclass is a different failure and is raised), its text matches `SequenceNumber = N is already being cancelled`, and N equals the requested number. The SDK exposes no typed reason for this refusal; the observed refusal is status 400, condition `amqp:not-allowed`. Every other `ServiceBusError` is raised. Tests: `test_a_subclass_with_the_refusal_text_is_not_recognised` and `test_a_subclass_with_the_refusal_text_is_raised_from_cancel` (unit suite). Mutant checks: an `isinstance` guard fails 2 tests; a removed guard fails the subclass test.
3. **Emulator timing defect (measured).** On emulator 2.0.1 a cancel that returns success does not stop a scheduled message whose due time is under about 16 s away: the message is delivered at its due time under a new sequence number. Measured again on 2026-10-27 at the due times below (cancel returned success each time): due 3 s delivered; due 10 s delivered; due 17 s held (not delivered). The earlier series was 3, 5, 10, 15 s fail and 17, 19, 22, 25, 30 s work. The contract case `test_a_cancelled_message_is_never_received` keeps its assertion and uses `CANCEL_DELAY` = 30 s, which applies to every factory. The adapter's emulator cancel test (`test_a_scheduled_message_that_is_cancelled_never_arrives`) schedules 30 s ahead and waits 33 s. Consumers of `scheduled.work` must still re-check their state on arrival. Production Azure Service Bus behaviour at short delays is not measured here and is a human-only check.
4. **Session receive window.** A receive on the session-enabled subscription with `NEXT_AVAILABLE` opens its link with a window of `max(wait, 5 s)`, capped at `SESSION_OPEN_LIMIT` = 10 s (`session_open_window` in `backend/tests/contract/factories.py`). The session client is created with `retry_total=0`, so an empty open fails at once instead of retrying for about 40 s; the emulator's empty answer is the open timeout, which is no message for a topic subscription only. A timed-out queue open is raised. Tests: `test_a_session_open_window_never_exceeds_the_budget` (10.0 exact), `test_a_short_wait_gets_the_minimum_session_open_window` (5.0), `test_a_timed_out_topic_session_open_is_no_message`, `test_a_timed_out_queue_open_is_raised_not_swallowed`, `test_the_factory_passes_a_bounded_window_to_the_session_receiver`. The original 0.3 s window raised `OperationTimeoutError` in all 12 servicebus contract cases (lesson L127; recorded in evidence).
5. **Unknown topic is `ValidationFailed`.** The prompt's test 3 says `ValueError`. The port docstring and the contract suite say `ValidationFailed`, so the port wins. A naive enqueue time is refused with `ValidationFailed` in the adapter as well (`ServiceBusEventBus.schedule`).
6. **SDK body.** `ServiceBusMessage(body=bytes).body` is a generator of chunks. Tests read it with `b"".join(...)`.
7. **Unique ids.** Emulator tests that send to `inbound.replies` use a unique message id per run (lessons L6 and L101).
8. **Direct send in one integration case.** `test_a_queue_message_keeps_its_message_id` (`backend/tests/integration/adapters/outbound/bus_servicebus/test_service_bus.py`) sends through the SDK queue sender directly, bypassing `publish()`, because the port's `publish()` takes topics only. The case uses the adapter's own `to_service_bus_message` mapping, so the message id it checks is the one the adapter sets.
9. **Characterisation changes (no red test before the change; each has a characterisation test that fails if reverted, `backend/tests/contract/test_characterisation.py`, checked by reverting each value).** Recorded here with the reason, because each is a timing or harness value that the emulator and the clock decide, not a rule a unit test can fail on:
   - `CANCEL_DELAY` in `test_event_bus_contract.py` changed from 2 s to 30 s. Reason: the emulator defect in item 3 (2 s and 10 s are delivered). Proven by the measurement in item 3 and by the contract run passing at 30 s.
   - The session open window in `factories.py` changed to `max(wait, 5 s)` capped at 10 s, from a 5 s window with no cap. Reason: item 4. The cap is proven by the red test that failed on the unbounded value (3600 > 10).
