   - `retry_total=0` on the session client (`factories.py`, `tests/support/servicebus.py` `client(**options)`). Reason: setup per servicebus case fell from about 40 s to about 10 s, and the full contract run from 702 s to 271 s. Not unit-testable; the contract run is the proof.

10. **Drain reads leftovers without parsing.** The factory's drain takes what the emulator still holds before a case, including a message another run left that is not this contract's JSON (seen as `svc-1 update` on the shared emulator, which stopped the case in setup). `_take` completes the message unparsed; `_receive_from` parses. Red test: `test_the_drain_removes_a_leftover_it_cannot_read` (`backend/tests/contract/test_factory_drain.py`), which failed with `JSONDecodeError` before the change.

11. **Cross-run isolation on the shared Redis (cache suites).** The contract's Redis harness flushed every `diucs:*` key at start and close, so two overlapping cache suites (from two worktrees) deleted each other's keys: two overlapping runs failed 6 to 12 cache cases each, a single run passed. `RedisCache` takes `key_prefix` (default `diucs:`, production unchanged), and the contract harness uses `diucs-contract-<uuid>:`, flushing only that prefix. Red tests first: `test_redis_isolation.py` (both failed before the change, `assert None == 'accepting'`). The integration suite `tests/integration/adapters/outbound/cache_redis` was moved onto its own per-run prefix in item 13 (`825bd78`).

12. **Cache TTL cases check away from the expiry instant; the stored TTL is read from the store.** The contract's TTL cases checked the key or lock at exactly its TTL on a real Redis. A probe that repeats the case's sequence (set 2 s TTL, sleep 1 s, get, sleep 1 s, get) found the key still present at the second check in 2 of 60 runs, with 12 ms of TTL left. The cases now leave at least 1 s between each check and the expiry they test (`test_a_key_is_present_before_its_ttl` with a 10 s TTL; `test_a_key_is_missing_once_its_ttl_has_passed`; `test_a_lock_is_free_once_its_ttl_has_passed`). The slack has a cost: a cache that stored half the TTL passed all 24 Redis cache cases. The key path is now checked without a sleep: `test_a_key_is_stored_with_the_ttl_it_was_given` (`backend/tests/contract/test_redis_ttl.py`) reads the store's own PTTL after a 10 s set and requires it within 500 ms of 10 s. Mutant: `px // 2` in `RedisCache.set` fails it (`assert 9500 <= 4989`), and the 24 cache cases still pass. The exact expiry instant is checked only on the in-memory fake's fixed clock, not on the store's clock: `test_a_lock_is_held_one_millisecond_before_its_ttl_ends_and_free_at_the_instant` (`backend/tests/unit/adapters/testing/test_memory_cache.py`). A wall-clock expiry check on a real store has no deterministic red; the store-reported PTTL is the deterministic check.
13. **Integration cache suite on a per-run prefix.** Five overlapping pairs of `tests/integration/adapters/outbound/cache_redis` before the change failed in 4 of 5 rounds (`assert 2 == 1` on the channel count from another run's subscriber; a deleted fence key). Red test `test_a_subscriber_of_another_run_is_not_counted` (`6d1327f`). Fix (`825bd78`): each case uses `diucs-integration-<uuid>:`; the default `diucs:*` flush helper is removed.

Tests: `backend/tests/unit/adapters/outbound/bus_servicebus/test_service_bus.py`, `backend/tests/integration/adapters/outbound/bus_servicebus/test_service_bus.py`, `backend/tests/contract/test_event_bus_contract.py`, `backend/tests/contract/test_session_window.py`, `backend/tests/contract/factories.py`. Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/I02.md`.
## DEC-110 SMTP email adapter: relay settings, error mapping, the footer gap, and logging (I09)
Status: accepted (builder build-I09, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: I09 (`adapters/outbound/email_smtp/smtp_email.py`), `bootstrap/settings.py`, I10 (consumer of `EmailSender`), D15 (email footer), `docs/spec/contracts.md` Additions. Numbered DEC-110: DEC-109 is taken on `task/W01-bff-foundations` (lesson L43), and DEC-109 is the highest number on any branch before this entry.

1. **Layering.** Adapters may not import `bootstrap` (the layers contract). `SmtpEmail` therefore takes a structural `SmtpSettings` Protocol that `Settings` satisfies. The prompt's `SmtpEmail(settings)` shape is kept.
2. **Relay settings.** `smtp_host` is unset by default. `send` then raises `SendFailedError` ("the SMTP relay is not configured") and never falls back to localhost. Defaults: `smtp_port` 587, `smtp_starttls` True, `smtp_user` and `smtp_password` (a `SecretStr`) unset, `email_from` `DIUCS Capacity <capacity@diucs.example>`. The choice between an SMTP relay and a transactional provider is still open. The port hides it and no provider-specific header is added. The provider and the real credentials are human-only.
3. **Replies.** A 4xx answer from RCPT or DATA is `TransientSendError`. A refusal where every recipient gets a 5xx is `SendReceipt("", False)`: no Message-ID is returned for a refused send (as DEC-107 item 4 and lesson L141). Any other 5xx (for example 554 on DATA) is `SendFailedError` and is not retried. Connection loss, timeouts and `OSError` are `TransientSendError`. Server reply text never goes into an error.
4. **Residual: a retry can send twice.** A timeout or a 4xx after DATA can mean the relay already queued the message. The consumer's retry then sends a second email. Each attempt gets a new Message-ID (`<uuid4@diucs.example>`), so the relay cannot match the duplicate. Not mitigated here. It is the same risk as DEC-107 item 5 and is for the owner and the clinical safety officer.
5. **Link label.** The port carries no link label. When `html` is None, the label is the words before the URL in its sentence ("Confirm or update"), or "Open link" if there are none. `text_to_html` escapes all text, turns each URL into one button-style anchor, and does not repeat the label.
6. **Logging (H-09).** The adapter logs the generated Message-ID, an SMTP status code and the exception class name only. It never logs an address, a body or an exception's text, since the server's reply can echo the address. `aiosmtplib` 5.1.3 logs nothing (checked in the installed package). The send port has no message id, so the outbox message id is logged by I10, not here. The log test was changed after the red commit: the in-process aiosmtpd server logs what it receives under `mail.log`. That server stands in for the relay, so the test checks the other records. The change is disclosed in the evidence file.
7. **Footer gap, not fixed here.** The screen footer (`ui-screens.md` line 1046) is "The link works once, needs no login, and expires at 10:30. This email has no patient information. Replies to this address are not read." The D15 template footer (`domain/messages/templates.py`, `FOOTER`) omits the last sentence. This adapter adds no copy, and D15 is delivered, so the template is not edited here. A D15 correction is a follow-up. Email wording is IG-pending (DEC-042 item 3), so no real email goes out until the corrected wording is approved in writing.
8. **Dependencies.** `aiosmtplib` 5.1.3 is a runtime dependency. `aiosmtpd` 1.4.6 is a dev dependency, used only as the in-process test server. Both are pinned in `uv.lock`.

Tests: `backend/tests/unit/adapters/outbound/email_smtp/test_smtp_email.py`. Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/I09.md`.

## DEC-122 Base UI components: white has no token, Card is built, empty stepper steps to min (W03)
Status: accepted (builder decision, owner to confirm) · Affects: W03 and every screen that uses `components/ui`.

1. **White.** The prompt asks for white backgrounds and white text on primary, but `tokens.css` has no white token, and `tokens.test.ts` pins the colour set to the design-system table, so a token cannot be added inside W03. The components use the CSS system colour `Canvas` (white in this light-only app, and it follows forced-colours mode). No hex, rgb or named colour appears in any component stylesheet (`styles.test.ts`). The owner may add `--c-white` to the design system, after which `Canvas` is replaced in one pass.
2. **Card.** The prompt's goal and rules describe a Card, but the file list omits it. `Card.tsx` and `Card.module.css` are built, with `index.ts` exporting it, so the goal is met. The heading prop is `heading` with `headingLevel` (2 to 6, default 2); the section is named by its heading.
3. **Stepper from empty.** Decrease from `null` gives `min` (prompt). Increase from `null` also gives `min`, because no figure has been chosen and one step lands on the lowest allowed value. Clearing the input reports `null`.
4. **Option button shape.** `OptionButton` takes `selected` and `onSelect`; its accessible name includes the hint. Button `Button` takes `onClick` and `type` as well as the prompt's props.

## DEC-125 BFF session cookie and CSRF: choices beyond the prompt (W02)
Status: accepted (builder decision, owner to confirm) · Affects: W02 (`frontend/lib/auth/session.ts`, `csrf.ts`, `lib/bff/handler.ts`, `app/api/bff/session/route.ts`), every BFF route (W09 onward).

1. **Dependency.** `jose` 6.1.0 is added to `frontend/package.json` as an exact dependency (the prompt names it). Age check (DEC-109 item 1, at least two weeks before the build): the npm registry's per-version publish time for 6.1.0 is 2025-08-27T21:43:57Z, so it qualifies. The 2026-10-20 date seen on the package page is the package's metadata `modified` field, which changes whenever the package record changes, not a release date. The newest release that meets the two-week rule is 6.2.12 (2026-09-05); 6.2.13 (2026-10-20) does not. Decision: keep 6.1.0 (no version change, no re-run needed for that reason). Revisit when a later release is at least two weeks old and a security fix is needed. Tests for these modules run in the node environment (`// @vitest-environment node`), because jose rejects jsdom's Uint8Array.
2. **Session has `expiresAt`.** The prompt's `Session` shape carries it, contracts.md did not; contracts.md is updated. It is checked twice: jose `exp` (whole seconds) and `expiresAt` itself (`<=` now is void). A session already expired is refused at seal time.
3. **Secret.** `SESSION_SECRET` must be base64 of exactly 32 bytes. A missing or malformed value throws an error naming the variable only; `withBff` turns it into 500, never into "no session" or a pass.
4. **Request reads are pure.** `withBff`, `checkCsrf` and `sessionFromRequest` read the `Cookie` header of the request, matched by exact name. `getSession()` (Next cookie store) is for Server Components. Writes use `cookies().set`.
5. **CSRF default.** `withBff` checks CSRF on POST, PUT, PATCH and DELETE always; `csrf: true` adds the check to other methods; `csrf: false` cannot switch it off for an unsafe method. Order: session (401), role (403 forbidden), CSRF (403 csrf).
6. **Constant time.** Header and cookie are hashed with SHA-256 and the digests compared with `timingSafeEqual`, so length never changes the path. An empty header or cookie fails.
7. **GET keeps the CSRF token.** If the `diucs_csrf` cookie already holds a 43-character base64url value it is returned, not rotated, so a second tab keeps working; otherwise a new one is issued.
8. **No-store.** Session responses, the withBff refusals and the responses withBff builds from thrown errors send `Cache-Control: no-store` (set in `withBff`; `lib/bff/errors.ts` is not changed).
9. **Cookie attributes.** Session: HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict, Path=/, Secure unless `NODE_ENV` is `development`, no Domain, Max-Age to expiry. CSRF: same, not HttpOnly. Clearing uses the same attributes with Max-Age 0.
10. **Residuals.** No server-side revocation: a stolen cookie works until `expiresAt` (the lifetime is chosen by the sign-in task, W09). DELETE with an expired cookie answers 401 and the browser drops the cookie at its Max-Age. The `server-only` package (DEC-109 item 6) is not added.

Tests: `frontend/lib/auth/session.test.ts`, `csrf.test.ts`, `frontend/lib/bff/handler.test.ts`, `frontend/app/api/bff/session/route.test.ts`. Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/W02.md`.

## DEC-128 Test harness containers are removed with their anonymous volumes (F10-fix)
Status: accepted · Affects: `backend/tests/support/neo4j.py`, every pytest run that uses the Neo4j harness. Amends DEC-013 (which says the harness removes its container).

- The neo4j image declares anonymous volumes for `/data` and `/logs`. `docker rm -f` leaves them behind, so every pytest run leaked two volumes (measured: 70 volumes, 18 GB in about one hour with four parallel builders, which used up the machine's disk allowance).
- Decision: both removal paths, `stop_throwaway_neo4j` and `reap_stale_test_containers`, call `docker rm -f -v <name>`. The reaper's name-prefix and label checks are unchanged.
- Cleaning existing leaks: `docker volume prune -f` removes only volumes that no container references, so it is safe while other builders' containers run.

## DEC-129 UI tasks build from the repo snapshot of the design canvas; changes are collected for v1.1
Status: accepted (owner instruction) · Affects: every UI task (W-series, F09 follow-ons, X-series UI). Supersedes the build hold in lesson L136.

- The live design canvas has moved past the snapshot in `docs/spec/sources.json` (L136). The owner has ruled that the repo's `docs/spec/ui-screens.md` and `docs/design/` are the specification for this build, and that UI tasks proceed without waiting for a canvas refresh.
- Any difference a builder or verifier notices between the repo snapshot and the live canvas is not a failure. Record it in the task's evidence under "Canvas differences" (what the snapshot says, what the live canvas says, if known) so it can be gathered for v1.1.
- Once every task in the plan is delivered, the owner will run a v1.1 pass for required changes, including the canvas refresh. W03 was delivered before this ruling and its canvas comparison was not run (verifier note); it is included in the v1.1 pass.
- The source-of-truth artifacts are not edited.

## DEC-130 The platform is multi-tenant: a tenant id is carried on every node and bound at one execution point
Status: accepted (owner instruction: "it will be multi tenanted; it was missing from the specifications") · Affects: every backend task that reads or writes the graph, the audit chain, the outbox, idempotency, Redis keys, Service Bus messages, the BFF session and the API. The source-of-truth specs are not edited (honesty rules); this decision records the gap and the owner reconciles the specs in v1.1.

Findings that make this tractable (checked in the code, 2026-10-28): all 78 Cypher statements are parametrised `NamedQuery` objects in one registry, and `driver.run_query` (adapters/outbound/graph_store/driver.py) is the only call site that executes application Cypher (migrations, the health ping and the test wipe are the exceptions). Two statements use f-strings, and only to splice constant fragments.

1. **Tenant model.** A tenant is the organisation that owns a service directory and its reporters (for example one ICB or SPA operator). Property name `tenantId` (string). Every node carries it, including the nodes the mapper writes as fragments (G02 to G05) and version copies. Relationships do not carry it: a relationship is in the tenant of the nodes it joins, and no query may join nodes of two tenants.
2. **One binding point.** The tenant is bound in a context for the unit of work (a context variable set at the edge). `run_query` adds `tenant=<bound tenant>` to every call and refuses to run, with a fixed-message error, when no tenant is bound. A caller cannot pass or override `tenant`.
3. **The predicate cannot be injected automatically.** Rewriting Cypher text is unsafe. Every statement writes `tenantId: $tenant` on each node it matches, merges or creates. `NamedQuery` registration rejects any statement that does not mention `$tenant`, unless it is on a short explicit allowlist of global statements (schema migrations, health ping, reference-data reads decided under item 5).
4. **The real proof is behavioural.** A two-tenant test seeds both tenants, then runs every registered query as tenant A and asserts it returns nothing that belongs to tenant B, and that every write leaves tenant B unchanged. The test is generated from the registry, so a new query is covered automatically.
5. **Reference data** (Concept nodes and code systems) is shared and read-only across tenants unless a task finds it must differ; it is never written by a tenant request. Identifier nodes are tenant-scoped.
6. **Tenant comes only from a verified source:** the signed bearer token or BFF session for requests, and the validated message properties for workers. Never a request body, query string or header the caller controls. Staff sign-in maps a user to exactly one tenant.
7. **Uniqueness becomes composite** (`tenantId` plus the current key) in a migration. Composite uniqueness works on Neo4j Community 5.26 (lesson L126). The audit chain, outbox, idempotency keys, link-token hashes, Redis keys (prefix `diucs:<tenant>:`) and Service Bus message properties become tenant-scoped. The audit hash chain is one chain per tenant.
8. **Cross-tenant access** (the support console, O-series) is a separate, explicit, audited privilege, not a default. Open: which roles may hold it. Until decided, no role does.
9. **Tests.** The harness creates a unique tenant per run and `clean_graph` deletes only that tenant's nodes, so runs can share one Neo4j and run in parallel. Schema-changing and container-lifecycle tests still run alone. Until the tenancy work is delivered the harness keeps one Neo4j per run and agents run Neo4j tests one at a time.
10. **Sequence.** First the seam (items 2, 3, 4 and the test harness), so every new query is written tenant-aware. Then the existing 78 statements and the mapper, the audit chain, then Redis, Service Bus and the BFF/API. In-flight tasks (U02, U03, I04, W02) finish on the current model and are migrated by the sweep.

## DEC-131 SMS and email are one Twilio subaccount per tenant; the inbound number identifies the tenant
Status: accepted (owner instruction) · Affects: I06, I07, I09, I10 and every task that sends or receives a text or an email. Refines DEC-002 and DEC-130.

1. **One subaccount per tenant.** Each tenant has its own Twilio subaccount with its own sender number(s). Email follows the same pattern with a per-tenant sender identity and credentials (the product, for example a SendGrid subuser, is chosen under DEC-003 when I09 is revisited). Account creation, numbers, sender registration and spend stay human-only.
2. **Inbound SMS resolves the tenant by the number called (`To`) and `AccountSid`,** looked up in the tenant's configuration. The tenant is never taken from the message body or the sender. An unknown number gets a fixed empty answer and a log of the id only. The webhook signature is then verified with that subaccount's auth token, and a failure is a 403 with a fixed message. Delivery receipts are matched by `AccountSid` and message id.
3. **The shared-phone problem is solved at the channel for SMS.** A person's mobile number can belong to a reporter in two tenants as two separate Person records. A reply reaches the right tenant because it arrives on that tenant's number. Booking references (four digits) are unique within a tenant, and a reply is matched only inside the tenant its number resolves to.
4. **Credentials are never stored on graph nodes.** The tenant record holds a secret reference id, and the secret store holds the auth token. Tests use a fake secret store.
5. **Still to bind separately:** link tokens (the token hash lookup yields the tenant), pairing codes and device credentials (bound to the tenant at pairing), text verification codes (issued on the tenant's number) and push subscriptions.

## DEC-132 Tenant is the legal entity; organisations collaborate in a Network through grants and read-only projections
Status: accepted (owner instruction, five answers recorded below) · Affects: DEC-130 items 1, 5 and 8; every task that shows capacity to more than one organisation (board, wall screen, advisor views, week summary); tenant administration. Refines DEC-130 and DEC-131.

Owner answers: (1) network members see the network capacity; (2) what is shared today is capacity, the service directory and criteria (the NHS Directory of Services will be integrated later; for now directory data is entered manually); (3) a lead organisation hosts the platform and is the network owner; (4) when an organisation leaves, its history stays; (5) there is one network with tenants in it; today's need is to share capacity, and a shared patient record and care coordination tool is a much later possibility.

1. **Tenant = legal entity.** Each organisation that feeds the platform is one tenant and the data controller for its own data. DEC-130 isolation applies between tenants without exception. Nothing in a tenant's scope is ever readable by another tenant.
2. **Network.** A `Network` node (id, name, owner tenant, status) groups member tenants. The **network owner** is the lead hosting organisation, itself a tenant. It administers membership and holds the data sharing agreement (the agreement's reference and version are stored, never its content). The owner gains no read access to members' private data by being the owner.
3. **Membership and Grant.** A `NetworkMembership` (network, tenant, status, joined, left) and a `Grant` (grantor tenant, network, scope, purpose, effective from, effective to, approved by the grantor's administrator and by the network owner, version) are the only way data is shared. Scopes today: `capacity`, `directory`, `criteria`. Purpose today: "network capacity view". A grantor can end a grant at any time; it stops future sharing only.
4. **Projections, never cross-tenant queries.** A projector worker reads a member's data inside that member's tenant scope and writes a minimum, allowlisted copy into the network scope (a scope with its own id, treated like a tenant by DEC-130; each copied node records `sourceTenantId`). Members read the network capacity view through a read-only use case bound to the network scope after checking membership and the grant. No query joins nodes of two scopes, and a unit of work binds exactly one scope. Allowed fields: capacity status and figures, as-of time and not-updated flag; service name, category, location, district coverage, opening hours, service-level contact; the current criteria version. Never shared: reporters' names, phone numbers, devices, PINs, link tokens, communications, bookings, cases, audit entries.
5. **Who sees the network view.** Staff of a member tenant whose role may see the board see the network capacity view as well as their own tenant's. Reporters on phone and link see their own tenant only. A wall screen is paired to one tenant and is set by that tenant's administrator to show the tenant board or the network board (UI task to decide the control).
6. **History stays.** When a tenant leaves, its membership ends and its grants lapse. Projections already written stay in the network scope for audit and history, marked with the leave date. No new data is projected. Deleting a tenant's own data follows its own retention, not the network's.
7. **Future patient-level coordination is out of scope** and would need a new grant scope, a new purpose, a new data sharing agreement, information-governance and clinical sign-off, and its own decision. The projection allowlist is enforced by the privacy guard (D17) and a test, so no patient-level field can enter a projection. The directory projection carries a `source` property (manual today, Directory of Services later) so the integration does not change the model.
8. **Cross-tenant support and operator access** remains a separate, explicit, audited privilege that no role holds yet (DEC-130 item 8).
9. **Human-only:** the data sharing agreement, controller and processor roles, and information-governance approval of the grants and the projection allowlist.

## DEC-133 No human gates, and one `main` branch
Status: accepted (owner instruction) · Affects: the diucs-delivery skill, DEC-006, DEC-042, DEC-050, DEC-062, DEC-130 item 8, DEC-131 item 1, DEC-132 item 9, and every earlier decision that left a question pending for a person.

1. **Nothing waits for a human.** Information governance, clinical safety, data sharing, message wording, retention, tenancy design, spec changes and pushing to the remote are decided by the lead developer, recorded in this file, built and carried on with. The owner is told only when genuinely stuck: every remaining task needs something that does not exist in this environment (a real credential, account or spend), a limit cannot be cleared, or an action would be irreversible and outside the repo.
2. **Truthfulness stays.** Nothing may state that an external certification or approval exists when it does not (clinical safety sign-off, DPIA, DTAC, penetration test, information-governance approval). Status is described as "prepared, not externally certified". This never blocks a task.
3. **Real credentials** (Twilio subaccounts and numbers, Entra, hosting) are built and verified against fakes and listed as "needs a real credential at deploy".
4. **Specs are kept current by the lead developer.** `docs/spec/`, `docs/design/`, the plan and prompts are updated in the same change as the decision that alters them. The live claude.ai artifacts are not editable here; differences are recorded in the evidence.
5. **One `main`.** `main` is the only long-lived branch, on the remote and locally. Task branches are short-lived: created in a worktree, fast-forwarded into `main`, `main` pushed after every delivery, then the branch and worktree deleted. Nothing else is kept on the remote. Commits that must survive as evidence are tagged (`evidence/<name>`), not left on a branch. `main` is never force-pushed.
6. The delivery ledger is backed up into `docs/delivery/` (`delivery.py render --out docs/delivery`) at every stage gate and whenever branches are consolidated, so a fresh clone can restore it.

## DEC-134 Tenancy: answers to the audit's open questions
Status: accepted · Affects: the tenancy plan (`docs/delivery/tenancy-plan.md`), DEC-130 items 3, 5 and 8, DEC-132 items 3 to 5. Decided under DEC-133 from the seven audits in `docs/delivery/tenancy-audit/`.

1. **Concept, CodeSystem and Identifier nodes are tenant-scoped** (and network-scoped for projections). Amends DEC-130 item 5: no node that a tenant request can write is shared. Read-only code-system definitions may be shared later, never written by a request.
2. **The back end issues the tenant-bound token.** The BFF exchanges the verified sign-in for a back-end token. A shared signing secret in the BFF that can mint any tenant is not used. This replaces the W09 design.
3. **One sub-domain per tenant.** The tenant for any unauthenticated or pre-authentication request (text-code, lost phone, PIN reset, pairing, link pages) comes from the host. The invitation text sent from the tenant's own number carries the tenant's URL. Pairing codes are unique within a tenant and entered on the tenant's host.
4. **Roles.** Add `tenant-admin` and `network-admin` to `ROLES`. A `platform-operator` role exists only for tenant lifecycle (create, suspend) and has no data access.
5. **Cross-tenant support access** is the existing O06 support-code design, issued by the tenant's administrator, time-limited and audited. No role holds standing cross-tenant access.
6. **Network projections store the inputs, not a status.** The projector writes the profile subset, the latest report, the confirmed count and the reply window; the network read computes status with the same pure `compute_status` at read time. Periodic reprojection runs as well. A stored status would stay "accepting" after a service stops reporting.
7. **Criteria shared:** the current structured criteria (accepts and excludes), not the review workflow, approvals or reviewer identities.
8. **Cross-organisation work is read-only.** Network rows have no request-booking or outcome actions. An advisor phones the provider. The advisor's own case records the destination as typed snapshot properties (organisation name, service name, as-of time) copied at the time, never a link across scopes. Alerts, bookings, cases, the clinical queue, planning and reporters stay tenant-only. The network week summary shows capacity-derived measures only.
9. **Global statements** that may run before a scope is bound are an explicit allowlist, as exceptions to DEC-130 item 3: the link-token hash lookup, the lookup of a tenant by called number and `AccountSid`, the `Tenant`, `Network`, `NetworkMembership` and `Grant` registry reads, and migrations and the health ping. The outbox relay and the scheduler iterate tenants and bind each in turn, so they are not global statements.
10. **Audit chain:** each scope has its own chain; the hashed text is not changed (so the existing chain still verifies); only the genesis differs for new scopes. The existing chain becomes the default tenant's.
11. **Backfill:** no production data exists, only development data. A migration assigns existing nodes to a default tenant. The generated two-tenant test proves nothing is left unstamped.
12. **Seam in two steps:** a `TENANCY_MODE` of `warn` (log and count unscoped statements, do not refuse), then `enforce` once the P-series and G05 are migrated. Flipping to `enforce` is recorded as its own decision, and from then the skill's full rules apply.
13. **Case references** are unique per tenant among open cases (domain-rules 3.2, L29), reusable after close. The lifetime-unique constraint (migration 0022, 9,000 values) is stricter than the rule and is replaced when composite keys land.
14. **Per-tenant configuration** (name, support phone, time zone defaulting to Europe/London, sender number and subaccount reference, threshold overrides within safe bounds) lives on a `Tenant` node. "Dorset" and single-organisation wording in prompts and templates are removed. `ENTRA_TENANT_ID` is renamed `ENTRA_DIRECTORY_ID` so it cannot be confused with a platform tenant.
15. **Statement counts:** the registry holds about 124 statement ids (not 78); 13 statements are built from Python strings and each is reviewed by hand.
16. **Order.** The interim harness fixes (machine-wide lock, Neo4j memory limits, owner-based reaper, Bolt readiness probe) go first, independent of tenancy. Then the seam in warn mode, composite constraints (which run alone), repository migration, enforce, then the network layer. In-flight tasks (U02, U03, I04, W02) finish on the current model with their listed fixes.

## DEC-112 ConfirmCapacity choices the prompt leaves open (U02)
Status: accepted (decided by the builder under DEC-133; no owner gate) · Numbered DEC-112 from the block reserved for this builder (DEC-112 to DEC-114). Checked on main and every task/* branch: DEC-112 is unused elsewhere. Affects: U02 (`application/use_cases/confirm_capacity.py`), U01 (`application/use_cases/report_capacity.py`, `record_report` and `check_may_report`), P01 (`CapacityRepository`), U05, R05, L01 (callers), H-15.

1. **Audit and `record_report`.** The prompt requires `use_case="ConfirmCapacity"` and a snapshot of `value`, `derived_from` and `consecutive`, while also requiring the reuse of `record_report`, which wrote a fixed use-case name and snapshot. `record_report` gains two optional keyword arguments, `use_case` (default `ReportCapacity`) and `audit_snapshot` (default the U01 snapshot). **U01 touch, for the owner:** the same change renames `_check_may_report` to the public `check_may_report`, because ConfirmCapacity imports it. The U01 test file is unchanged, and `tests/integration/application/test_report_capacity.py` passes unchanged on this branch's tip. U01 behaviour is unchanged. The change is in U01's file, which is outside U02's file list; this item records it.
2. **`consecutive`** is the count including this confirmation (1 or 2), counted since the last number report by effective time.
3. **`derived_from`** is the id of the latest report (by effective time), so a second confirmation derives from the first, not from the number. The value is the latest report's value.
4. **Counting.** `trailing_confirmations` (query `Q-CAP-TRAILING-CONFIRMS`) orders by effectiveDateTime then lastUpdated, the same order as `latest`, and counts leading `confirmed` types until the first other type. Reports of other services are not counted.
5. **Permissions** are those of U01 (`check_may_report`, reused, so a reporter confirms only own services and an advisor or lead only as advisor-phoned). An unknown or inactive service is `NotFound`. The permission check runs before the no-report and limit checks, so a refused actor learns nothing about the figure.
6. **Replay and the shared key space.** The rule of DEC-104 and DEC-106 applies: the key is decided first, outside and again inside the unit of work. The same command by the same actor returns the stored result with `replayed=True`. A different command, or a key stored by another use case, is `Conflict`, and nothing is written. A key that `ReportCapacity` stored and that is reused for a confirmation is refused with `Conflict` (test `test_a_key_stored_by_a_report_is_a_conflict_for_a_confirmation_and_writes_nothing`; mutant M7). A key that `ConfirmCapacity` stored and that is reused for a report is also refused with `Conflict` and writes nothing (test `test_a_key_stored_by_a_confirmation_is_a_conflict_for_a_report_and_writes_nothing`; mutant M-C). The mechanism differs by direction. `ReportCapacity`'s early and in-unit replay check replays only a `ReportCapacity` result, so a confirmation's stored result falls through it; the refusal comes from `Neo4jIdempotencyStore.put` (`adapters/outbound/graph_store/idempotency_store.py`, lines 208 to 217), which `ReportCapacity` reaches through `uow.idempotency` inside its unit of work. It raises `Conflict` when a different result is already stored for the key. That `Conflict` rolls back the unit of work, including the report written before the put, so no second report is committed. This corrects the earlier wording of this item, which said the reverse direction 'falls through and writes a second report'. That was wrong: it was measured on the rebased tip and the store refuses it. Round 3 (verifier finding F2) corrects the name of the method: the earlier text named `DriverIdempotencyStore.put`, the out-of-unit wrapper in the same file. It has no caller in `src` and is not on this path. The store's refusal is the only guard for this direction, so removing it (mutant M-C, applied to `Neo4jIdempotencyStore.put`) is caught by the test above.
7. **Echo.** C3 with the confirmed value goes only for a text source with a performer role, as U01. The next-prompt time follows DEC-101.
8. **Concurrency: the service lock comes first.** The unit of work first takes the service's write lock (`SET s.id = s.id` on the HealthcareService, query `Q-CAP-LOCK-SERVICE`, the P04 pattern, lessons L73 and L99), then checks the replay key, then reads the latest report and the count, then writes. Two confirmations for one service therefore run one after the other, and the second reads the first's committed report. Before this item, the count was read without a lock, so two simultaneous Y replies with different keys could both read a count of 1 and both be written, giving three in a row; that is the race the verifier found, now closed. Tests: `test_a_second_confirmation_waits_for_a_held_confirmation_then_is_refused` forces the interleaving (the first commit is held with its lock, a second task must still be waiting after one second, then is refused; exactly two confirmed reports), and `test_sixteen_confirmations_at_once_let_exactly_two_through` is a supplementary 16-caller race (lessons L94 and L102: a timing race alone guards nothing reliably). Mutant M-LOCK (lock removed) fails the held test.
9. **Suspended and inactive services.** domain-rules 1.3 step 1: an active suspended flag makes the status Not updated. U02 does not refuse a confirmation for a suspended service, as U01 does not refuse a report; the confirmation is stored and the result status is Not updated (test `test_a_suspended_service_confirms_and_is_not_updated`). An inactive service is `NotFound` (test `test_an_inactive_service_is_not_found`). Decided under DEC-133: a suspended service keeps accepting confirmations, because the figure is still the reporter's latest word and the suspension already shows as Not updated; refusing would lose a true report and give the reporter a reason to phone. This stays the same as U01.
10. **Lock port.** `CapacityRepository.lock_service(service_id)` writes nothing and returns when the lock is held for the rest of the unit of work. An unknown service is left to the `services.get` check, which raises `NotFound`. The lock is taken before the replay check, so two identical keys cannot both write. From round 3, `ReportCapacity` takes the same lock first, before its replay check, its read of the latest report and its default effective time (DEC-139).
11. **Tenancy (DEC-130 to DEC-134, transitional rule).** The statements this task adds, `Q-CAP-LOCK-SERVICE` and `Q-CAP-TRAILING-CONFIRMS` (round 2), and `Q-CAP-LATEST-CONFIRMED-AT` (round 3, DEC-140), match the service by its `key` property, which names the service by its own FHIR key and carries no tenant yet. All three are listed as tenant-pending in `docs/delivery/evidence/U02.md`. No tenant code is added here; the tenancy migration adds `tenantId: $tenant` to all three. The lock needs no other change, because it locks the one tenant-scoped service node.
12. **Known limit inherited from U01: the service-existence probe (round 3, F3).** The profile lookup raises `NotFound` before `check_may_report` (`confirm_capacity.py`, and the same order in `report_capacity.py`). A reporter therefore gets `NotFound` for an unknown or inactive service id and `NotAllowed` for an active service that is not theirs, so the id can be probed within a tenant. Decision (round 3): keep the order. ConfirmCapacity shares `check_may_report` and the lookup with U01, so changing the order here alone would make the two use cases disagree, and U01's order is its tested behaviour. Within one tenant, a reporter learns which active service ids exist in their own tenant, and nothing else about them. Cross-tenant probing is the real concern, and it is closed by the tenancy work: TEN-010 makes the service and capacity repositories tenant-scoped, so another tenant's id reads as absent (`NotFound`), and TEN-027 migrates U02 and its order of checks. Until then this limit stands and is listed as "Not done" in the evidence.

## DEC-135 Test harness load protection: one container at a time, memory limits, owner-based reaper, Bolt readiness, per-test limit (F10b)
Status: accepted · Affects: `backend/tests/support/neo4j.py`, `harness_lock.py`, `proc.py`, `watchdog.py`, `backend/tests/conftest.py`, every pytest run that uses the Neo4j harness. Amends DEC-013 (readiness timing) and DEC-108 (reaper rule). DEC-008 (one throwaway Neo4j per pytest session) still holds. Carries out the interim part of DEC-134 item 16 and DEC-130 item 9. No tenancy design is touched.

Context (measured): four agents running pytest at once each started a Neo4j. Load reached 15 on 4 cores, `docker exec cypher-shell` readiness probes timed out and showed as false test failures, containers and volumes leaked and the disk filled. One serial run on a quiet machine passes.

1. **Cross-process lock.** `start_throwaway_neo4j` takes an exclusive `flock` on `/tmp/diucs-neo4j-harness.lock` (path from `DIUCS_NEO4J_LOCK`) before it asks Docker for anything, and holds it until `stop_throwaway_neo4j`. A second session polls every 0.5 s for up to `DIUCS_NEO4J_LOCK_TIMEOUT` seconds (default 3600), then fails in the `lock` phase with a message naming the holder's pid (the holder writes its pid into the file). The operating system frees a flock when its process dies, so a killed run never blocks the next one (proved with two real processes, one SIGKILLed). A process can own several containers (the harness's own tests start extra ones beside the session container): holds are kept per container name, the lock is taken with the first and freed with the last, and a release by a name that never acquired changes nothing. The manual `flock /tmp/diucs-neo4j-tests.lock <command>` that agents wrap around pytest is a different file on purpose: it queues whole runs, this one guards the container. Taking the manual lock first and this one second can never deadlock, and nothing takes them in the other order. Both are documented in the `neo4j.py` docstring. The lock file is opened with `O_NOFOLLOW` (a symlink at that path is refused in the `lock` phase and its target is not touched) and is made 0666 on purpose whatever the umask, because agents may run as different users and all of them must be able to take it; the price is that any local user can also hold it, which is acceptable for a development-machine tool. The port for the container is picked after the lock is held, so a long wait cannot leave it stale. No `fcntl` (Windows): no lock, and a log line (see item 6).
2. **Memory limits.** The container is started with `--memory 1g` and Neo4j `server.memory.heap.initial_size` = `max_size` = 256m and `pagecache.size` = 128m (environment `NEO4J_server_memory_heap_initial__size` and the others). Overridable with `DIUCS_NEO4J_MEMORY`, `DIUCS_NEO4J_HEAP` and `DIUCS_NEO4J_PAGECACHE`; a value that is not a size such as `512m` or `2g` is refused in the `config` phase before Docker is called. Measured with `docker stats` every 10 s: idle after ready 586 to 603 MiB; peak 746 MiB over the whole `tests/integration` run (535 tests, nine containers counting the harness's own tests); the unlimited container before this change read 493 MiB idle, and with no limit Neo4j sizes its heap and page cache from the host's memory (16 GB here). A first trial limit of 768m was rejected: idle use was already 586 MiB and a start-up spike of 709 MiB was seen, which leaves no margin before the kernel kills the JVM. The 1 GiB limit is 37% above the 746 MiB docker-stats peak (27% of the limit was unused at that peak). The verifier read the cgroup counters of every container in a full run: peak RSS 663 MiB, peak 915 MiB including reclaimable page cache, `failcnt` 0 and no OOM kill. The margin is not large for a heavier future test; `DIUCS_NEO4J_MEMORY` raises it. A real-Docker test reads the settings back from the server (`dbms.listConfig`), because a misspelt `NEO4J_` variable is silently not applied.
3. **Owner-based reaper (amends DEC-108).** Each container carries the owner labels `diucs-test-owner-pid`, `diucs-test-owner-start` (field 22 of `/proc/<pid>/stat`, the process start time), `diucs-test-owner-host` (`socket.gethostname()`), `diucs-test-owner-pidns` (`readlink /proc/self/ns/pid`, for example `pid:[4026531836]`) and `diucs-test-owner-boot` (`/proc/sys/kernel/random/boot_id`). The sweep at session start, judging each container by its own owner labels, works in three steps.
   - **Comparable owner.** The owner's host, pid namespace and boot must equal this process's, and all three must be non-empty. Pids and start times mean something only inside one pid table on one boot of one machine, so only then is a pid looked up here.
   - **Gone.** Only when the pid is comparable and this process can see that no such process exists: the stat file is there and the start time differs (pid reuse), or the process is a zombie, or the stat file is absent and `kill(pid, 0)` says there is no such process (`ProcessLookupError`). The container is removed (`docker rm -f -v`, DEC-128) at any age.
   - **Unknown.** Anything else keeps the container until `OTHER_HOST_MAX_AGE` (6 hours). That covers an owner in another pid namespace or boot or on another host name, an owner label that is only partly written (an empty host, namespace or boot), and an owner this process cannot see: an unreadable stat file (permission denied, no descriptors left, any other error), a garbled stat line, or a stat file that is absent while `kill(pid, 0)` succeeds or is refused with `EPERM`. Those are the `hidepid=2` case, where another user's live process gives `ENOENT`, not `EACCES`. `process_start_marker` raises `ProcessUnreadable` for every one of them; it returns None only for a zombie or a pid that the kernel says does not exist.
   - **Legacy.** A container with no owner label at all (made before F10b) keeps the 90-minute age rule (`REAPER_MAX_AGE`, DEC-108). So does a container whose pid label is not made of ASCII digits, or whose start label is empty or not made of ASCII digits. "Number" means ASCII digits only: `str.isdigit()` also accepts "²" and "٥", which the labels never contain. A garbled start label means the owner cannot be compared with the live process, so it is unknown, never gone (B5, round 4; a live owner with a garbled label was removed at 5 minutes before the fix; round 5 closed the non-ASCII-digit gap for the owner labels; round 6 closed it for the start field of the stat line too, in `proc.parse_start_marker`).
   The scope rules of DEC-108 item 2 are unchanged and are checked twice: the name must start with `diucs-test-neo4j-` and the container must carry `diucs-test=true`, so `diucs-local-*` and every other container is out of reach. Checked on the real kernel in round 3: a live owner in `unshare --pid` with the same host name was kept at 5 minutes; a sweeper running as uid 65534 under `hidepid=2` could not see its owner's `/proc` entry, got `ProcessUnreadable` from `kill(pid, 0)` returning EPERM, and would keep it.
4. **Bolt readiness (amends DEC-013 timing).** Readiness is `GraphDatabase.driver(...).verify_connectivity()` followed by `RETURN 1` with the Python driver in the test process, each attempt bounded to about 5 s (a server that accepts a connection and stays silent was measured: 5.0 s), retried with a backoff from 0.25 s to 2 s. The total limit is 120 s (`DIUCS_NEO4J_READY_TIMEOUT`) and its clock starts after the image is present: `docker image inspect` first, `docker pull` (900 s limit) only if it is missing, then `docker run` (120 s limit, no pull inside it). A daemon that cannot be reached (`image inspect` failing with a connect or permission error) is the `docker` phase, not a missing image. Every failure raises `HarnessStartError`, a `Neo4jNotRunning`, whose message and `.phase` name where it failed: `config`, `lock`, `docker` (Docker unavailable), `image`, `start` (docker run refused or timed out, with the first line of Docker's reason) or `ready`. A ready timeout removes the container (`rm -f -v`); so does a refused or timed-out `docker run`, which can leave a created container and its anonymous volumes. The lock is released on every failure path. `wait_until_ready` keeps its name and gains the same probe, for the restart tests.
5. **Per-test time limits, no new dependency.** The root conftest wraps each test's fixture set-up (default 900 s, `DIUCS_TEST_SETUP_TIMEOUT`) and call phase (default 300 s, `DIUCS_TEST_TIMEOUT`) in `time_limit`; 0 turns either off. A SIGALRM raises `TimeLimitExceeded` (a `BaseException`, so a library's `except Exception` cannot swallow it) naming the test and the stack where it was; the test fails and the next one runs (shown for a sync hang, an async hang and a hung set-up). The harness pauses the set-up clock (`paused()`) for its lock wait and image pull, which have their own bounds, and gives back what was left. If the soft stage cannot stop a test, a timer thread acts `DIUCS_TEST_HARD_GRACE` seconds later (default 60): it releases pytest's output capture, writes the test's name and every thread's stack to the real stderr, and ends the process with exit code 3 (shown end to end under pytest's default capture). The lock is then freed by the OS and the next reaper removes the container. Slowest call measured in round 3: 80.2 s in the full serial run (`test_connection_recovery.py::test_in_flight_write_fails_retryably_and_the_service_recovers`), 3.7 times below the 300 s default; earlier measurements were 62 s on a quiet machine and 77.2 s at load about 6. Slowest set-up measured in round 3: 22.7 s (the support run, `test_neo4j_harness.py::test_write_tx_creates_a_node_that_count_nodes_sees`) and 22.6 s (the full run, `test_api_support.py::test_the_test_container_clock_is_the_r01_test_instant`), about 40 times below the 900 s set-up limit. The round-2 figures of 26.4 s and 22.3 s and the verifier's 22.9 s and 26.7 s were from other runs and are not the basis of these numbers. Teardown is not limited. After a soft timeout of an async test, its coroutine may stay pending on the session loop; the hard stage is the backstop.
6. **Not covered.** The lock stops only harness starts: a container started by hand, `docker compose` use, and the front end's fixed e2e port are outside it. The port is still picked by binding port 0 and releasing it, so a non-harness process can still take it between the pick and `docker run` (the harness's own starts can no longer race). Linux only: the lock needs `fcntl`, the limits `SIGALRM`, the owner labels `/proc`; elsewhere the lock and owner labels are skipped and the new unit tests, which import `fcntl` and use `SIGALRM` and `SIGKILL`, are not collected (CI is Linux; not run on Windows or macOS). No `atexit` or SIGTERM handler removes the container of a plain `kill`; the next session's reaper does, now at once instead of after 90 minutes. Container reuse across sessions (TEN-E05) is not done. A full serial run still takes about the time it did; this makes it safe to queue, not faster.

7. **Known limits, round 3.** (a) A harness process whose own `/proc` cannot be read writes no owner labels at all, so its containers keep the 90-minute legacy rule and a run longer than 90 minutes could lose its container in that environment. This is not reachable on the machines here (Linux, `/proc` readable for the owner). (b) Teardown has no time limit. That includes a thread that a soft timeout left running in `run_in_executor`: the session loop's shutdown waits for it, the verifier measured 43 s, and a thread that never returns would stop the process from exiting. The hard stage covers only the call phase, and it is not started during teardown. (c) On a daemon shared by several users, the sweep removes another user's `diucs-test-neo4j-*` container with the `diucs-test` label when that container's owner is visibly gone; the owner's own labels decide, and the name and label checks are what limit the scope.

Tests: `tests/unit/support/test_harness_lock.py`, `test_process_start_marker.py`, `test_start_container.py`, `test_reaper_owner.py`, `test_time_limit.py` (fake runner, clock, probe and process table; real-process lock and hard-stage tests; pytester runs of the conftest) and `tests/integration/support/test_harness_protection.py` (two real-Docker checks). Red commit `741ed94`, green commit `5b89c25`, limit change `f15b99c`; round 1 of verification failed three findings, fixed with red `0d64020` and green `a0eb06a`, with a pinning commit `9229129`; round 2 failed one blocking finding (B4, owners that are not visible) and the non-blocking items, fixed with red `989cc1c`, green `679c746` and a pinning commit `d44bcd7` (see items 3 and 7). The previous reaper and removal tests are unchanged and pass.

## DEC-136 Tenancy lifecycle, roles and cross-organisation workflows: answers to audit F
Status: accepted · Affects: the tenancy plan, the S5 to S9 prompts, DEC-130 item 6, DEC-134 item 8. Decided under DEC-133 from `docs/delivery/tenancy-audit/F-lifecycle-flows.md`. Where this decision and DEC-134 overlap, this one is the later and more specific statement.

1. **No cross-organisation booking now.** A booking request carries age band, need and urgency, which is patient-level data, and DEC-132 item 7 keeps patient-level data inside the tenant until a future care-coordination phase. Network rows are read-only; the advisor phones the provider and records the call in their own tenant's case (DEC-134 item 8). The proposed `TEN-N16` bookings task is dropped. When care coordination is built it gets its own grant scope, purpose and decision. Bookings, requests and the S5 booking flow work inside one tenant.
2. **Isolation is application-enforced in one shared Neo4j Community database** for the pilot. The database driver choice stays behind the repository so a tenant can move to its own database later. Because Community cannot enforce that every node has a `tenantId`, a startup and daily invariant check counts nodes without one and fails loudly (a node without a tenant would be invisible, so a service could silently vanish from the board).
3. **A provider registers in its own tenant; the network owner admits the listing.** The listing becomes visible to the network only when a grant exists and the network owner has admitted it. The network owner may suspend a listing; a suspended listing stops projecting and shows as unavailable.
4. **Criteria:** two approvals inside the provider, as the existing registration and criteria workflow requires. There is no per-version network endorsement: the grant approved by the network owner covers the scope.
5. **No cross-tenant writes of any kind.** Advisor-phoned positions are not written into the provider's or the network scope. The provider's own lead changes the provider's reporter numbers and cover.
6. **Platform and support roles** exist only in a separate operator directory, never mapped from a customer's own directory. Support access is one tenant at a time, for one hour, by a support code issued by the tenant's administrator (DEC-134 item 5), audited, with no break-glass in the application.
7. **One session per tenant, a user may hold one in each tenant they work for.** This amends DEC-130 item 6. A person in two tenants has one Practitioner per tenant, linked only by an opaque `accountRef`; sessions, tokens, PINs, devices and push subscriptions are never shared between tenants.
8. **Registration needs two distinct approvers** (resolves the open point in DEC-032 and DEC-062; `domain-rules` 6.1).
9. **Alerts and clinical work stay tenant-only.** The network owner's tenant hosts the SPOA's advisors, clinical advisors, leads, cases and bookings. A clinical advisor from another organisation holds an account in the owner's tenant. Alerts are not driven by network events for now.
10. **Lifecycle parameters:** only the platform operator creates a tenant; offboarding takes 30 days; a released phone number is quarantined for 90 days before reassignment; purging a tenant needs two operators; a tenant cannot be activated until its retention is set (the lead developer sets the default and records it; no external gate).
11. **Hosts:** one sub-domain per tenant for every web entry (DEC-134 item 3), including link pages and the installed app, so each tenant is its own browser origin and its own service worker and storage scope. A link whose host and token disagree is a 404.
12. **Per-tenant derived hash keys** for phone hashes and tokens, with the legacy key used only by the default tenant.
13. **Time and calendars:** `timeZone` is stored per tenant and per network (allow-list `Europe/London` for now). Bank holidays are not modelled anywhere in the specs or prompts and are added as a task before the pilot.
14. **Telemetry is per tenant.** Cross-tenant analytics are anonymised aggregates across at least three tenants, only under the data sharing agreement. The network owner sees no per-member on-time breakdown.
15. **Scale assumption:** 10 to 30 organisations in year one. Who pays for numbers and sender registration is outside the build.
16. **Hazards:** the 29 `H-T` and 15 `H-N` rows proposed in audit F are added to the hazard log as the tenancy tasks land, each with its control and verifying test.
## DEC-115 SendPrompts and the scheduling helpers: link-token wiring, skips, C2 window, no-reporter case (U03)
Status: accepted (builder build-U03, for the owner to confirm) · Affects: U03 (`application/use_cases/send_prompts.py`, `application/scheduling.py`), U04, U05, U06, U15 (callers), I03 (relay), I10 (consumer). Numbers: this task uses DEC-115 and DEC-116 only. Both were checked on 2026-10-28 and appear on no other ref: `git show <ref>:docs/delivery/decisions.md | grep '^## DEC-'` for `main`, `origin/main`, `task/F10b-harness-load-protection` (DEC-135), `task/TEN-001-seam-warn` (DEC-137), `task/U02-confirm-capacity` (DEC-112) and `task/skill-sonnet-consult`. (Corrected in round 3: the earlier text said DEC-111 was the highest number, which was false. `main` already had DEC-133, DEC-134 and DEC-136, and has DEC-138 now. DEC-115 and DEC-116 are gaps in `main`'s numbering, not the next free numbers, and the choice of them is recorded in DEC-116.)

1. **The link-token service is a factory, not an instance.** The prompt gives `SendPrompts(..., link_tokens: LinkTokenService, ...)`. `LinkTokenService` holds a `LinkTokenRepository` bound to one Neo4j transaction (P10), so one instance cannot serve a use case that opens a new unit of work per command, and issuing the token outside the unit of work would leave a live link when the prompt fails to commit. `SendPrompts` takes `link_tokens: LinkTokenServiceFactory` (`Callable[[LinkTokenRepository], LinkTokenService]`) and calls it with `uow.link_tokens`, so the token, the communication, the outbox message and the ladder commit or roll back together. Callers in the composition root pass `lambda repo: LinkTokenService(repo, secrets, clock)`. Test: `test_a_failure_part_way_leaves_nothing_behind`.
2. **The token is tied to the communication and the phone.** Purpose `capacity-reply`, `about_id` the communication id (this prompt for this role), `phone_hash` the hash of whoever holds the role now (cover honoured with `role_holder`), expiry reply-by (due + 30 minutes). The link is `<link_base_url without a trailing slash>/<token>`.
3. **Skips write nothing.** *Superseded by item 12 (every skip re-arms the chain, is audited, and its idempotency record is stored).*  An inactive or unknown-inactive service, an active `suspended` flag, a due time outside the hours (`is_in_hours` at the due instant), or a prompt whose reply window has already closed (now at or after due + 30 minutes) returns `sent=False` with a reason and writes no row, token, message or audit entry, and stores no idempotency record. The closed-window skip is an addition to the prompt: sending a link that is already expired would be a dead link in a patient-facing path. An unknown service id raises `NotFound`.
4. **C2 splits the unit label.** *Superseded in part by item 13 (no "today" fallback; an unreadable window is refused).*  `unit_label` is the whole phrase ("referrals in the next 2 hrs", domain-rules 1.1), which C1 uses as it is. C2 takes `unit` and `window` apart (L46): the unit is the words before the first " in ", the window the rest. A label with no " in " gives the whole label as the unit and the window "today". The "today" wording is an assumption for the owner.
5. **A reporter who cannot be texted is left out.** *Superseded by item 15 (a service-level ladder is created, so "no ladder" no longer holds) and by item 24 (an audit entry is written and names the reason, so "no audit entry" no longer holds).*  A role holder who is missing, inactive or has no phone hash gets no message and no ladder. If no role can be texted, the result is `sent=False` with the reason "no reporter can be texted for this service", and the next `prompt-due` is still scheduled so the cycle continues; no audit entry is written. The missed-report ladder is about a communication id, so a service with nobody to text gets no reminder, phone task or escalation from this use case. That is an open risk for the owner and for U06 and U15 (H-13): a service whose reporters have no phone is silent. A check at registration or on the lead's board is the proper control.
6. **Idempotency.** *Superseded by item 14 (the fingerprint follows DEC-104, with the actor) and by item 12 (a skip is stored too).*  The key is the command's (`prompt:<service>:<due iso>`). The stored value is the result plus a fingerprint of the service and the due instant, and a replay with another body is `Conflict` (as DEC-104). The replay check runs inside the unit of work before any write (as DEC-106). Only a send (including the no-reporter result) is stored, since a skip writes nothing.
7. **Scheduling helpers.** *Reviewed in round 3 and not changed by items 10 to 21. `application/scheduling.py` has had no commit since round 1 (`git log --format='%h %s' -- backend/src/diucs/application/scheduling.py` lists `e159490` and `4956056` only). Item 19 adds a test of its UTC conversion with London-zoned inputs, which is a test and not a change to this item. The orchestrator's list of superseded items named item 7, and the review found nothing in it that a later item replaces.*  `schedule_work` converts the due time and `now` to UTC, writes the `ScheduledWork` row (status `scheduled`, no sequence number) and a `ScheduleWork` outbox message on `scheduled.work` with the payload `work_id, kind, about_id, enqueue_at` (`enqueue_at` is the canonical UTC text, `2026-10-23T11:00:00Z`). A naive time raises `ValidationFailed`. `cancel_work` reads the stored row and acts on that, not on the copy it is given: work that is not `scheduled` is left alone (no message), a sequence number the relay stored is kept, and the `CancelWork` message id is `cancel-<work id>`, so one job is cancelled once. `about_id` is stored as given (a communication id or a service id, not a `Type/id` key).
8. **Audit.** One entry per sent command, `use_case="SendPrompts"`, action `C`, entities the communication ids then the service id, snapshot the due instant and the number of prompts. The command's actor is recorded and not authorised: the scheduled-work consumer (I05) passes a system actor.
9. **Delivery detail.** *Superseded by item 10 and DEC-116 (the payload carries `sealed_content` in place of `text`, `subject` and `html`; the content is sealed with AES-256-GCM).*  The `SendMessage` payload carries `communication_id, message_id, channel, recipient_role_id, text` and, for email, `subject` and `html`. It carries the role id, never a phone number or a hash (the link token text is inside `text`, as the text channel must send it).

Tests: `backend/tests/integration/application/test_send_prompts.py`. Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/U03.md`.

### DEC-115, round 2 (review of verify-U03): items 10 to 21; round 3 items 22 to 29 follow

These items revise DEC-115 items 3, 4, 5, 6 and 9 where they conflict, and each of those items is marked superseded in place above. Item 7 was reviewed and stands (see its note). The earlier text stays for the history. Where the two differ, this round wins.

10. **The link token is never stored (finding A).** The `LinkToken` node holds only the token's keyed hash (unchanged, P10). The `Communication` record holds the rendered text with `[link]` in place of the link. The `SendMessage` outbox payload holds `sealed_content`: the JSON of `{text, subject, html}` sealed with the link-token secret. *Superseded by DEC-116: the seal below was a standard-library construction and is replaced by AES-256-GCM with associated data. What stays of this item is that the outbox holds the sealed content and never the token text.* The seal was HMAC-SHA256 counter-mode keystream with a random 16-byte nonce and an encrypt-then-MAC tag (both keys derived from the secret). The sender (I10) opens it with `unseal_content` at send time. **Owner decision, open at the time (closed by DEC-116, which the builder took under DEC-133):** this is standard library only. No dependency was added, because `cryptography` is not in `backend/pyproject.toml` or `uv.lock`. A reviewed AEAD (AES-GCM through `cryptography`) is preferable and needs a dependency decision. The seal is not reviewed cryptography. **Consequence for I10:** a `SendMessage` from U03 carries `sealed_content`, not `text`. U01 echoes still carry plain `text`. I10 must handle both. The tests `test_no_stored_node_and_no_outbox_payload_holds_the_token_text` and the seal tests cover this. Mutants M8 (plain token in the communication) and M9 (plain content in the outbox) fail it.
11. **C1 shows the full date and time of the figure (owner decision K, finding K).** The figure reads `(as of 23 Oct 08:15)` and a figure from another day reads `(as of 22 Oct 17:00)`. This replaces the prompt's test 1 text `(as of 08:15)`. The owner's decision overrides the prompt, as DEC rules allow. The reporter is asked to confirm with `Confirm or update:` (D15 wording, not changed here). **Open point for the owner:** a figure older than the current window is still sent as C1 with its date, and the template opens with "We have". It is not hidden and not presented undated. If the owner wants a stale figure asked for afresh (C2) rather than confirmed, that is a D15 wording change and a rule in this use case. Test `test_a_figure_from_a_previous_day_shows_its_date`. Mutant M-date (the date is dropped) fails it.
12. **Every skip re-arms the chain (finding B).** For inactive, suspended, out of hours, closed reply window, and no readable window, the use case still schedules the next `prompt-due` at `next_due(profile, due + 1 second)`. It writes an audit entry whose snapshot names the reason, and stores the idempotency record, so a replay does not arm a second prompt. The skip sends nothing and writes no communication or token. This replaces the item 3 statement that a skip writes nothing. Tests: `test_a_skipped_prompt_sends_nothing_and_re_arms_the_next_prompt` (four cases with the expected next instant), `test_a_replay_of_a_skip_does_not_arm_a_second_prompt`. Mutant M10 (the re-arm is removed from the skip path) fails them.
13. **C2 needs a readable window, and nothing is invented (finding G).** `split_unit_label` takes the unit and the window apart at the first " in ". It returns None when either part is empty. A first prompt with no readable window is not sent. The reason is `SKIP_NO_WINDOW`, the chain is re-armed, and the audit entry records it. The `today` fallback of item 4 is removed. C1 needs no window (`test_c1_does_not_need_a_window_in_the_unit_label`). Tests: `test_a_first_prompt_with_no_readable_window_is_not_worded_and_is_recorded` (two labels). Mutant M12 (the refusal is removed) fails it.
14. **Fingerprint follows DEC-104 (finding H).** The fingerprint is the service, the due instant in UTC, and the actor's role and practitioner id. This supersedes item 6, which had the body only. A reused key with another due time, or from another actor, is `Conflict`. The early read outside the unit of work, and the check inside it, both go through `_replay` (finding F: the `idempotency` constructor argument is now used for the early read). Tests: `test_a_reused_key_with_another_body_is_a_conflict` (finding C), `test_a_reused_key_from_another_actor_is_a_conflict`. Mutant M7 (the compare is removed) fails both.
15. **No textable reporter starts a ladder for the service (finding I).** Reminder +30, phone task +60 and escalate-to-lead +90 are scheduled about the service id. The prompt-due is re-armed as usual, and the result is `sent=False`, `skipped_reason=SKIP_NO_REPORTER`. This supersedes item 5, which said no ladder. The lead still gets the step at +90. Tests: `test_no_textable_reporter_starts_a_ladder_for_the_service`, `test_an_inactive_holder_is_treated_as_no_textable_reporter`. Mutant M13 (the service ladder is removed) fails the first.
16. **How U06 finds a ladder (finding J).** A reporter prompt's ladder is about its communication id. U06 finds it with `for_about(communication_id, kind)`. The communication id is in the `SendMessage` payload. A service-level ladder (no textable reporter) is about the service id. U06 finds it with `for_about(service_id, kind)`, and never without the kind, and it picks the prompt by `due_at`: the prompt's due plus the step's offset (30, 60 or 90 minutes). Two prompts of one service never share a due instant, so the match is unique. Tests: `test_a_reporter_prompt_ladder_belongs_to_its_communication_not_the_service`, `test_a_service_ladder_is_found_by_service_kind_and_due_time`. No repository query is added, because the repository files belong to P07 and P08.
17. **Atomicity is tested after the writes (finding D).** The audit append is the last write of a command. The test wraps the unit of work so that the append raises, after the communication, the token, the outbox messages, the ladder, the next prompt and the idempotency record have been written. It asserts that every counted label, and the idempotency record, is unchanged. Test: `test_a_failure_after_the_writes_leaves_nothing_behind`.
18. **The constructor takes the secret store.** *Superseded by DEC-116 item 7: the use case takes a `ContentSealer`.* `SendPrompts` has a new last parameter, `secret_store: SecretStore`, used only to seal the content with the link-token secret (`LINK_TOKEN_SECRET`). The `link_tokens` factory of item 1 is unchanged.
19. **London conversion (finding E).** The fold=1 test is replaced. The new cases use London-zoned inputs where reading the wall time as UTC gives a different instant: 01:30 BST on 2026-10-25 (`00:30Z`) and 10:00 BST on 2026-10-23 (`09:00Z`). The old comment, which said `02:30 UTC`, was wrong and is gone. Mutant M11 (`schedule_work` reads the London wall time as UTC) fails the new test. This is an after-the-fact red: the correct code already passed the old test, so the red is shown by the mutant (lesson L138).
20. **Scope of the round.** Round 2 adds the tests in a red commit before the implementation, with the stubs named in the DEC-010 way. Items 10, 11, 12, 13 and 15 need a new red. Items 14, 17 and 19 are after-the-fact reds shown by mutants.
21. **Tenancy notes and dead letters (DEC-130 to DEC-136, transitional rule).** No new Cypher is added. The seal key is read in one function (`SendPrompts._link_secret`), so a per-tenant secret is a one-place change. The topic names are the constants `MESSAGES_TOPIC` and `SCHEDULED_WORK_TOPIC`. A dead-lettered outbox message keeps its sealed payload and an error text, and neither holds a token (test `test_a_dead_lettered_message_holds_no_token_text`). The use case words nothing that names an organisation: C1 and C2 come from the D15 templates, and no test or code here says "Dorset" (DEC-134 item 14). A token is also issued when the text template shows no link (C2 on the text channel). It is tied to the phone and the communication and expires at reply-by, and no one holds it, so it cannot be used. The tenant-pending statements are listed in the evidence file.

### DEC-115, round 3 (verify-U03-r2 findings): items 22 to 29

22. **The dated C1 wording is carried through (finding 2).** `docs/plan/prompts/U03.md` lines 57 and 69, `docs/spec/ui-screens.md` lines 929 and 963, and `docs/spec/domain-rules.md` line 281 now read `(as of 23 Oct 08:15)`. This is the same change, as DEC-133 item 4 requires. Prompt test 1 is "text contains `(as of 23 Oct 08:15)`", not `(as of 08:15)`.
23. **Reply-path hazard against U05 (finding 3).** A Y to a C1 whose figure is older than its window would be recorded as the current figure, because the D11 reply policy (`decide_capacity_reply`, through its private `_decide_confirm`) never looks at the figure's date: `_decide_confirm` reads only whether the prompt is open, the recorded value and the count of consecutive confirms. The hazard is row **H-31** of `docs/delivery/hazard-log.md` (numbered H-31 because H-29 and H-30 are taken on `main`), against U05. Its rule: a Y on a figure older than its window is not recorded as current. The fix is in U05 (`HandleInboundText` builds the `PromptState`) and the test is written with U05; `docs/plan/prompts/U05.md` now says so. U03 enforces what it can: it never sends a figure undated (item 11), so the reporter reads the date of the figure that a Y would confirm. U03 cannot refuse a Y, because it has no reply path. **Decision under DEC-133:** a stale figure stays a C1 with its date (item 11), and is not turned into a C2, because that would reverse owner decision K and D11 must hold the rule whichever message was sent.
24. **The audit names the real reason (finding 4).** A send with no textable reporter now writes `skipped_reason = SKIP_NO_REPORTER` in the audit snapshot, as the result does. Before the fix the snapshot said `none`. Test `test_a_no_reporter_prompt_audits_the_real_reason` was committed red (f06880f) and is green now.
25. **Sealed content is bound to its tenant (DEC-130 item 3, finding 1).** Opened as another tenant, sealed content is refused. Test `test_sealed_content_opens_only_in_its_own_tenant`. *Mechanism superseded by DEC-116 item 3: the tenant is part of the AES-GCM associated data.* The tenant is the `SendPrompts` constructor argument `tenant_id`, supplied by the composition root. It is the only tenant code added, it is tenant-pending, and no Cypher is added.
26. **Dead letters hold no token (finding 1).** A dead-lettered `SendMessage` keeps its sealed content and an error text, and neither contains the token. Test `test_a_dead_lettered_message_holds_no_token_text`.
27. **Two red-run corrections, disclosed.** (a) Test `test_a_role_whose_holder_is_not_stored_is_left_out`, committed red in 65e3e50 (round 3b), was removed. The person repository refuses to save a role whose practitioner is unknown (`NotFound` on `save_role`, P03 invariant), so the state cannot exist in storage. The `holder is None` check in `_phone_hash` stays as a defensive guard. (b) Two expectations in `test_the_unit_label_is_split_into_unit_and_window_or_refused` were corrected in the green commit. An unreadable label is returned whole (`"referrals in"` and `"referrals in "`), because nothing is worded from an unreadable label, so the unit is not used.
28. **No "Dorset" and no single-organisation wording (DEC-134 item 14).** `grep -i dorset` finds nothing in the U03 files. The C2 template is D15's and is not changed here.
29. **A profile with no next due instant is refused, not dropped (finding 6 coverage).** `_rearm` raises `DomainError` when `next_due` returns None. Before, it skipped the re-arm silently. A valid profile always has a next due instant, so the branch is unreachable for valid profiles. Test `test_a_profile_with_no_next_due_instant_is_refused_and_writes_nothing` (monkeypatches `next_due`); it was committed red in 5ef1f73. Mutant U1 (the guard removed) is killed. Also in round 3: the in-unit-of-work replay check (the `isinstance(stored, _StoredPrompt)` test inside the unit of work in `execute`) had no test that reached it, because the early read catches ordinary replays. Test `test_a_replay_seen_only_inside_the_unit_of_work_is_still_a_replay` was added after the fact: the code passed it when it was written. Mutant R4 (that check removed) is now killed. Before this, the note that "in-unit-of-work replay removed survives by design" was wrong for that check.

## DEC-116 Sealing the prompt content with AES-256-GCM from `cryptography`; key, associated data and strict decoding (U03, under DEC-133)

Status: accepted (builder build-U03; the dependency choice is the builder's under DEC-133, no human gate) · Affects: U03 (`application/use_cases/send_prompts.py`), the port `application/ports/outbound/sealing.py`, the adapter `adapters/outbound/content_seal/aes_gcm.py`, `backend/pyproject.toml`, `backend/uv.lock`, I10 (the consumer that opens the sealed content), and any later task that seals message content. Supersedes the seal described in DEC-115 item 10, which was a standard-library construction without a reviewed cipher. Numbering: DEC-116 is not used on `main` (its highest is DEC-138), on `origin/main`, on `task/F10b-harness-load-protection` (DEC-135), on `task/TEN-001-seam-warn` (DEC-137), on `task/U02-confirm-capacity` (DEC-112) or on `task/skill-sonnet-consult`, checked on 2026-10-28 with `git show <ref>:docs/delivery/decisions.md | grep '^## DEC-'`. It is a gap in `main`'s numbering. The builder kept DEC-116 (already cited by the code and tests) rather than take DEC-139, because a gap cannot collide with a number that a branch takes later.

1. **The cipher and the dependency.** AES-256-GCM from the `cryptography` package, pinned exactly as `cryptography==50.0.2` in `backend/pyproject.toml`. That is the current release on PyPI on 2026-10-28 (the JSON at `https://pypi.org/pypi/cryptography/json` gives `info.version` 50.0.2, uploaded 2026-09-30, 28 days earlier, so the two-week rule is met). Rounds 2 and 3d pinned 50.0.1 (uploaded 2026-08-25), which was not the current release, and said it was 63 days old, which was wrong (it was 64). `uv lock --check` exits 0. The lock file changed only in the `cryptography` entry (its version, sdist and wheel lines); `uv` 0.11.32 rewrote `revision = 5` as `revision = 3` and that one line was put back to 5 by hand, the value the rest of the repository uses. `cryptography` was not a dependency before this task, so this is a new runtime dependency, recorded here under DEC-133.
2. **The key.** `derive_key(secret)` (in the adapter) is HMAC-SHA256 of the link-token secret with the label `diucs:prompt-content:v2`, giving 32 bytes. A secret shorter than 32 bytes is refused (`ValidationFailed`, field name only). A key that is not 32 bytes is refused by `seal_content` and `unseal_content`.
3. **The associated data.** Canonical JSON (sorted keys, no spaces) of `{message_id, recipient_role_id, tenant_id}`. `message_id` is the outbox message id, which is now generated before the seal. Sealed content therefore cannot be moved to another message, tenant or recipient. Tests: `test_content_moved_to_another_message_tenant_or_recipient_is_refused` (three cases).
4. **The nonce.** 96 random bits from `os.urandom`, one per seal, stored at the front of the sealed text. There is no public nonce parameter, so a caller cannot choose or reuse one. Tests: `test_two_seals_of_one_text_differ`, `test_each_seal_has_its_own_nonce`. Mutant: a constant nonce.
5. **Encoding is strict.** The text is `v2.` followed by unpadded base64url. The decoder refuses any `=`, any character outside the alphabet (`validate=True` with `altchars`), any text that does not re-encode to itself (non-canonical), any body shorter than nonce plus tag, a wrong version prefix, and any authentication failure. Each refusal is `ValidationFailed` with the field name only. Tests: a flipped bit at every byte position (two bits per byte), a truncated tail, an appended byte, padding, non-canonical base64, a wrong key, a swapped nonce, a moved message, tenant or recipient, a `v1.` seal, a character outside the alphabet, a non-ASCII character, and a body too short.
6. **Where the cipher lives (corrected in round 3, after the architecture test failed).** The application layer may import no third-party package (`APPLICATION_EXTERNAL = ()` in `tests/architecture/test_import_rules.py`). The round-2 placement in `send_prompts.py` broke that rule. The cipher is now an adapter, `adapters/outbound/content_seal/aes_gcm.py` (`AesGcmContentSealer`), behind the port `ContentSealer` in `application/ports/outbound/sealing.py`. The use case calls the port only. The adapter package is covered by the `Adapters are independent` contract as `diucs.adapters.outbound.*`.
