7. **Constructor.** `SendPrompts` takes `sealer: ContentSealer` in place of the round-2 `secret_store`, and `tenant_id` as its last argument, both supplied by the composition root. DEC-115 item 18 is superseded by this. **Tenant.** This is the only tenant code added. It is tenant-pending: once the seam binds the tenant (DEC-130 item 3), `tenant_id` is read from the bound context instead, and the test in DEC-115 item 25 stays.
8. **Consumer.** I10 opens a `SendMessage` with `derive_key(link_secret)` and `unseal_content(payload["sealed_content"], key, message_id=<outbox message id>, tenant_id=<the tenant of the message>, recipient_role_id=payload["recipient_role_id"])`. The outbox message id is the id of the outbox row the message was stored under. A U01 echo still carries plain `text` and does not use this path. I10 handles both.
9. **Residual, for the owner.** The protection rests on the link-token secret's strength (at least 32 bytes) and on who can read the outbox. Anyone who can read a sealed payload and holds the secret can open it. A rotation plan for the secret is not built here. Its owner and schedule are open.
10. **Checks.** The seal tests are in `backend/tests/unit/adapters/outbound/content_seal/test_aes_gcm.py` and need no Neo4j. Mutants S1 (associated data dropped), S2 (unseal associated data dropped), S3 (constant nonce), S4 (canonical check removed) and the key-length, tenant and tag checks are killed. S9 (`validate=False`) survives and is an equivalent mutant: the canonical re-encode check refuses the same text. The canonical check is the control, and S4 shows it is needed.

## DEC-119 The Service Bus runner pauses after a retry and never exits on a failure (I04)

Status: accepted (builder build-I04, instructed by the orchestrator after verification) · Affects: I04 (`adapters/inbound/workers/servicebus_runner.py`), and every consumer that runs on it: I05, I08, I10, U09 wiring · Hazard H-27, DEC-012.

1. **A retry outcome pauses the receive loop.** An `IdempotentConsumer` returns retry for any exception that is not a `PermanentError`, which includes a Neo4j or bus outage. The message is still abandoned, so Service Bus redelivers it. Before it receives again, the receive loop pauses 1 s, then 2, 4, 8, 16 and 30 s (cap) for each further retry. A message that completes or is a duplicate resets the pause to 1 s. A dead-letter neither pauses nor resets. Without the pause, an outage of a few seconds redelivers the same capacity event at once, up to the max delivery count of 10, and dead-letters work that would have succeeded when the store returned. One pause is taken per batch, not per message.
2. **The receiver connection failure uses the same schedule**, separately counted, and it resets when a receive returns.
3. **The loop catches any Exception**, not only `ServiceBusError` and `OSError`, so the worker never exits (DEC-012). `asyncio.CancelledError` and the stop signal pass through. Logs carry the exception class name only.
4. **Cost, accepted, corrected in round 2:** a poison message (one that raises an error that is not a `PermanentError`) is delivered up to 10 times, with the pauses in item 1 between the deliveries (about 181 s in total: the pause after each of the ten retries, 1+2+4+8+16+30+30+30+30+30 s, including the pause after the tenth abandon). On a **non-session subscription or queue** this stalls the whole receiver for those 181 s, which delays every message behind it. On a **session entity** only the one session loop stalls; the other sessions keep running. Handlers should raise `PermanentError` for a message that can never succeed.
5. **Outage limit (round 2), arithmetic, not a test:** the broker dead-letters a message at its 10th delivery. The nine gaps between the ten deliveries hold 151 s of pauses (1+2+4+8+16+30+30+30+30). With each attempt taking A seconds to fail (A of 31 to 43 s, the in-flight failure time in lesson L14), a continuous outage of about 10 x A + 151 s, that is about **7.7 to 9.7 minutes**, dead-letters the capacity events that were in flight. If attempts fail at once, the limit is 151 s, about **2.5 minutes**. The tenth pause follows the last abandon, which the broker dead-letters, so it is not part of this figure. I04 does not extend the limit; it only stops a short outage from using the attempts. The alarm on the broker's dead-letter count is for the O-series. Recorded as H-30, whose "not proved" line says the same.
6. **Not done:** lock renewal for a long handler (the queue lock is one minute).
Tests: `backend/tests/unit/adapters/inbound/workers/test_servicebus_runner_loop.py` (retry pause and outage) and `::test_cancelling_the_runner_is_not_swallowed` (round 2, a swallowed cancellation fails it). Hazard: H-27 (residual) and H-30 (outage limit).

## DEC-138 Twilio is called through the global endpoint api.twilio.com
Status: accepted (owner decision after testing) · Affects: the live Twilio check (backend/tests/live), the network secret's Allowed websites, and any later Twilio adapter (I06, I07, I10) and the DEC-131 subaccount setup. Supersedes the regional host api.dublin.ie1.twilio.com used in the live check since 75cd0cc.

1. All Twilio calls go to `api.twilio.com`. The Ireland (IE1) regional host is not used.
2. The network secret's Allowed websites must be exactly `api.twilio.com`, with one Username and password secret carrying the API key SID and secret (the proxy attaches credentials only to the exact host listed).
3. Data residency: a request to the global endpoint is not guaranteed to be processed in Ireland. Whether that is acceptable for patient-adjacent message content is a decision the lead developer has recorded here as made by the owner's testing outcome; the information-governance material (DEC-133 item 2) must describe the routing as global. If a regional requirement appears later, this decision is reopened, not worked around.
4. The live check still sends nothing: it reads the account list only.

## DEC-139 The service lock comes before the latest read and before the default effective time, in both use cases (U02 round 3, F1a)
Status: accepted (builder decision, build-U02-r3, under DEC-133; no owner gate) · Numbered DEC-139: DEC-138 is the highest number on `main`; DEC-135 is on `task/F10b-harness-load-protection` and DEC-137 on `task/TEN-001-seam-warn`; DEC-139 is on no local branch (checked with `git show <branch>:docs/delivery/decisions.md`). Affects: U02 (`application/use_cases/confirm_capacity.py`), U01 (`application/use_cases/report_capacity.py`, `ReportCapacity.execute`), `CapacityRepository.lock_service` (`Q-CAP-LOCK-SERVICE`), and the board's order of reports (`latest()`, `Q-CAP-LATEST`). Finding F1 (HIGH, lost update) of verify-U02-r2.

1. **Order inside the unit of work.** Both use cases take the service's write lock (`lock_service`) as the first statement of their unit of work. The replay check, the read of the latest report and the count, and the default effective time all come after the lock. The default effective time is the clock read after the lock.
2. **Why.** `latest()` orders by effective time, not by commit order. A default effective time taken before the lock is the time the caller started, not the time the figure is written. A report that waits behind a confirmation then has an earlier effective time than the confirmation, sorts before it, and the board keeps showing the figure the confirmation copied. The verifier reproduced it: number 5 at 10:04 waited for a confirmation at 10:05, the confirmation committed 3, and the board showed 3. Taken after the lock, the default time is later than every figure committed before it, because the clock does not go backwards and the lock serialises the writers. Taking the lock in `ReportCapacity` alone does not fix it: the time would still be captured before the lock.
3. **Order of refusals kept.** The naive-time and future-time checks on an explicit `effective_at` still run before the unit of work, with the clock read before the lock. The order of refusals is therefore unchanged: value, flag, effective time, then profile and permission. Only the default time moves.
4. **Symptom found while reproducing.** Before this round, a confirmation that waited behind a number read a `now` earlier than that number and raised `ValueError("last_report_at must not be after now")` from `assess_freshness` (`domain/rules/freshness.py`, line 62). That is a plain exception on a path a reporter can reach, not a domain refusal. Test `test_a_confirmation_waiting_behind_a_held_number_is_stored_after_it` reproduces it on the pre-fix code. The fix removes that path for these writes.
5. **Tests.** `test_a_number_reported_while_a_confirmation_is_held_is_not_hidden_behind_it` is the verifier's race; it kills the default time captured before the lock in `ReportCapacity` (mutant M-R-PRELOCK). `test_a_confirmation_waiting_behind_a_held_number_is_stored_after_it` kills the same capture in `ConfirmCapacity` (M-C-PRELOCK). `test_a_number_that_read_the_figure_before_a_confirmation_is_not_written_behind_it` kills the lock removed from `ReportCapacity` (M-R-LOCK); the verifier's race does not, because the tie-break decides it. The lock in `ConfirmCapacity` is killed by the existing held-confirmation tests (M-C-LOCK).
6. **Residual, not changed.** Two writes with the same effective instant tie and fall back to the write time (lesson L65). The clock has microsecond precision, so a tie needs two writes at one instant.

## DEC-140 A number given a time before the latest confirmed figure is refused and writes nothing (U02 round 3, F1b)
Status: accepted (builder decision, build-U02-r3, under DEC-133; no owner gate) · Numbered DEC-140: see DEC-139 for the free-number check; DEC-140 is on no local branch. Affects: U02 and U01 (`ReportCapacity.execute`), the port method `CapacityRepository.latest_confirmation_at` and its query `Q-CAP-LATEST-CONFIRMED-AT`, `domain/errors.py` (`EffectiveBeforeConfirmation`), `docs/spec/contracts.md` Additions, and the text-reply path (U05), which will map the refusal to a reply. The reply wording is not set here.

1. **Rule.** A report with an explicit `effective_at` earlier than the effective time of the latest committed confirmed report of the same service is refused with `EffectiveBeforeConfirmation`, a `ValidationFailed` subclass with field `effective_at`. The check runs inside the unit of work, after the lock and after the permission check, so an unauthorised reporter still learns nothing (DEC-112 item 5). Nothing is written: no report, outbox message, audit entry, communication or idempotency record.
2. **Why.** The confirmation's figure is the one the board shows after the confirmation. A number stored behind it would never reach the board, and the reporter would believe the new figure was shown. Refusal fails closed: the reporter sends the figure again at the current time, which is accepted and becomes the latest figure (test `test_a_refused_back_dated_number_can_be_sent_again_at_the_current_time`). The refusal stores nothing under its key, so the same key or a new one may be used.
3. **"Earlier than" is strict, as decided.** An explicit time equal to the confirmation's instant is not refused by this rule. Two writes at one instant tie and fall back to the write time (lesson L65), so such a number is not reliably the figure the board shows. This residual is recorded here and not changed; it needs two writes at the same microsecond.
4. **Scope.** Only explicit times are checked. The default time is taken after the lock (DEC-139), so it cannot be earlier than a committed confirmation.
5. **Not changed.** A number back-dated behind a later number that is not a confirmation is still accepted, as U01 allows (DEC-100), and is then hidden behind that later number on the board. That is U01 behaviour. Changing it needs its own decision.
6. **Error family.** `EffectiveBeforeConfirmation` subclasses `ValidationFailed`, so the existing handling of `ValidationFailed` at each edge covers it without new code.
7. **Tenancy.** `Q-CAP-LATEST-CONFIRMED-AT` matches the service by `key` and reads `Observation` through `FOCUS`, the same shape as `Q-CAP-TRAILING-CONFIRMS`. It is tenant-pending under the transitional rule (DEC-112 item 11). It adds no key, channel or queue.
8. **Tests.** `test_a_number_given_before_the_latest_confirmation_is_refused_and_writes_nothing` (kills the check removed, mutant M-B-CHECK), `test_a_refused_back_dated_number_can_be_sent_again_at_the_current_time`, and `test_a_number_given_after_the_latest_confirmation_is_accepted_and_becomes_the_latest` (passes on arrival; it guards against refusing every back-dated number). The query has its own test, `test_latest_confirmation_at_is_the_newest_confirmed_effective_time`, in `backend/tests/integration/graph_store/test_capacity_repository.py`.

## DEC-141 Severity gate, round cap and generated evidence (process reset)
Status: accepted · Affects: `references/severity-and-rounds.md`, `assets/verifier-brief.md`, `assets/builder-brief.md`, `scripts/record_checks.py`, `docs/delivery/known-issues.md`. Decided under DEC-133 after five days in which most delivery time went to verification rounds that failed on evidence wording, not on behaviour (F10b took eleven rounds; TEN-001 five; U02 four).

1. **Only blocking findings fail a task.** A wrong or unsafe behaviour, a failing or vacuous acceptance test, a tenant-isolation leak, patient data or a secret, a required check that fails, or an evidence claim that the code contradicts where a reader would rely on it for safety. Wording, quote exactness, line numbers, a surviving mutant that changes no behaviour, and documented residual limits are notes (`references/severity-and-rounds.md`).
2. **Notes go to `docs/delivery/known-issues.md`, one line each, with a trigger.** A verifier may not hold a task for a note. A builder may fix documentation-only notes in the delivery commit.
3. **At most two verification rounds per task.** After a second FAIL the orchestrator reads the diff, then delivers with the findings recorded, sends one targeted fix with a stated acceptance test, or splits the task. A third full round is not run.
4. **Check tables are generated.** `scripts/record_checks.py` runs the checks without a pipe, records each exit code and last line, and writes the table with the commit sha and a dirty-tree flag. Hand-written check tables and paraphrased quotes of earlier commits are not evidence.
5. **Independence is unchanged.** The verifier is still never the builder. The tenant, safety and privacy questions are classified BLOCKING when in doubt.
6. **Recovery rule (lesson L79) stands.** A claim by another session clears a task's evidence. To restore, the original builder label re-claims and replays the evidence rows from the ledger log for the same tip, and the restoration is noted in the verify note. U02 was restored this way and delivered at 953e4ab.

## DEC-142 The tenancy seam is a registry of fixed statements and a generated two-tenant test, not a Cypher reader (supersedes DEC-137)
Status: accepted · Affects: `adapters/outbound/graph_store/queries.py`, `driver.py`, `application/tenancy.py`, the tenancy plan (TEN-001 to TEN-005). DEC-137 was written on `task/TEN-001-seam-warn` and never merged to main; it is withdrawn. Amends the mechanism of DEC-130 items 4 and 5 (the rule that every statement is tenant-scoped stands).

Context. Neo4j Community has one database, so tenants cannot be separated by the database. TEN-001 built a hand-written Cypher reader that proves each statement scoped by parsing it. Five verification rounds found a new shape that slipped through each time (a bare carriage return ending a `//` comment, a duplicate `tenantId` map key, an `UNION` branch, `OR` after the stamp, a back-quoted `true`, a node pattern after `SHORTEST`, a whole-node `SET`). Proving arbitrary Cypher scoped is open-ended. Checking fixed statements is not.

1. **Every statement is fixed text in code.** `run_query` takes only a registered `NamedQuery`, never a string. No f-string, concatenation or caller-supplied text reaches the driver. An architecture test fails the build if any module outside the two adapter query modules calls the driver, a session or a transaction `run`.
2. **Registration is the gate.** A statement is registered once, at import, with a name, its text, and a scenario (item 5). `run_query` refuses a query object that is not in the registry. The registry is built from constants, so the text of every statement can be listed and reviewed.
3. **Tenant binding is unchanged.** `run_query` adds `tenant` from the bound context, refuses when none is bound, and a caller cannot pass `tenant` (this part was verified in TEN-001 rounds 1 and 2 and is kept: `tenancy.py`, the binding in `driver.py`).
4. **A cheap check at registration, not a proof.** A statement must mention `$tenant`, or be on the short global allowlist (migrations, the health ping, shared reference data). This catches omissions only.
5. **The proof is a generated two-tenant test on real Neo4j.** Each registered statement carries a `Scenario`: a seed for two tenants that use the same keys (the collision case), the parameters, and the expected effect for tenant A. The generated test runs the statement as tenant A and then as tenant B, and asserts that a read returns only the caller's rows and that a write leaves the other tenant's graph unchanged and changes the caller's as expected. A statement with no scenario fails the test, so a new statement cannot be added without proof. The verifier checks that each scenario uses colliding keys.
6. **Network scope (DEC-132)** uses the same mechanism: a scope id is bound the same way, and projection statements are registered statements with their own scenarios.
7. **What is dropped.** The tokenizer and structural pass in `queries.py`, the rule codes, `is_unscoped`, and the round-3 to round-5 reader work. The TEN-001 branch is not merged; its tip (`2bdca21`) is kept as a verified bundle, `docs/delivery/archive/ten-001-seam-warn-2026-10-28.bundle` (a tag push is refused by the sandbox proxy).
8. **What is kept.** `tenancy.py` (the tenant context, the scope id and its validation), the `run_query` binding and refusal, and the architecture scan for raw driver use.
9. **Residual risk, stated.** A developer can write a wrong statement together with a weak scenario that passes. The controls are review of each scenario alongside its statement, the verifier's predicate-removal mutant per task, and the generated test running on every statement every time.
10. **Cost.** The seam is three tasks, not five rounds of one: the registry and architecture rule, the generated test, and the conversion of the 107 existing statements into registered ones with scenarios (bundled by module, DEC-143).

## DEC-143 First milestone is a two-tenant vertical slice; tenancy work is bundled by module
Status: accepted · Affects: the tenancy plan (`docs/delivery/tenancy-plan.md`), `docs/plan/tasks.json` (epic T), the order of stages S2 to S5.

1. **Milestone M1.** A reporter sends a capacity figure by text; it is received, stored and shown on the wall board; this works for two tenants at once, each seeing only its own services, on real Neo4j. It proves the core loop and tenancy together. Networks and the cross-tenant projection come after M1 (DEC-132).
2. **Bundle the tenancy work.** The 86 TEN tasks stay as the checklist but are claimed, built and verified in about eight bundles by module: graph-store repositories, audit, application use cases, outbound adapters, inbound adapters and workers, API and sign-in, front end, and tests and harness. One claim, one evidence file and one verification per bundle. The fixed cost of claim, evidence, verification and delivery is paid per task, so fewer, larger tasks cut it sharply.
3. **Parallelism is capped by the machine.** At most three builders run at once. Neo4j integration tests run one directory at a time under the lock. After the seam lands, each test run uses a unique tenant so runs can share one Neo4j.
4. **The orchestrator selects M1's tasks by dependency** and records them in `docs/plan/milestones.md`.


## DEC-144 The registry seam as built (TEN-001, refines DEC-142)
Status: accepted · Affects: `application/tenancy.py`, `adapters/outbound/graph_store/queries.py`, `unconverted.py`, `driver.py`, `health.py`, `resource_store.py`, `backend/tests/architecture/test_single_execution_point.py`, every `run_query` call site. Decided by the TEN-001 builder under DEC-133 where DEC-142 and the prompt left a point open.

1. **`run_query(tx, query, /, *, registry=None, **params)` and `run_global_query` take a `NamedQuery`.** In order, before any Cypher is sent: a string, or any object that is not the registered statement (the same id with other text, mode, scope or scenario is not it), raises `QueryNotRegistered`; a `tenant` or `parameters` keyword raises `TenantParameterRefused` (`parameters=` is neo4j's own second way to hand over parameters); a global statement through `run_query`, or a tenant statement through `run_global_query`, raises `GlobalStatementMisuse`; no scope bound raises `TenantNotBound`. Otherwise the statement runs with `tenant=<bound scope>` written first. A bound network scope passes the same parameter (DEC-132). The 92 call sites changed from `run_query(tx, "Q-X", ...)` to `run_query(tx, REGISTRY.get("Q-X"), ...)`: mechanical, no statement text changed.
2. **The ratchet is `UNCONVERTED`, in `graph_store/unconverted.py`.** It lists 126 ids: the 109 tenant statements registered at import by the two adapter packages (110 on main, less the ping) and the 17 `Q-RES-*` templates. All of them lack `$tenant`, and all have `scenario=None`. (The 107 in the TEN-001 prompt predates statements added since; the count here is the measured one.) A tenant statement that does not mention `$tenant`, or has no scenario, registers only when its id is on the list; `ScenarioMissing` and `TenantPredicateMissing` otherwise, naming the id and never the text. The list may only shrink: a test pins `len(UNCONVERTED) <= 126`, and a second test requires it to equal `REGISTRY.ratchet_ids()` exactly, so a converted statement must leave the list.
3. **One transitional exemption, and it is the ratchet's.** While the statements are unconverted the existing repositories, use cases and integration tests (none of which bind a tenant yet; TEN-003 builds the fixtures) must keep working. So a statement that does not mention `$tenant` (`is_tenant_pending`) runs with no scope bound and with no `tenant` parameter, as before the seam; with a scope bound it receives `tenant` unused. A statement that mentions `$tenant` never runs unbound. This is the warn-mode exemption of the withdrawn reader without the mode, the counters or the log: when the list is empty the branch is deleted (the last conversion bundle does it). Until then "an unbound call is refused" holds for converted statements only, and an unconverted statement is not isolated by this seam. That is the state of every statement today.
4. **Global statements.** `GLOBAL_QUERY_IDS` is a read-only mapping from id to reason and holds one entry, `Q-NEO4J-PING` (`health.py` now registers it with `scope="global"` and runs it through `run_global_query`). A global statement must be on it and must not mention `$tenant`. Migrations are not `NamedQuery`s: `migrations.py` runs schema statements with a raw `tx.run` and is the one other file the architecture test allows to execute. Shared reference data reads (Concept and code-system lookups) are not put on the allowlist here: they stay on the ratchet until the conversion bundle for the mapper and resource store decides which are global.
5. **Templates.** The 17 resource-store statements are `QueryTemplate`s, registered at import with one `{insert}` place. `REGISTRY.instance(template_id, insert)` expands one the first time a label chain or relationship type is used, only when the insert fullmatches `(:[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*)+`, so only identifiers can enter the text, and returns the same object after that. An instance takes the template's ratchet place by its id before the first colon; so a plain statement id cannot contain a colon (`Q-RES-LOCK:Evil` could not borrow the template's place). The labels come from resource types at run time, so these instances cannot all be registered at import; that is the one exception to "registered once, at import", and the registry, not the caller, builds the text.
6. **`Scenario` is a placeholder** (`Scenario(description: str)`) in `queries.py`, so the `scenario` field exists on `NamedQuery` and `QueryTemplate`. TEN-004 replaces the type and builds the generated test.
7. **The architecture test** has these rules: raw `.run` or `.execute_query` only in `driver.py` and `migrations.py`; `run_query` and `run_global_query` named only in `graph_store/` and `audit_neo4j/`; `NamedQuery(` and `QueryTemplate(` constructed only there; no call of either runner with a string literal or a built string, and no `registry=` keyword in `src` (the tests pass their own registry); no f-string, `+`, `%`, `.format`, `.join` or `+=` that builds a string with an upper-case Cypher word. Seven statements splice a module constant today (capacity 2, communication 3, scheduled work 2); they are a ratchet by file and count, which a conversion bundle lowers. The ContextVar rule, the binding-sites rule and the no-`tenant`-parameter rule are reused from the archived branch. A probe module that breaks every rule fails the check (the test builds the tree plus a probe).
8. **Dropped from the earlier branch (DEC-142 item 7), and not built:** the tokenizer and structural pass, the rule codes, `is_unscoped`, warn and enforce mode, `TenancyMode`, the counters and logs, `Settings.tenancy_mode`. `bootstrap/settings.py` is not changed. `ScheduledWorkRepository` lost an unused `store` constructor argument, because the `registry=` rule would otherwise have to allow it.
9. **What this seam does not do.** The check at registration mentions `$tenant` only; `$tenant` in a `RETURN` or a comment passes it. Cypher built from names the scan cannot see as Cypher text is not caught by the architecture test (the registry, which runs only registered text, is the backstop). Registration at import is a convention the tests do not enforce, except for template instances. None of this proves a statement is isolated: the generated two-tenant test (TEN-004) is the proof, and it is not built yet.

## DEC-148 HandleInboundText: capacity replies, the age test behind hazard H-31, and the choices the prompt leaves open (U05)
Status: accepted (builder build-U05, under DEC-133) · Affects: U05 (`application/use_cases/handle_inbound_text.py`), U01 and U02 (an echo switch on their commands), D15 (message C12), U04 (the C9 records it reads), U07 (the out-of-range flag), U22 (booking words), I07 (the consumer that calls this use case), H-31 and H-32 in the hazard log. Numbers: DEC-148 was checked on 2026-10-28 against `main` (top DEC-144) and `task/B1a-uow-harness`, `task/I07-sms-inbound`, `task/R05-board-confirm` (none has DEC-148); DEC-145 to DEC-147 are held by other branches and were not read.

1. **The shape.** `HandleInboundText(uow_factory, clock, ids, idempotency, cache, report_capacity, confirm_capacity)`. One read pass in a unit of work that is not committed (phone, roles, latest prompt, the figure and the count for each service, the cache key), then the writes. A figure goes to `ReportCapacity`, a Y to `ConfirmCapacity`, each in its own unit of work with its own audit entry and echo (the rules of U01 and U02 are not repeated). Then one last unit of work writes this use case's own record: every refusal as a `Communication` (`in_response_to` the prompt) plus a `SendMessage` outbox message with the U01 payload (`communication_id`, `message_id`, `channel`, `recipient_role_id`, `text`), one `AuditEntry` (`use_case="HandleInboundText"`, action `C`, snapshot `{outcome, items, messages}`) and the idempotency record. The reply texts hold no token, so the payload carries `text` as U01 does and is not sealed (U03 seals because its text carries a link token).
2. **Hazard H-31: the age test (the decision the prompt asks for).** A Y is acted on only if the figure it would confirm was **no older than `CONFIRMABLE_FIGURE_AGE` = D05's `STALE_WARNING_AFTER` (2 hours) at the moment the prompt named it**: `figure_is_out_of_date(as_of, prompt_sent_at)` is `prompt_sent_at - as_of > 2 h`, both as UTC instants. Exactly 2 hours is inside the window, as D05's `stale_warning` is strictly more than 2 hours. A figure reported after the prompt has a negative age and is not out of date. The figure is the service's latest report, which is what `ConfirmCapacity` would confirm; its time is its `effective_at`. Why these choices: (a) the constant is the one the board already shows ("Data is over 2 hrs old"), so there is one number for stale, not two; (b) the unit label's window ("in the next 2 hrs") is text, not data (DEC-115 item 13), and many labels have none (beds, occupied patients), so it cannot be the test; (c) it is measured at the prompt's send time, not at the reply: a service that reports every 2 hours has a figure about 1 hour 55 minutes old when the next prompt goes out, and an age test at the reply time would refuse about half the Ys in the ordinary cycle; the reporter read the figure with its date in the prompt (DEC-115 item 11) and the prompt closes 30 minutes after it was sent, so the figure at the reply is never more than window plus 30 minutes old. The test runs only when D11 has already said `RecordConfirm`; a third Y is still C6 and a late Y is still C5. The refusal calls no `ConfirmCapacity`, writes no report and leaves the stored figure with its own time. Consequences, accepted: a service that reports less often than every 2 hours (a daily ward, a twice-a-day service) can never confirm by Y and always sends the number; a Y to a C8 reminder is refused when the figure is old, which it usually is by then.
3. **What the reporter is told (C12, new).** A Y that is refused for age gets C12: `DIUCS: {service}. The figure on record, {value} {unit}, is out of date (as of {as_of}), so Y was not used. Send {reply_with} (0 = closed). By {reply_by}.` `as_of` is the full London date and time (`format_stamp`, `23 Oct 08:15`, the wording of DEC-115 item 11), `reply_with` is `a number` or, on a grouped prompt, `B and a number` (the template refuses any other text), and `reply_by` is now plus 30 minutes. C12 is text only, added to `MessageId`, `templates.py`, `contracts.md` and `domain-rules.md` section 9. It counts as a prompt (item 4), so the number sent next is judged against it, as it is after a C5. The prompt allowed C2 wording; C2 does not say the figure is out of date, and a reporter who sent Y and is asked a bare question would not know why. A C5 ("Too late") over a stale figure still invites a Y; that Y is then refused by this test, so the reporter is told twice but nothing stale is recorded.
4. **The open prompt.** The latest of C1, C2, C5, C8, C9 or C12 sent to any role this phone holds, by send time; reply-by is `reply_by(sent_at)` (sent plus 30 minutes, `schedule.REPLY_WINDOW`). No prompt at all: nothing is recorded, the help text (C11) is sent, outcome `help`. A reply is acted on as the role the prompt was sent to (`recipient_role_id`); a phone with two roles for one service acts as that one.
5. **Who is dropped.** An unknown phone, a known person who holds no reporter role, and a prompt about a service that is no longer active get outcome `dropped`: no write, no message, no idempotency record. A booking word (`CONFIRMED`, `DECLINE`) from a known phone is `booking-not-handled`: nothing written or sent (U22 adds them). A malformed hash raises `ValidationFailed` and two active people on one hash raise `Conflict` (the person repository's own refusals): the message is not processed, which fails closed.
6. **The time of the reply.** The reply is judged at `min(received_at, clock.now())`: a delay in the queue does not make a reply on time late, and a received time in the future is not believed. A naive `received_at` raises `ValidationFailed("received_at")`. The reports and confirmations themselves are stamped by U01 and U02 with the clock, as they always are.
7. **The out-of-range flag (U07 is not built).** `RecordNumber.flag` is passed to `ReportCapacity` in `ReportCapacityCommand.flag`, as the prompt says. U01 refuses any flag with `ValidationFailed("flag", ...)` until U07 (its test `test_a_report_flag_is_refused_until_u07_records_flags`). A figure the reporter has sent twice must not be lost, so when the refusal is for the field `flag` alone the use case passes the same figure again without it. **Until U07 lands the flag `out-of-range-twice` is not recorded** (known-issues; the first call is the proof of what was meant). When U07 lands the first call succeeds, the fallback never runs, and `_report` and the test `test_the_out_of_range_flag_is_dropped_only_while_report_capacity_cannot_record_it` are removed.
8. **An echo switch on U01 and U02.** `ReportCapacityCommand` and `ConfirmCapacityCommand` get `send_echo: bool = True`, part of the command fingerprint. False sends no echo (the performer is still recorded). This use case passes True for a prompt about one service (the C3 or C4 is the reply) and False for a grouped prompt, which is answered by one C10 ("suppress per-service C3"). This is the one change to U01 and U02 files; it is the smallest way to meet the prompt without writing a second echo.
9. **Grouped prompts (C9; U04 is not built).** U04's record keeping is one `Communication` per grouped service, all `C9`, the same send time and text. The group is every service of this phone with a C9 of that send time and text. The letters are rebuilt with `letters_for` over the service names, as the prompt did, and then **checked against the stored C9 text** (`B) UCR West ` must be in it): if a service has left the phone since, every later letter would shift and a reply would land on the wrong service, so on any mismatch, or fewer than two services, nothing is recorded and the help text is sent. Nothing is guessed: a figure with no letter, a letter that is not in the group, a letter on a prompt about one service, or any unrecognised part makes the whole text not understood (C11, nothing recorded). A plain Y confirms every service of the group; `Y A` and `A 4` name one. Each item is judged separately (D11 per item, the age test per service). The reply is one C10 for what was recorded (`updated`), with `waiting` the services of the group this text did not name and that no report has answered since the prompt (the latest report is not later than the send time); each refused item gets its own C5, C6, C7 or C12 with its own letter. Limits (known-issues): C7 says "send 40 again", which on a grouped prompt must be lettered, and a refusal sent for one service becomes the phone's latest prompt, so a later lettered reply for another service of the group is not understood until a new prompt.
10. **What a number with no figure on record gets.** A late number or a Y when the service has no figure cannot be worded as C5 or confirmed: C2 ("How many ... can you take ...?") is sent, using `split_unit_label`; a unit label with no readable window cannot be worded as C2 (DEC-115 item 13) and the help text goes instead. Outcome `late` for a late number, `need-number` for a Y.
11. **Outcomes.** The prompt's eight (`recorded`, `confirmed`, `late`, `confirm-limit`, `recheck`, `help`, `dropped`, `booking-not-handled`) and two more: `stale-figure` (item 3) and `need-number` (a Y with nothing to confirm). A text that is several items takes the first outcome of this order: recorded, confirmed, recheck, late, confirm-limit, stale-figure, need-number, help; `service_ids` are the services whose items had that outcome, in letter order (service order for one service). `HandleInboundTextResult` also has `replayed: bool = False`, as U01 to U03 results do.
12. **Idempotency and a retry.** The provider message id is the command key. The record keeps the result and a hash of the phone and text (the receive time is left out: a redelivery may carry a later one); a repeat with the same body replays the result with `replayed=True` and writes nothing; another body or phone is `Conflict` (DEC-104). The figure goes to U01 or U02 under `{message id}:number:{service}` or `{message id}:confirm:{service}`. The writes of U01 and U02 commit before this use case's own record, so a failure in between leaves the figure written and no record. The retry (same message id) finds the sub-key, does not decide again from a state that now includes its own write, finishes the record, and writes the figure once. The cache is written last, after the commit, for the same reason: a retry must not read its own first try as a repeat.
13. **The out-of-range figure (cache key `recheck:{phone_hash}:{service_id}`).** The value is the figure as text. The time to live is until the later of the prompt's reply-by and the end of the correction window of the latest figure (a figure inside the correction window is judged after the prompt has closed, and its repeat would otherwise never be recorded), and at least one second. The key is deleted when a figure is recorded or a Y is confirmed for that service, so a later 40 is a first 40 again. The cache adds the tenant prefix; the key is built in one function (`recheck_key`).
14. **The text is never stored.** Not in an audit snapshot, a communication, the idempotency record (a hash only) or a log. The reply messages never echo the typed text. A test searches the audit, communication, outbox and snapshot nodes for the phone hash and the typed text.
15. **Tenancy (DEC-130 to DEC-134).** The command has no tenant and a test pins its fields. The consumer (I07 and its successor) binds the tenant resolved from the number called before it calls this use case, and 'unknown phone' means unknown within that tenant. This task adds no Cypher: every read and write goes through the existing repositories and use cases, whose statements are on the ratchet and run in the bound scope. The cache prefixes its keys. The cross-scope reads this task would need are none: a reply concerns one phone, one tenant and its own services.
16. **Two small things.** `format_stamp` (`london_time.py`) words the full date and time of a figure for C12; U03's private `_stamp` does the same for C1, and a test pins the two to the same wording until U03's file is next changed (known-issues). U04 should reuse `format_stamp`.
17. **Round 2 (verify-U05-r1 finding 1, H-32): a Y is refused when a number has been reported since the prompt.** Items 2 and 6 let a Y confirm a figure the reporter never saw: a figure newer than the prompt has a negative age, so the age test passed it. Now `_judge_confirm` also refuses, with C12 (the same "figure out of date, send a number" message) and outcome `stale-figure`, recording nothing and not calling `ConfirmCapacity`, when a **number** report for the service has an `effective_at` strictly later than the time the prompt was sent (`reports_since(service, prompt_sent_at)`, read in the same unit of work as the rest of the judgement; no new Cypher). Exactly at the prompt time is inside. The C12 names the figure now on record and its date, so the reporter sees what changed. Difference from the wording of the round-2 brief, which said "the latest figure's effective time is later than the prompt": a **confirmation** after the prompt is not counted, only a number. The brief's literal rule would refuse the second Y to one prompt (the first Y writes a confirmation stamped after the prompt), and so make the third Y unreachable, which breaks the existing tests `test_a_third_y_in_a_row_is_refused_with_c6` and `test_a_grouped_third_y_for_one_service_is_refused_with_c6` and the D11 rule of two confirmations in a row. A confirmation repeats the figure the prompt named, so it does not make the figure one the reporter has not seen; a number does. The age function `figure_is_out_of_date` is unchanged (a figure newer than the prompt is still "not too old"; the newer-figure refusal is a separate test). Consequence, recorded in known-issues: a Y after the reporter's own number (`4` then `Y` on one prompt) is refused with C12, because that number was reported after the prompt; the reporter sends a number. The existing test `test_the_commands_name_the_reporter_the_text_source_and_the_role` sent `4` and then `Y`; its order is now `Y` then `4`, with every assertion unchanged (the only existing test whose steps changed, apart from the one the brief named, `test_a_figure_reported_after_the_prompt_is_not_judged_out_of_date`, which pinned the bug and is replaced by `test_a_y_is_refused_when_a_number_was_reported_after_the_prompt`).
18. **Round 2 (finding 1): the judged report is passed to `ConfirmCapacity`.** `ConfirmCapacity` re-reads the latest under its own lock after this use case judged, so a report landing between the two was confirmed unjudged. `ConfirmCapacityCommand` has a new optional field `expected_latest_id: str | None = None` (default None: existing callers are unchanged, and it is not part of the command fingerprint, because it is a precondition and not part of what the command is: a replay of a stored result is returned before the check, as the stored write was judged when it was made). When set, `ConfirmCapacity` reads the latest under the service lock and, if its id is another, raises `LatestReportChanged` and writes nothing (no report, no echo, no audit, no idempotency record, so the same key may be tried again). `LatestReportChanged` is a subclass of `Conflict` (the family of "the request clashes with the current state of the record"; the R05 409 mapping and any `except Conflict` already treat it as one) and carries `latest_value` and `latest_at` of the report that is now the latest. The check comes after `NothingToConfirm` and before the consecutive-confirm limit. This is the one change to U02 source in this round, minimal, with tests in `test_confirm_capacity.py` (the judged report is written, a newer report refuses and writes nothing, the default unchanged, nothing to confirm still first, and a held-lock race). `HandleInboundText` passes the id of the report it judged and maps the refusal to C12 naming the figure now on record, outcome `stale-figure`; the reporter is not told the Y was recorded and nothing is. Test of the race in the use case: `test_a_report_that_lands_between_the_judgement_and_the_write_is_not_confirmed` (the service lock is held, the use case judges, a number lands, the lock is released).
19. **Round 2 (finding 2): a Y processed long after it was received is refused (fails closed).** The Y was judged at `min(received_at, clock)` but `ConfirmCapacity` stamps `effective_at` with `clock.now()`, so a Y received at 10:25 and processed at 14:00 recorded a figure from 08:15 (5 h 45 min old) as current at 14:00. New named constant `CONFIRM_PROCESSING_LIMIT` = 10 minutes (**[ASSUMED]**, in `docs/spec/domain-rules.md` section 2.2 beside the other assumed values; the value is the owner's to change). When `clock.now() - received_at` is greater than the limit the Y is not recorded, `ConfirmCapacity` is not called, the reporter gets C12 and the outcome is `stale-figure`; at exactly the limit the Y is inside it. Both instants are compared as UTC, so the clock changes of 2026-03-29 and 2026-10-25 cannot move the line (tests with the processing time on the other side of each change). The C12 is sent at the clock time, not at the received time: its reply-by counts from when the reporter is told (the Y received at 10:25 and refused at 14:00 asks for a number by 14:30, and a number at 14:20 is on time against that C12), through an optional send time on the internal reply. **A Y delayed by an outage or a queue is therefore refused and the reporter is asked for a number**: this fails closed, at the cost of one extra text after an outage. A number (not a Y) is not held to the limit: it carries its own value, and its handling is unchanged. A figure the reporter sends is judged at the time it was received as before (item 6).

20. **Round 3 (verify-U05-r2, H-32): "a number reported after the prompt" is decided by record order, not by the effective time.** Item 17 compared a number's `effective_at` with the prompt time. U01 allows an advisor-phoned report with an explicit `effective_at` (DEC-140), so a lead could record a 7 at 10:03 for 09:59 (or exactly 10:00) after a C1 of 10:00 that named 3, and the Y of 10:05 was confirmed and stamped a figure the reporter never saw. (`expected_latest_id` cannot catch it: the id is read after the 7 landed.) Now the test is the store's write time. **Mechanism (a), no U03 change and no new Cypher statement:** `CapacityReport` has a new optional field `written_at: datetime | None` (default None, `compare=False` so it is not part of a report's identity), which `report_from_fhir` fills from the Observation's `meta.lastUpdated` (the resource store already stamps it and `get` already returns it; the mapping to FHIR never writes it). `recorded_since_prompt(report, prompt_sent_at)` is true when `written_at` is at or after the prompt's time cut to the whole second, both as UTC instants; a report with no write time counts as since (fail closed). `_number_after_prompt` applies it to the latest report and to the reports `reports_since` returns (effective from the prompt on), counting numbers only. A refused Y records nothing, does not call `ConfirmCapacity`, and sends C12 (outcome `stale-figure`), as before. Mechanism (b) (the prompt names the report id it showed) was not needed: it would change U03, which is delivered. A new Cypher statement was avoided on purpose: the registry now refuses a new tenant statement unless it mentions `$tenant` and has a scenario, and the capacity statements are still tenant-pending. **Same-second consequence (accepted, stated):** the store stamps whole seconds (lesson L65) and the prompt time is cut to the whole second, so a tie counts as newer. A legitimate Y is wrongly refused only if the figure it answers was written in the same whole second as the prompt was sent, or in the same second as a later one; the reporter is then asked for a number (C12). The rule is not fail-open anywhere in that second. **Choices:** (1) the effective time no longer decides, so a number written before the prompt with an effective time after it (only a direct write can do that: U01 refuses an effective time after now) is the figure the prompt named and the Y confirms it if nothing was written since; the old rule (effective strictly after the prompt) is replaced, not kept alongside, and `test_the_newer_figure_test_is_strictly_later_than_the_prompt` is replaced by the whole-second tests. (2) A number written after the prompt whose effective time is earlier than the latest report's, so that it is neither the latest nor effective from the prompt on, is not looked at: it does not change which figure is current, so the Y confirms the figure the prompt named, which is still current. (3) A confirmation is never a newer number (item 17 stands): the second Y to one prompt is still handled as before (`test_the_second_y_to_one_prompt_is_still_confirmed_after_the_first_ys_confirmation`). (4) The `expected_latest_id` check and `CONFIRM_PROCESSING_LIMIT` are unchanged. (5) The tests set the store's clock (`STORE_CLOCK`, the `uow_clock` fixture of the test module) and move it with `Rig.say`, because the default test clock is later than every prompt in the file. (6) Not changed: `_waiting` (the C10 list of services nothing has answered) still reads `effective_at <= prompt_sent_at`; a back-dated figure leaves its service on the "still waiting" list, which asks for one more reply and records nothing.

Tests: `backend/tests/integration/application/test_handle_inbound_text.py`, `backend/tests/unit/application/use_cases/test_handle_inbound_text_age.py`, `backend/tests/unit/domain/messages/` (C12), `backend/tests/unit/domain/rules/test_london_time.py` (`format_stamp`), and the `send_echo` tests in the U01 and U02 integration files. Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/U05.md`.

## DEC-149 The Twilio inbound webhook (I07): tenant from the number and account, one refusal for everything unverified, and two small port additions
Status: accepted · Affects: `adapters/inbound/sms_webhook/routes.py`, `adapters/inbound/fhir_rest/app.py` and `errors.py`, `application/ports/outbound/cache.py` (`Cache.set_if_absent`), `event_bus.py` (`EventBus.enqueue`, `BusMessage.tenant_id`), `tenant_lines.py` (new), the Redis, Service Bus, in-memory and `ManualEventBus` adapters, `.importlinter`, I08 (consumer of both queues), TEN-007 and TEN-041 (the real line directory). Decided by the I07 builder under DEC-133. Numbered DEC-149: DEC-145 to DEC-148 are taken on other branches.

1. **Routes.** `POST /webhooks/sms/twilio` (a text) and `POST /webhooks/sms/twilio/status` (a delivery receipt), in `sms_webhook/routes.py`, mounted by `create_app`. The FHIR body-type check (`BodyTypeMiddleware`) now skips paths under `/webhooks/`, because Twilio posts a form and the check answered 415. Everything else under it is unchanged, and a test pins that `/webhooksx/...` is not exempt. Two named `ignore_imports` pairs in `.importlinter` (L108): `fhir_rest.app -> sms_webhook.routes` (the mount) and `sms_webhook.routes -> sms_twilio.twilio_sms` (it reuses I06's `parse_status_callback`, so the status vocabulary has one definition). Each has a probe module in `test_import_rules.py`. `diucs.adapters.inbound.sms_webhook.routes` is added to `BINDING_MODULES` in the architecture test: it is an edge that binds a verified tenant (the file said the edge tasks add their module in their own commit).
2. **Collaborators come from `app.state.sms_webhook`** (a frozen `SmsWebhookDeps`: cache, event bus, phone hasher, secret store, line directory, public base URL), set by the process that serves the app, as the live stream does for its cache (DEC-096). The clock and the unit of work come from the container. A missing one is a 500 for every request (a configuration error never opens the route). No production entry point exists yet, so nothing sets it outside the tests; the process that serves the app must pass `Settings.base_url` as the public base URL and the real cache, bus, secret store and directory.
3. **Tenant from the number and the account, never from the caller (DEC-131 item 2).** A new port `InboundLineDirectory` (`application/ports/outbound/tenant_lines.py`) has `for_number(to, account_sid)` and `for_account(account_sid)` and returns an `InboundLine(tenant_id, token_secret)`: the tenant and the secret-store reference of that subaccount's auth token (DEC-131 item 4). The only implementation is the fake `FixedInboundLines` (a number-to-tenant map; a number matches only with its own account, an account belongs to one tenant). The tenant directory is built by TEN-007 and TEN-041 plugs its inbound-number resolver in here. `To` and `AccountSid` in an unverified body only choose which token to check against. The tenant is bound (`bind_tenant`) only after the signature verifies, and everything after (the allow-list read, the replay key, the queue message) reads it from the binding, not from an argument. Delivery receipts use `AccountSid` alone (`To` is the recipient there, DEC-131 item 2: "matched by AccountSid and message id").
4. **One refusal.** A request with a query string, a body that is not one plain urlencoded form (wrong type, over 64 KiB, over 100 fields, a field twice, not UTF-8), a missing or malformed signature, `To` or `AccountSid`, a number or account that is not in the directory, a line with an invalid tenant id or no token in the secret store, or a signature that does not verify, gets the same answer: 403, `text/plain`, the body `Forbidden`. The reason goes to the log only (a code, never a number, tenant list or text). **This departs from DEC-131 item 2 and the I07 prompt**, which answer an unknown number with 200 and empty TwiML and a bad signature with 403: that pair lets anyone who can reach the URL tell a tenant's number from a number that is not one. A request whose number is unknown cannot be verified, so refusing it is also the safe reading. No database is read before the signature verifies (a test counts unit-of-work openings). The signature is `X-Twilio-Signature` over the public URL from settings plus the sorted POST parameters, compared with `hmac.compare_digest`. No claim is made about response timing between the refusals.
5. **What an accepted request does.** After verification, with the tenant bound: a blank text, a text over 1,600 characters, a `MessageSid` that is not 2 to 64 letters and digits, a sender that is not a UK mobile, two active people on the sender's phone (Conflict, DEC-065) or no active person on it are all dropped with 200 and the same empty TwiML as an accepted text, so the answer says nothing about the allow-list. The allow-list read is `uow.people.find_by_phone_hash` in a unit of work that is never committed. An accepted text is queued as `BusMessage(id=MessageSid, topic="inbound.replies", event_type="SmsReceived", payload={phone_hash, text, received_at, provider_message_id}, tenant_id)`; `received_at` is the container clock in UTC. The number is never queued or logged.
6. **Replay marker.** A new `Cache.set_if_absent(key, value, ttl) -> bool` (one `SET NX PX` on Redis; one step on the fake; a ttl that is not positive is a `ValueError`; contract tests run on both). The key is `idem:sms:<tenant>:<MessageSid>`, built in `replay_key` from the bound tenant, one day long (`REPLAY_TTL`, architecture.md F1). Two tenants can therefore receive the same sid. The marker is taken after the allow-list read and before the queue send; if the send raises, the marker is removed and the route answers 500, so Twilio's retry is queued and not taken for a replay. If the removal itself fails the marker stays for a day and that one text is lost; this is logged by class name, and the Service Bus duplicate detection on the id (20 s) and the consumer's durable check (I04) are the other two layers. Receipts take no marker: their id `MessageSid:MessageStatus` is idempotent at the bus and in the tracker.
7. **Queue send.** A new `EventBus.enqueue(queue, message)` sends to a queue now (Service Bus `send_messages` on the queue; `ManualEventBus` records it with `enqueued_to` and `take_enqueued`). `publish` takes topics only and `schedule` takes a time (I02), so neither could send the prompt's `inbound.replies` message. A name that is not a queue is a `ValidationFailed`. `BusMessage` gains `tenant_id: str | None = None`, which the Service Bus adapter sends as the application property `tenantId` (DEC-130 item 7). It is optional until every producer sets it; the outbound-adapters bundle makes it mandatory and makes the consumer bind it.
8. **Receipts.** A verified receipt whose `MessageStatus` I06 maps is queued as `BusMessage(id="<MessageSid>:<MessageStatus>", topic="inbound.receipts", event_type="SmsDeliveryReceived", payload={provider_message_id, status (mapped: queued, sent, delivered or failed), error_code or None}, tenant_id)` and answered 200 with the same empty TwiML. A status I06 does not map (accepted, scheduled, read, ...) is dropped with 200. This is the payload I08 reads ("the I07 status callback fields").
9. **Logging.** A line carries a reason code and the Twilio message id (when it is well-formed), and for a configuration fault the tenant id. Never a number, a text, a phone hash, a token or the signature. A test captures every log record of accepted, dropped and refused requests.
10. **Tenant-pending.** `Q-PERSON-PHONE-FIND` (the allow-list lookup) is unconverted (no `$tenant`, DEC-144 item 3), so the person lookup is not yet filtered by tenant in Neo4j. The route binds the tenant before it runs, so converting the statement isolates it with no change here. Until then a person who is in the graph is found for any tenant's number. The edge, the replay marker and the queue message are tenant-scoped now.
11. **Needs a real value at deploy:** each tenant's Twilio subaccount, number and auth token (secret store), the real directory (TEN-007, TEN-041), the production wiring of the cache, bus and secret store, and the public base URL per tenant host (DEC-134 item 3 gives each tenant its own host; today one base URL from settings is used).



## DEC-146 The unit of work binds one scope on a scoped transaction (TEN-002, refines DEC-144)
Status: accepted · Affects: `graph_store/driver.py`, `unit_of_work.py`, `connection.py`, `idempotency_store.py` (`DriverIdempotencyStore` only), `audit_neo4j/audit_log.py` (`ensure_audit_head` only), `queries.py`, `application/tenancy.py`, `application/ports/outbound/unit_of_work.py`, `tests/architecture/test_single_execution_point.py`. Decided by the B1a builder under DEC-133 where the TEN-002 prompt and DEC-144 differ. The prompt was written before the registry design and speaks of warn and enforce mode; DEC-142 item 7 and DEC-144 item 8 withdrew the mode, so those parts are not built.

1. **Not built, because the mode is gone.** `TenancyMode`, `set_tenancy_mode`, `Container.tenancy_mode`, the start-up log line that counts unscoped statements, the unbound-open counters (`unbound_open_counts`, `reset_unbound_open_counts`) and their once-per-kind warning, `RawTransactionRefused`, and the `kind` argument of `scope_for_new_unit`. `container.py` and `settings.py` are not changed. The prompt's test 14 (container) and the `enforce` halves of tests 3, 5, 8, 11, 12 and 18 do not exist. TEN-030 empties the ratchet and makes the refusals unconditional.
2. **`ScopedTx(tx, scope)` and `Tx`.** `ScopedTx` is a frozen pair with no `run`. `type Tx = AsyncManagedTransaction | AsyncTransaction | ScopedTx` is the transitional alias; the repositories keep their annotation (they receive a `ScopedTx` through the same `cast` the unit of work always used) and TEN-030 narrows the alias. `run_query` and `run_global_query` take a `Tx`. The scope in `run_query` is `tx.scope` for a `ScopedTx` and the ambient scope for a raw transaction. A `ScopedTx` never falls back to the ambient context: a unit opened under `a1` sends `a1` however the context is rebound afterwards, and a unit opened with nothing bound stays unbound even if a scope is bound later. The order of checks in `run_query` (DEC-144 item 1) is unchanged; only where the scope comes from changed. A raw transaction is still accepted, because every existing statement runs on one in tests and in the repositories' own tests; refusing it belongs with the ratchet's end (TEN-030).
3. **An unbound unit of work opens.** DEC-144 item 3 keeps unconverted statements running with no scope, and none of the existing use cases or integration tests binds one (TEN-003 builds the fixtures), so `Neo4jUnitOfWork` opens with `unit_scope` None when nothing is bound. On such a unit an unconverted statement runs with no `tenant` parameter and a converted statement raises `TenantNotBound` before any Cypher is sent. This is the same exemption as DEC-144 item 3, carried onto the unit; it is not a new one. `scope_for_new_unit()` returns `current_scope_or_none()` and is the one place TEN-030 will add the refusal.
4. **The claim.** A second context variable `_UNIT` in `application/tenancy.py` holds the unit that is open in this context. `claim_unit(owner)` raises `UnitOfWorkAlreadyOpen` (fixed message, no argument) when it is set; `release_unit(owner)` clears it only for the owner. `Neo4jUnitOfWork.__aenter__` takes the claim after the used-once check and before it opens a session; it gives it back on `commit()` (after the commit), on a refused commit (after the rollback), on `rollback()`, on leaving the block, and when opening the session or beginning the transaction fails. It is a context variable, so a task started inside an open unit copies the claim and is refused, and sibling tasks started from a context with none each claim their own (the rules of lesson L151, shown by tests). A unit is entered and left in the same task; left in another one, its claim stays in the context that entered it. The architecture test now counts two context variables in the kernel and allows `_UNIT`, `claim_unit` and `release_unit` to be referenced only in the kernel and `unit_of_work.py`.
5. **Other transaction openers read the scope once, before the session opens.** `DriverIdempotencyStore.get` and `put`, `ensure_audit_head` and the new `Neo4jConnection.run_scoped_read` and `run_scoped_write` call `scope_for_new_unit()` outside the managed-transaction callback and wrap the transaction as a `ScopedTx` inside it, so a retry of the callback (the driver may run it more than once) uses the same scope. `run_read` and `run_write` still hand out a raw transaction (the health ping uses them). The scoped helpers claim no unit slot: workers run them outside a unit of work. `Neo4jIdempotencyStore`'s constructor keeps its annotation; `DriverIdempotencyStore` wraps with `cast`. No statement text changed.
6. **`unit_scope`.** A read-only property on `Neo4jUnitOfWork` and on the `UnitOfWork` Protocol, typed `ScopeId | None`. It raises `RuntimeError("the unit of work is not open; use it inside 'async with'")` before the unit is entered and stays readable after it finishes. It is not called `scope` because `uow.scope` is the scope list repository.
7. **Registry rule.** `QueryRegistry._check` refuses a statement whose id is on `GLOBAL_QUERY_IDS` unless it is registered with `scope="global"` (`ValueError` naming the id), whether or not its text mentions `$tenant`. TEN-001 already registered the ping as global and run it through `run_global_query`, so `health.py` needed no change; the test of that was added here. `migrations.py` is unchanged and still runs on the raw global path; a test shows `apply_migrations` and `neo4j_health` work with nothing bound and with a tenant bound.
8. **What this does not do.** No statement is stamped or converted; no existing statement is isolated by this task. A raw transaction is still accepted and takes the ambient scope. A unit opened with nothing bound still runs unconverted statements with no `tenant`. A repository that is handed a `ScopedTx` and calls `tx.run` would fail, but the architecture test already forbids that call outside `driver.py`. Test files that nest two units of work were looked for and are listed in the evidence.

## DEC-145 $board and $confirm-capacity: choices the prompt leaves open (R05)
Status: accepted (builder build-R05, under DEC-133; no owner gate) · Numbered DEC-145: renumbered at the round 2 merge with main, where TEN-001's registry seam had already taken the previous number. Affects: R05 (`adapters/inbound/fhir_rest/routes_board.py`, `mapping.py`, `app.py`), `CapacityRepository.get`, R04 and R07 to R09 (they add to `mapping.py` and route beside `$board`), W05 (the parser of `$board`).

1. **Who confirms, and as what source.** The prompt allows `reporter` (own service) and `lead`. `ConfirmCapacity` reuses `check_may_report` (DEC-112 item 5), which lets a lead report only as `advisor-phoned`. The route therefore passes `ReportSource.APP` for a reporter and `ReportSource.ADVISOR_PHONED` for a lead. The service-scope check (`ensure_can_act_for`) runs first, so a reporter for another service, and an unknown id sent by a reporter, get the same 404 body.
2. **The body is not read.** The service comes from the path and the actor from the signed token; nothing in the body is used, and no tenant is read from anywhere (DEC-130). The R01 middleware still requires a JSON content type on a POST, so a client sends `application/fhir+json` with an empty `Parameters` (or any JSON); the body is only hashed for the replay guard.
3. **Empty comment is omitted.** FHIR has no empty string, and a `valueString` of `""` does not validate. A board entry whose `comment` is empty has no `comment` part, as a null time has no part. W05 reads an absent part as an empty comment.
4. **Refusals are 409 `business-rule`.** `ConfirmLimitReached` (two confirmations in a row) and `NothingToConfirm` (no figure on record) are both answered `Send the number for <service name>.`, with the name of the service the actor just acted for, read from the stored profile. This is the one diagnostic with dynamic text; it is organisation data, never request text or patient data. Nothing is written, and the refusal is not cached, so it can be retried once a number is sent.
5. **Replay.** The R03 cache name is `confirm-capacity:<service id>:<key>` (under `idem:<sub>:`), not the bare key, because the cache digest covers only the body: one key sent for two services would otherwise be answered from the first service's response, and a reporter would believe the second was confirmed. The use case receives the raw key, so after the cache is lost the durable store refuses the same key for another service as a Conflict (test `test_one_key_cannot_confirm_two_services`).
6. **Port addition.** `CapacityRepository.get(report_id) -> CapacityReport | None` (P01 files `ports/outbound/repositories.py` and `graph_store/capacity_repository.py`, no new query: it reads the Observation through `ResourceStore`). The route reads back the report the use case wrote, by the id in the result, instead of `latest()`, which could return a newer report written by someone else. Recorded in `contracts.md` Additions.
7. **`mapping.py` is created here.** Its prompt owner is R04, which is not delivered. R05 needs two pure functions in it, `board_to_parameters` and `report_to_fhir`. `report_to_fhir` produces valid R4B (`focus` is a list), unlike the graph store's own form, so it is separate from `capacity_repository.report_to_fhir` on purpose. R04 must add to the file, not replace it, and may reuse `report_to_fhir` for `POST /fhir/Observation`.
8. **Wiring.** `ApiContainer` gains `ids` (the composition root already has it). `create_app` includes `routes_board.router` after the events router and before any `HealthcareService/{id}` route (R04 must register after it, or `{id}` would take `$board`). `ConfirmCapacity`'s early replay read goes through a small store on the application's unit of work, so the inbound adapter imports no outbound adapter.
9. **Tenancy amendment, not built (transitional rule).** The prompt's amendment asks `$board` for the tenant display name and `scope=network`. There is no tenant in the model yet (no Tenant node, no tenant on `Actor`), and networks come after M1 (DEC-143 item 1). Both wait for the API and sign-in bundle; the cross-scope reads they need are written in `docs/delivery/evidence/R05.md`.


## DEC-147 The harness binds a tenant per run: identity, fixtures, guard and tenant-prefixed ids (TEN-003, refines DEC-144)
Status: accepted · Affects: `backend/tests/conftest.py`, `backend/tests/support/tenancy.py`, `backend/tests/support/uow.py`, `backend/src/diucs/adapters/testing/fixed_ids.py`, `backend/pyproject.toml` (one marker), and every integration test, which now runs with a tenant bound. Decided by the B1a builder under DEC-133 where the TEN-003 prompt left a point open or assumed the withdrawn warn mode (DEC-142 item 7, DEC-144 item 8). F10b's protection (the harness lock, the memory limits, the owner-labelled reaper, the readiness probe and the per-test time limit) is untouched: `neo4j.py`, `harness_lock.py`, `proc.py` and `watchdog.py` are not edited.

1. **No mode.** The prompt says the bound tenant is "passed along in warn mode". There is no mode: an unconverted statement receives the bound `tenant` unused (DEC-144 item 3) and a converted one uses it. The conftest does not set or restore anything about enforcement; turning enforcement on is TEN-030 and nothing here moves it.
2. **The run identity.** `RunIdentity(pid, started, nonce)` as the prompt says; the ids are `tt-<token>`, `tt-<token>-b` and `net-<token>` with `<token>` = `<pid base 36>-<start base 36>-<nonce>`. It validates its fields: a pid from 0 to 2**22 and a start from 0 to 2**36 - 1 (the largest values that keep every id within 31 characters: the longest id is 23 characters) and a nonce of four lower-case hexadecimal characters, else `ValueError`. `parse_run_id` returns an identity only when the value is exactly what that identity writes (so `tt-039u-...`, which decodes but is not written that way, is `None`), which makes it an exact inverse for the orphan sweeper of TEN-033.
3. **The binder and the guard.** `bound_tenant` is a synchronous yield fixture over `bind_tenant` (lesson L151). The hook that adds it to every integration item puts it first in the item's fixture closure, so that every other fixture, sync or async, is set up with the tenant bound (an `append` would set up a seed fixture before the binding; a test shows an async fixture sees it). The guard wraps the test function itself, set as `item.obj` before pytest-asyncio wraps it, because a teardown fixture runs outside the test's task and cannot see a binding the body left behind. `guard_async_test` checks after the body and only when it did not raise; `guard_sync_test` does the same for a plain test, in the call phase, so a leak in a sync test is a failure of that test, not an error at teardown. A teardown is not used for either. The prompt names only `guard_async_test`; the sync guard and `guard_test` (which picks one) are additions.
4. **`no_tenant` marker (an addition).** The existing integration tests of TEN-001 (`test_driver_tenancy.py`) prove that an unbound call is refused, which needs nothing bound. A test marked `@pytest.mark.no_tenant` is not given `bound_tenant` and is guarded to leave nothing bound (`assert_bound_to(None)`). The marker is registered in `pyproject.toml`. It is used only where a test needs an unbound context; the list is in the evidence.
5. **`as_tenant`, `in_tenant`, `tenant_uow_factory` take a tenant, not a network.** A network `ScopeId`, or a string that is not a tenant id (including `net-...`), raises `ScopeInvalid`: the network fixtures are TEN-060's. `in_tenant` checks its tenant when the decorator is built. `tenant_uow_factory(driver, tenant, clock=None, *, factories=None)` takes an optional `factories` mapping (an addition, so a test can add a repository to the default ones as `neo4j_uow_factory` allows); with none it uses `DEFAULT_FACTORIES`.
6. **Environment.** `pytest_configure` exports `DIUCS_TEST_TENANT`, `DIUCS_TEST_OTHER_TENANT` and `DIUCS_TEST_NETWORK`. A sandbox session run in-process by the smoke and hook tests would overwrite them in the parent; the hook tests set them through `monkeypatch` first so the real values come back, and run the sandbox in an empty context so nothing it leaves bound reaches the outer session.
7. **Unchanged on purpose.** `SequentialIds()` and `SequentialIds(prefix=...)` and the default of `build_for_tests` still produce `id-1` (a test pins it); `SequentialIds.scoped(scope, prefix="id")` makes `<scope>-<prefix>-<n>` and validates the scope with `ScopeId.parse` (a network id is allowed). The wipe is still the whole database, `count_nodes` and `node_props` are still global, and the container is still one per run.
8. **What this does not do.** No tenant-scoped wipe, no attached mode, no shared instance, no parallel session (TEN-033 to TEN-035). The run tenant is bound but no existing statement is stamped, so nothing about the existing statements is isolated by it; the first converted statements (TEN-009 onwards) will run in it, and the first unconverted-statement mistake shows in a two-tenant test then. The cross-test leak of a binding that a test sets in a place the guard cannot see (a thread, a task that outlives the test) is not caught.

## DEC-150 The scenario, the generated two-tenant test and the starter set as built (TEN-004, refines DEC-142 items 5 and 10)
Status: accepted · Affects: `credential_store.py`, `audit_neo4j/outbox.py`, `idempotency_store.py`, `graph_store/unconverted.py` (eight ids leave the list), `docs/delivery/known-issues.md`, `adapters/outbound/graph_store/scenario.py` (new), `queries.py` (`Scenario` moves there; `QueryRegistry.template`), `backend/tests/support/tenant_seed.py`, `query_probes.py`, `predicate_removal.py` (new), `backend/tests/integration/graph_store/test_tenant_isolation.py`, `backend/tests/unit/adapters/outbound/graph_store/test_scenario.py` and `test_tenant_ratchet.py`, `.importlinter` (one ignored import). Decided by the TEN-004 builder under DEC-133 where DEC-142, the prompt and its amendment left a point open. The prompt's reader-based parts (probe table, rule codes, `is_unscoped`, warn mode) and its Cypher-in-tests lint with a baseline (`test_test_cypher.py`) are not built: the amendment withdrew the reader, and the lint is a separate control of the 115 test literals that no acceptance item of the amended task needs.

1. **A scenario is data, not Cypher.** `Scenario(description, seed, params, lookup, expect, insert)`. The seed is a `TenantSeed` of `SeedNode(ref, labels, props)` and `SeedRel(start, type, end)`: one world, built for each tenant, so every key is the same in both. The seed never holds `tenantId` or `seedMarker` (validation refuses it); the seeder stamps both on every node it creates and refuses a graph that holds an unstamped node afterwards. `Scenario("text")` with nothing else still builds, because the registry tests of TEN-001 and TEN-002 use that form; `validate_scenario` refuses it (no nodes).
2. **Two value types say how a value differs between the tenants.** `Marked("note")` is `note-A` for tenant A and `note-B` for tenant B, so a row that came from the wrong tenant looks different. `Unique("res-1", "resource_id")` is `res-1` for A and `res-1-B` for B and names a single-property unique constraint of the production schema that stops two tenants sharing the value until TEN-005 (DEC-130 item 7). The constraint must be in `SINGLE_PROPERTY_UNIQUE` (a table the integration test compares with `SHOW CONSTRAINTS`, so it loses an entry in the change that drops one), and a `Unique` in a seed must sit on the label and property of its constraint. Nothing is dropped or altered here: the test runs on the shared harness schema.
3. **The collision rule is `validate_scenario`.** The keys must collide across the tenants: every `lookup` parameter (the ones that pick the node the statement works on) is either a plain value the seed holds, which both tenants then share, or a `Unique` the seed holds; one that the seed does not hold, or a `Marked` one, is refused. With no `lookup` the seed must hold a plain shared value. The seed must hold at least one `Marked` or `Unique` value, so the tenants can be told apart. `collision_blocked` is true when every lookup is `Unique`. A test pins, per statement, the constraints its scenario leans on (`CONSTRAINTS_LEANED_ON`, `test_tenant_ratchet.py`) and the set of collision-blocked statements; both may only shrink, and TEN-005 empties them.
4. **The runner is `run_isolation`**, over the statements that have a scenario, in two phases (A first, then B first). In a phase: wipe, seed the first tenant alone, run the statement as that tenant on a `ScopedTx` in an explicit transaction that is rolled back, and keep the rows and the snapshot of the tenant's data (the baseline: the statement with the other tenant absent); the baseline must equal the scenario (`unexpected-rows`, `unexpected-effect`). Then seed the second tenant and run again: the rows (`rows-differ`), the first tenant's data afterwards (`own-state-differs`) and the second tenant's data before and after the statement in the same transaction (`other-tenant-changed`) must equal what they were, no node may lack a tenant except the registry labels (`unstamped-node`) and no relationship may join two tenants (`cross-scope-relationship`). Where the scenario has a value that differs per tenant, a third run uses the other tenant's rendering (the foreign-id run): rows, own data and the other tenant's data must equal what that run gave with the other tenant absent (`foreign-id-effect`). A statement that raises is `probe-error` with the exception class name only. A finding names the statement, the phase, the kind and counts or label names, never a value, a Cypher text or a tenant id. Times a statement stamps itself (`datetime()`) are masked when two runs are compared, and only then.
5. **`expect` is the effect for the caller.** `rows` (each a subset of the actual row, nested maps too, as a multiset), `nodes` and `rels` (net change per label and per relationship type; what is not named must not change), `after` and `gone` (nodes the caller's data must hold or no longer hold, by labels and properties). With none of `after`, `gone`, `nodes`, `rels` the statement must change nothing, so a read that writes fails.
6. **The universe** is `production_statements()`: every tenant statement of the module registry by base id, one entry per template (the instance its scenario's `insert` names, else a stand-in), without global statements, `Q-TEST-` ids and run-time instances. `scenario_gaps` and `ratchet_problems` read it: a statement with no scenario and not on the ratchet fails the test; the ratchet may hold only ids of `INITIAL_UNCONVERTED` (the 126 ids when TEN-004 was built, a literal in `tests/support/query_probes.py`, never edited again); a converted statement left on the list, a stale entry and a statement with neither conversion nor a place on the list are problems. The registry's own check (`ratchet_ids() == UNCONVERTED`) cannot see an id added to the list and to the code together; the initial list does.
7. **The predicate-removal check** is `mutant_of(query, occurrence=None)`: a copy in a private registry (its id on that registry's ratchet, so it can register) with every `tenantId: $tenant` map entry taken out, or one. The integration test requires the generated test to fail for the copy of every converted statement with every occurrence removed, and to pass for the original. Removing one occurrence of two can leave a statement that is still isolated, because a relationship never joins two tenants (the invariant) and the other stamp already fixes the tenant; those mutants are listed in the evidence, not asserted.
8. **Template scenarios.** `Scenario.insert` carries the label chain a template is run with; no template is converted here. `QueryRegistry.template(id)` is a read accessor for the tool that lists every statement.
9. **Limits, stated.** A scenario is as strong as its seed and its expectation: a leak the seed cannot provoke (a join with no data to join) is not found, which is why the leaky twins of the late addition (a variable re-bound after `WITH`, a subquery with its own variable, `UNION`, `OR true`, a stamp in a nested map, a negated predicate) are run on every change of the runner. A statement whose every lookup is `Unique` is proved only by the foreign-id run and the unstamped-node invariant until TEN-005. The seeder is the one place that creates nodes with raw Cypher outside `run_query` (test code).
The starter set (items 10 to 15): the eight statements converted to prove the mechanism on real code, and why the milestone slice's capacity and service statements are not among them.
10. **A statement can be converted only when no unconverted writer creates the nodes it matches.** `tenantId: $tenant` on a matched node needs the node to carry the tenant, and every node today is written by a statement that does not stamp it (the resource store writes every `Resource` node, the mapper every fragment).
