A converted statement that matches such a node finds nothing: `Q-CAP-LOCK-SERVICE` and `Q-SVC-LIST-ACTIVE` match a service that every test seeds through `ResourceStore`, `Q-CAP-TRAILING-CONFIRMS` and `Q-CAP-LATEST-CONFIRMED-AT` match the observation the report use case writes through it in the same unit, `Q-REG-DRAFT-*` and `Q-CASE-SET-POSTCODE` run inside a `save` that writes the Task or the Patient first. So the capacity lock, the trailing-confirms and latest-confirmed-at lookups and the service reads that the milestone slice uses first are not converted here; they wait for TEN-009 (the resource store stamps what it writes). The prompt's "for example" list is not a requirement.
11. **What is converted (eight statements).** Credentials: `Q-CRED-PRACTITIONER-EXISTS` (read), `Q-CRED-SET` (MERGE of a relationship and a node), `Q-CRED-GET` (a read that takes the write lock first), `Q-CRED-SAVE-STATE` (update), `Q-CRED-CLEAR` (delete): each matches a practitioner, which the resource store writes, but only as an anchor that the credential tests and every use of the store seed in a step of their own; the credential and its relationship are written by `Q-CRED-SET`. Outbox: `Q-OUTBOX-ADD` (create) and `Q-OUTBOX-PENDING` (read, no key, filtered by status), whose node only `Q-OUTBOX-ADD` creates; `Q-OUTBOX-LOCK`, `-PUBLISHED` and `-FAILED` match by id and still work on a stamped node, so they stay on the ratchet (TEN-016). Consumer idempotency: `Q-PROCESSED-MARK` (MERGE). Kinds covered: read, create, MERGE (two), update, delete, relationship traversal, a lock-then-read. This is eight, the top of the prompt's range.
12. **The practitioner is stamped by the test that seeds it** (`test_credential_store.py` stamps what `Neo4jPersonRepository` wrote with the run tenant), because the resource store does not stamp yet. This is the transitional state, not a design: TEN-009 makes the writer stamp, and the seed in the test goes then.
13. **A converted statement refuses to run with no tenant bound** (DEC-144 item 1). The API, worker and consumer edges bind a tenant in the tasks that own them (TEN-018 onwards, TEN-025); until then the converted statements run only where a tenant is bound (every integration test, the Twilio webhook of I07). No production path calls the credential store yet. The outbox relay and the processed-message marker are called by consumers that bind no tenant yet: the relay reads the pending messages of the bound tenant only, so a relay that serves every tenant must bind each in turn (TEN-016, TEN-025).
14. **Colliding keys, stated per statement.** The practitioner key collides (a non-unique property: the two tenants have the same `key`, different `id`s, because `resource_id` is unique), so `Q-CRED-PRACTITIONER-EXISTS`, `-SET` and `-CLEAR` are proved with a colliding lookup. `Q-CRED-GET` and `-SAVE-STATE` also look the credential up by its device id, which `credential_device_id` makes unique, so that half is proved by the foreign-id run. `Q-OUTBOX-ADD` and `Q-PROCESSED-MARK` create a node under a key no tenant holds, and the message id and the marker key are unique (`outbox_message_id`, `processed_key`): their proof is the unstamped-node invariant and the other tenant staying unchanged. `Q-OUTBOX-PENDING` has no key and shares its status filter. None of the eight is collision-blocked as a whole. The conversion bundles for statements called only by a unique key (`Q-OUTBOX-LOCK`, a service by `id`) cannot be proved with colliding keys until TEN-005.
15. **Two effects of single-property uniqueness, not changed here.** A second tenant that writes a device id, a message id or a processed-marker key that another tenant already holds meets the unique constraint, so `Q-CRED-SET` answers `Conflict("device already has a PIN credential")` and `Neo4jOutbox.add` answers `Conflict`, which tells the second tenant that the value exists. Both go with the composite keys (TEN-005).

## DEC-151 The wall board page: clock, organisation name, failure behaviour and the choices the prompt leaves open (W05)
Status: accepted (builder build-W05, under DEC-133; no owner gate) · Numbered DEC-151: main's top decision is DEC-149 and other branches hold DEC-148 and DEC-150. Affects: W05 (`lib/board/*`, `components/board/*`, `app/(wall)/screen/page.tsx`, `app/api/bff/board/route.ts`), W06, W07, W08 and the API and sign-in tenancy bundle (the `organisation` part).

1. **The page's "now" is the back end's `generatedAt`.** The header time, the header date and every age under a tile are measured from it, not from the browser or the web server clock. The time on screen is therefore the time the board was read, so a board that stops refreshing shows an old time instead of a fresh-looking one, and the ages always agree with the status the back end worked out at that instant. Until W06 adds live updates the page is a snapshot (hazard H-33).
2. **Tenancy: one board, the session's own (DEC-130, DEC-132, DEC-143).** `WallScreenPage` has no props and `GET /api/bff/board` reads nothing from the URL, query string, headers or body. The only thing that says whose board it is, is the back-end token in the sealed session. The network board and the scope toggle are not built (they come after M1 with the network tasks). Tests: a screen paired to organisation A never shows B's names or ids, a query string or `X-Tenant-Id` header is ignored, and the request to the back end carries the bearer token only (`page.test.tsx`, `route.test.ts`, `read.test.ts`, `e2e/board.spec.ts`).
3. **Organisation name.** R05 did not build the tenant display name (DEC-145 item 9). The parser reads an optional top-level `organisation` part (`valueString`) into `Board.organisation`; the header is `<organisation> urgent care capacity`, or `Urgent care capacity` when there is none, so no area name is a constant (DEC-014 item 5). The back end must emit the bound tenant's display name as that part; it is listed in `docs/spec/contracts.md` Additions and in the evidence's cross-scope reads. "Dorset" appears only in test fixtures.
4. **Who may read.** The BFF route and the page allow `wall`, `advisor`, `lead` and `exec` (the prompt), though the back end also allows `clinical` and `owner`. Any other role, or no session, goes to `/screen/pair` and the back end is not called. A back-end 401, 403 or 404 also goes to `/screen/pair` (the screen is not paired or its token is refused).
5. **Fail closed.** `parseBoardParameters` throws `BoardParseError` for a status it does not know, a missing required part, a part of the wrong type, a negative or non-integer count, a repeated part or a non-Parameters body; unknown parts are ignored. `readBoard` turns that into a 502 `FhirError` with fixed text. If the page cannot read the board for any reason other than item 4, it shows `BoardUnavailable` (a role=alert with "Board unavailable", "No figures can be shown. Use the whiteboard and phone process.", and the footer line) and no figure. This is not W06's Last known (which keeps old figures); it only avoids a blank or error page on a wall. An empty board shows "No services are listed. Use the whiteboard and phone process."
6. **Figures the prompt leaves open.** A not-updated tile always shows "?", "Phone to confirm" and "Used ? of N" whatever figure the back end holds. For accepting, limited and not accepting the big number is `effectiveAvailable`, or `established - used` (not below 0) when the service reports occupancy and has no free count, or "?" with "Phone to confirm" when neither is known. The bar fill is `used / established`, kept between 0 and 100, to one decimal place; unknown `used` fills 0. A report-less service reads "No figure reported yet"; a not-updated one with no report reads "No figure reported yet · was due HH:MM". "Not updated for X" counts from `reportedAt`. A next due time two or more London days away reads "next due 09:00 Monday". The next-due part is left out when the back end sends none. `categoryLabel` covers all eight category codes (the five in the prompt, plus "Urgent treatment", "Mental health crisis" and "Other") and turns an unknown code into plain words.
7. **Statuses that belong to W07, shown safely.** The back end can send over-capacity and out-of-hours, so the tile has a minimal, safe rendering: Over capacity shows the word and shape, `+N` (from `overBy`), the back end's unit label and a full hatched bar; Out of hours shows the word and shape, no number, no bar and "Outside opening hours". Pending bookings, the pressure banner, opening times and other W07 detail are not shown.
8. **Colours and the hatch.** Colours with no token (`#E9EDEF`, the two hatch greys, `#7A3500`, `#4B1C69`, the two bar hatch lights) are declared once as `--tile-*` values at the top of `Tile.module.css`, from the "Main" artboard, because `tokens.test.ts` pins the token set (as DEC-122 item 1). The design system says no gradients on the wall board; the prompt and artboard require a hatched not-updated tile and bar, which are a pattern that carries status (with the dashed border, "?" and the word), not decoration, so the hatch is built with `repeating-linear-gradient`. A test reads the shipped colours and checks WCAG AA contrast on each surface.
9. **Changes outside the prompt's file list.** `components/ui/StatusBadge.tsx` exports `ShapeIcon`, `STATUS_SHAPES` and `Shape` (additive, so the legend uses the same shapes). `app/tokens.css` gains `body { margin: 0 }` (the wall fills the screen). `test/setup.ts` installs the default MSW handlers before the first test (they were installed only after the first `afterEach`, so the first request of a file saw none). `e2e/smoke.spec.ts` is replaced by `e2e/board.spec.ts`. The browser tests run the dev server against a stand-in back end (`e2e/support/backend.ts`, started by `e2e/global-setup.ts`) and seal the session cookie with `jose` in `e2e/support/session.ts`, because Playwright cannot load `lib/auth/session.ts` (it imports `next/headers`).
10. **Not built.** Live updates, Last known (W06); pending, the pressure banner and the full over capacity and out of hours tiles (W07); pairing and the `/screen/pair` page (W08); the network variant (after M1).

## DEC-152 Wall board live updates and the Last known rule (W06)
Status: accepted (builder build-W06, under DEC-133; no owner gate) · Numbered DEC-152: main's top decision is DEC-151, other branches hold DEC-148 and DEC-150. Affects: W06 (`lib/board/{liveness,boardJson,client,clock,useBoardFeed}.ts`, `components/board/{LiveBoard,LastKnownBanner}.tsx`, `Board.tsx`, `Tile.tsx`), W07, W08, W20, hazards H-33 and H-20, DEC-151 items 1 and 5.

1. **The event names.** R06 sends `event: board` (DEC-111 item 2), not the prompt's `board.updated`, and never sends a booking event. The page refreshes on `board` and on `board.updated`, and ignores `booking.updated` (the wall shows capacity only; the advisor page, W20, will use it). The relay forwards all three names (DEC-153).
2. **An event only says "something changed".** The page never patches a board from an event. It refetches the WHOLE board and replaces the whole object, so a missed event is harmless and a stream that reconnects replaces everything at once, never merging. It refetches when the stream opens (the first time and after every reconnect), on a `board` event, and every 60 seconds. Requests are not stacked: a burst of events becomes one request now and at most one more.
3. **The stale rule (the spec gives no limit for the age of `generatedAt`; this is the one built).** The board is shown as Last known when any of these holds, and is current otherwise:
   - **offline**: the live stream has been down for more than 10 seconds (observability.md, "Stream gap") and the last refetch failed (the W06 prompt). The offline time is the first failure.
   - **unreadable**: an answer could not be read (not JSON, a status it does not know, a negative or fractional count, a missing part, a BFF 502) or a board is dated more than 60 seconds in the future. This is at once, whatever the stream does. The last good board stays on screen, with Last known on it, never blank.
   - **old**: the board's `generatedAt` is more than 3 minutes older than the back end's clock now (`STALE_AFTER_MS = 180000`, [ASSUMED]). Reason: the back end caches a board for 60 seconds (`BOARD_CACHE_TTL`) and the page polls every 60, so a healthy board is at most about 2 minutes old just before the next poll; 3 minutes allows one missed poll and no more. This rule is independent of the stream because the browser's EventSource cannot see keep-alive comments, so a stream that is open but dead looks healthy, and the stream reaches the back end through the same BFF as the poll.
   Every limit is a named constant in `lib/board/liveness.ts` (`POLL_INTERVAL_MS`, `STREAM_GAP_MS`, `STREAM_RETRY_MS`, `STALE_AFTER_MS`, `MAX_FUTURE_SKEW_MS`, `FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS`). A request that does not answer in 15 seconds is abandoned and counted as a failure, so a hung connection cannot stop the poll.
4. **The clock.** The age is measured on the back end's clock, not the wall computer's: the browser takes the offset (back end minus browser) from the `Date` header of every successful answer, and at first paint from the web server's clock (the page passes `serverTime`). A wall computer whose clock is an hour out neither shows an old board as fresh nor a fresh one as old. When an answer has no usable `Date` header the last offset is kept. The page checks the clock every second, so a board that stops refreshing for any reason (a request that hangs, a machine that slept) turns to Last known on the next second. The age and every time are instants; London local time is only for display, so the 2026-03-29 and 2026-10-25 clock changes cannot make an age negative or an hour out (tested).
5. **Order.** A board with an older `generatedAt` than the one held never replaces it (equal is accepted). A board dated in the future is refused (it would otherwise pin the wall on a time no later board can beat).
6. **What Last known looks like.** A banner (`role="alert"`, the only announcement) with "Tool offline since HH:MM" (the first failure, or the board's own time when there was none) and "Figures are the last known and may be wrong. Use the whiteboard and phone process.", exactly the BoardDown artboard. Outside the alert, so it is not announced again every minute, a line "Board last refreshed HH:MM · N min ago" (the age counts up). Every tile gets the badge "Last known" with the dashed shape, the not-updated hatch and border, "Last known HH:MM · N min ago" (the age is added to the artboard's meta, because a time alone does not say which day), and "Do not rely on this. Phone to confirm." in bold. The number stays as last reported. Two safe differences from "the number stays": a service that was Not updated stays "?" (its figure was already unknown), and an Out of hours claim is not repeated (opening hours may have changed since), so that tile shows "?" and "Phone to confirm". The tile's comment is hidden. The header time stays the time the board was built; the ages under the tiles count from the back end clock now.
7. **A state the prompt does not name: reconnecting.** When the stream has been down for more than 10 seconds but the board is still being refreshed, a quiet line in words, "Live updates paused. This board refreshes every minute.", shows under the header. It is not an alert and the tiles stay current.
8. **Announcements.** The prompt says to announce nothing on every refresh and design-system.md 5.4 item 4 says a polite live region announces each status change. The prompt is followed (it is the task's own statement; a wall is a display); no status-change announcement is built. Recorded for the v1.1 pass.
9. **A screen the back end stops knowing.** A refetch refused with 401, 403 or 404 shows Last known and, once, sends the browser to `/screen/pair` (as DEC-151 item 4 does at first load). The pairing page is W08, so until then that address is a 404.
10. **The page.** `WallScreenPage` still takes no input. It renders `LiveBoard` with the board it read and the web server's clock (`lib/board/clock.ts`, so a Server Component does not call `Date.now` itself). `Board` and `Tile` gained optional props (`lastKnown`, `streamPaused`); with none they render exactly as in W05. The first paint already says Last known for a board that is old when served.
11. **Tenancy (DEC-130, DEC-132, DEC-143).** One board, the session's own. Nothing in the browser sends, chooses or reads a tenant: `/api/bff/board` and `/api/bff/events` take no query, header or route segment, and the back-end token in the sealed session is the only identity. The network board and its stream are not built (after M1); the cross-scope reads they need are in the evidence.
12. **Files outside the prompt's list.** `lib/fhir/client.ts` (`fhirStream`, and the failure outcome shared with `fhirFetch`), `lib/board/parse.ts` (`isStatus` exported), `lib/board/{liveness,boardJson,client,clock,useBoardFeed}.ts`, `components/board/LastKnownBanner.{tsx,module.css}`, `app/(wall)/screen/page.tsx`, test and browser-test support (`test/fakeStream.ts`, `test/sse.ts`, `e2e/support/backend.ts`, `e2e/live.spec.ts`), and `docs/spec/contracts.md`.
13. **Known limits.** The page cannot tell an open stream from a dead one (EventSource hides keep-alives); the poll and the age rule cover it, so a dead stream with a working poll shows no warning, because the board is still being refreshed. The `Date` header has one-second resolution. A wall whose session expires shows Last known after 3 minutes at the latest and then goes to the pairing page.

## DEC-153 The BFF event relay (W06)
Status: accepted (builder build-W06, under DEC-133; no owner gate) · Affects: `app/api/bff/events/route.ts`, `lib/bff/events.ts`, `lib/fhir/client.ts` (`fhirStream`), W20.

1. **Who and what.** `GET /api/bff/events` is allowed for `wall`, `advisor`, `lead` and `exec` (the same as the board route, DEC-151 item 4), takes no input, and opens the back end's `GET /events` with the session's back-end token and nothing else. A back-end 401, 403 or 404 passes through with the same status; an unreachable back end is a 503; a 200 that is not `text/event-stream` is a 502. Nothing is relayed before that check.
2. **What it forwards.** Only events named `board`, `board.updated` and `booking.updated`, and in each only `serviceId` (a FHIR id, 1 to 64 of `A-Za-z0-9.-`), `kind` (`capacity`, `status`, `booking`) and `changedAt` (a UTC instant); any other field, and any value of the wrong shape, is dropped. An allowed event whose data cannot be read is still forwarded with `{}`, so the page still refreshes. Comments are replaced by a fixed `: keep-alive`; `id` and `retry` lines are never forwarded (they could change how the browser reconnects). A frame over 16 KiB ends the stream, so the browser reconnects.
3. **Streaming.** Headers `Content-Type: text/event-stream; charset=utf-8`, `Cache-Control: no-store, no-transform` (so the Next.js compression does not buffer it) and `X-Accel-Buffering: no`. Each event is sent as soon as its frame is complete. The back end connection is closed when the browser disconnects (the body is cancelled or the request is aborted), and the browser stream ends when the back end ends, so the browser reconnects and the page refetches the whole board.
4. **Not built.** The network stream (the relay opening the network scope's stream when the board is in network scope, from the W06 tenancy amendment): the network scope and its stream do not exist until after M1. It is a cross-scope read, listed in the evidence.


## DEC-154 A link token is 23 letters, so no random token can match the privacy guard (fix, outside the ledger)
Status: accepted (lead developer, under DEC-133; no ledger task) · Numbered DEC-154: the highest number on `main` and on every local branch and worktree (TEN-004, U05, W06) was DEC-151 when this was written (`git show <branch>:docs/delivery/decisions.md | grep '^## DEC-'`); renumber if another branch has since taken it. Affects: `domain/rules/tokens.py`, `application/ports/outbound/tokens.py`, U03 (`SendPrompts`), L01 and L02 (the link pages, planned), docs/spec/domain-rules.md section 7 and contracts.md, the D26 and P10 prompts. Amends DEC-057, DEC-115 and DEC-116 only where they say the token is 22 characters of base64url.

**The defect.** The link token was 128 bits as 22 characters of base64url. `render` runs the D17 guard over every field, including `link`. In base64url "-" is a word boundary for the postcode pattern and the digits can complete a postcode or an NHS number, so a random token sometimes held a patient-data shape (544 of 300000 for the seeded run, about 0.18 percent; the verifier saw 556). `render` then raised `ValidationFailed` ("link: a patient-data shape is not allowed"), `SendPrompts` failed, no capacity prompt was sent, the service went overdue and an alert could fire. It was also the cause of one flaky U03 integration test.

1. **The fix is at the source: option (b), a token that cannot match.** `new_link_token` writes the 128 random bits as one number in base 52 with exactly 23 letters (`LINK_TOKEN_ALPHABET` = A to Z, a to z; `LINK_TOKEN_LENGTH` = 23). No digit, no "-", no "_", no space. The guard's three patterns each need something such a token does not have: the postcode pattern needs a digit and a word boundary on both sides (a letters-only word of 23 has no boundary inside, and no postcode is that long); the NHS-number pattern needs ten digits; the titled-name pattern needs whitespace. This holds for every possible token, so it is proved from the patterns, not sampled. The tests also sample 300000 seeded tokens and find none.
2. **Entropy is not reduced.** 52 ** 23 is about 2 ** 131.1, which is more than 2 ** 128. A fixed-length base-52 number is one to one, so all 128 bits are kept (the source still asks for 16 bytes) and the token space is 2 ** 128 values. Domain-rules 7 and DEC-115 ask for at least 128 bits. The cost is one more character in the SMS link (23, was 22).
3. **The privacy guard is unchanged and not exempted.** There is no exemption of the link field. Domain-rules section 9 says no message carries a patient name, a full postcode, an NHS number or free text, and names no exemption for a link host or token; DEC-047 item 5 says the guard runs on every value a caller passes, whatever the field. Weakening or skipping it for `link` would also let a future caller put a postcode in a link. The guard is the arbiter, and the token now always passes it.
4. **A bounded regenerate loop (option a) was not needed** and is not added: nothing is rejected, so there is no retry and no new failure mode.
5. **The token source can be injected.** `LinkTokenService(repo, secrets, clock, rng=secrets.token_bytes)`. The default is the system source. A test passes its own bits to force a particular 128 bits through the real `issue` path (the forced-match tests use bits that made a postcode and an NHS-shaped token before the fix).
6. **Old tokens still resolve.** `hash_token` still accepts any URL-safe text (`[A-Za-z0-9_-]+`), so a link already sent (22 characters, base64url) can be resolved and burned. Nothing creates one any more. The link pages (L01, L02) must accept both forms.
7. **Other random identifiers.** The 6-digit pairing code ("482 913", all 1000000 codes checked) and the support code (XXXX-XXXX, 300000 sampled; each half is four characters, and a postcode needs five or more in one word) never match. A UUID4 (the id generator, the SMTP Message-ID) matches the NHS-number shape in about 0.83 percent of draws (ten digits in a row inside the last 12-character group), but no id is passed to `render` today (checked: the only fields are service name, figures, times, `link`, `reply_by`). It is a known issue with a trigger: nobody may put an id in a message field without a test.
8. **No tenancy change.** No Cypher is added or changed. The tenant-pending statements of U03 are unchanged.

Tests: `tests/unit/domain/rules/test_link_token_privacy.py`, `test_link_token_alphabet.py`, `test_tokens.py`; `tests/unit/application/ports/outbound/test_link_token_service_source.py`; `tests/integration/application/test_send_prompts.py` (`test_a_token_that_once_matched_the_privacy_guard_still_sends_the_prompt`). Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/X-link-token-privacy.md`.


## DEC-155 The tenant domain model, setting bounds, approver rule and lifecycle functions as built (TEN-006)
Status: accepted · Affects: `domain/model/tenant.py`, `domain/model/tenant_policy.py`, `domain/rules/tenant_settings.py`, `domain/rules/tenant_lifecycle.py`, TEN-007, TEN-021, TEN-022, TEN-026, TEN-028, TEN-036, TEN-064, TEN-065, TEN-073, TEN-074, `docs/spec/contracts.md` (new section), hazard rows H-T14, H-T15, H-T21. Decided by the TEN-006 builder under DEC-133 where the prompt left a point open. Refines DEC-134 item 14 and DEC-136 items 10 and 13; reopens none of them.

1. **Bounds.** Audit F section 6.1 gives the ranges of nine settings: reply window 15 to 60 minutes, stale warning 1 to 4 hours, correction window 5 to 30, maximum consecutive confirms 1 to 3, group window 5 to 20, services per phone 2 to 6, criteria reminder 7 to 30 days, clinical take timeout 5 to 20 and re-verification 7 to 30 days (a tenant may only shorten it). Audit B's 5 to 120 for the reply window loses to the plan and F (15 to 60). **[ASSUMED] proposals** (audit F gives none): reminder 15 to 90 minutes; phone task 30 to 180; escalation 45 to 360; second daily time 12:00 to 16:00; pressure watch 1 to 50 and high 2 to 100; many-changes window 1 to 30 minutes and count 2 to 10; phoned window 15 to 240; booking reply window 5 to 30 and phone prompt 2 to 15; withdrawal escalation 5 to 30; the alert sets (a non-empty subset of the alert kinds) and the not-updated hours (2 or 4, the choices of `alerts.NOT_UPDATED_HOURS`). Wording maxima 60, 40 and 60 characters are also [ASSUMED]. Every figure is a named `Final` constant in `tenant_policy.py` or `tenant_settings.py`; changing one needs a new decision (DEC-007). The all-minimum policy and the all-maximum policy are both legal (a test), so a range can never make a legal combination impossible.
2. **Withdrawal escalation has no module constant.** The rule book says the shift lead is alerted after 10 minutes without action but no rule module names the figure. The default is the literal 10 minutes in `tenant_policy.py` (`WITHDRAWAL_ESCALATE_DEFAULT`); the golden test pins it. TEN-064 adds the constant to the booking rules as an alias of `DEFAULT_POLICY.withdrawal_escalate_after`.
3. **Approver rule (DEC-032 pattern, stricter than audit F).** A change that touches any `clinical` key needs two approvers, all named, all different, and none of them the author; any other change needs one approver, who may be the author. "Changed" includes a key set back to its default and a key added or removed. The author must be named. An unknown key is refused. The refusals are `NotAllowed` with three fixed messages that name no ref. A bare string, a set, `None` or a number as the approver list is refused. The pure function holds the rule; the use case supplies the people (TEN-026).
4. **Class and scope.** A setting has a safety class (`clinical` or `operational`) and a scope (`tenant`, `network` or `both`). The two pressure thresholds are `both`; the alert defaults are `operational`; every other policy row is `clinical` on `tenant`; the three wording rows are `operational`, `tenant`, `required_for_activation`. A row added later defaults to `clinical` and `tenant` (the two-approver, no-network case) until its task says otherwise.
5. **Renames and fixed lists.** `coordination-phone` replaces audit F's `spoa-phone` (no deployment term in product code, as TEN-022). Suspend reasons are `security-incident`, `information-governance`, `clinical-safety`, `contract` and `operator-request` [ASSUMED], never free text. Organisation types are the five of `ui-screens.md` 2019 to 2023 plus `icb`.
6. **`Tenant` is keyword-only.** The prompt fixes the field order with `country_code` and `time_zone` (both defaulted) before `status` and `created_at` (not), which a positional dataclass cannot hold. `@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)` keeps the order and the names; nobody constructs a tenant positionally. `dataclasses.replace` runs every check again.
7. **Status and timestamps agree both ways.** `offboarding_started_at`, `offboarded_at` and `purged_at` are set if and only if the status is one of those the prompt names (not only "set for"), and the three suspend fields if and only if `SUSPENDED`. So a reopened tenant carries no old offboarding times and a later offboarding starts a new 30-day notice period. A reference (`dsa_ref` and the others) is not empty and has no leading or trailing space; `_` is an allowed character (the prompt's list is letters, digits, space and `._:/-`). A name has no control character (`str.isprintable`). A contact value is at most 254 characters.
8. **Functions beyond the prompt's list.** `reopen_tenant(tenant)` (offboarding or offboarded to active, epoch up, offboarding times cleared; it takes no instant because nothing is recorded) and `activate_tenant(tenant, facts, now)` (provisioning to active only when `activation_blockers` is empty, so the state machine has a function for every legal move and the facts check cannot be skipped by the caller). `resume_tenant` applies only to `SUSPENDED` (the table also lets offboarding move to active, but that is a reopen). `suspend_tenant` and `resume_tenant` raise the epoch; offboarding start and completion do not (prompt rule 8). **Note for TEN-073:** `start_offboarding` from `SUSPENDED` clears the suspend fields, so a `blocked` suspension (a security incident) becomes a read-only offboarding; TEN-073 decides whether that move needs the operator to confirm.
9. **Instants.** A naive, missing or non-datetime `now` or other instant given to a lifecycle function is a `ValueError` (as `london_time.py` and `alerts.py`); a naive field of a `Tenant` or `TenantConfig` is `ValidationFailed` naming the field. A first argument that is not exactly a `Tenant` is `TypeError("a Tenant is required")`. A `waived` flag waives the notice only when it is the value `True`.
10. **Numbers.** `number_assignable(status, quarantine_until, now)` takes `None` for a number that was never assigned (assignable). An `active` number is never assignable. A `retired` or `quarantined` number is assignable from the quarantine end inclusive, and never with no end on record. The quarantine is 90 days from `retired_at` (DEC-136 item 10).
11. **The policy holds whole units.** A duration must be a whole number of its setting's unit (minutes, hours or days) and a time of day a whole minute, so `as_settings` and `policy_from_settings` are exact inverses. Each of the five cross-field comparisons has its own message and names the later field: `reminder_after`, `phone_task_after`, `escalate_after`, `pressure_high_from`, `booking_phone_prompt_after`. The ranges are checked before the cross-field rules. `POLICY_FIELDS` (field, key, shape, unit) lives in `tenant_policy.py` and is the one source for the keys and units; `SettingValue` is defined there and re-exported by `tenant_settings.py` (so the policy can return `as_settings` without a cycle).
12. **Strict types in the registry.** A code set holds plain `str` values, not `AlertKind` members (`as_settings` returns plain text); a `bool` is never an `int`; a `float` is never an int; a time must be naive and whole-minute. Wording values go through the same text check inside `validate_value` and `policy_from_settings`, so a bad wording is refused wherever it enters. `validate_settings` returns the validated values in registry order and keeps a key that equals its default (it is an override until it is removed).
13. **Activation facts fail closed.** A fact that is not exactly `True` is missing. "Email is in use" and "registers services" and "network member" count as true unless they are exactly `False`, so a missing or odd value asks for the dependent fact. Contact kinds, retention classes and references are matched by exact type (a plain string equal to a member does not count) and from a `frozenset` only. A time zone counts only when it is a `str` on the allow-list.
14. **Parity with TEN-001.** TEN-001's pattern is identical, so nothing is adopted or changed. `application/tenancy.py` accepts `platform` (the kernel knows only the pattern and the `net-` prefix, so `ScopeId.tenant("platform")` is valid); the **domain refuses it** in `check_tenant_id` and in `Tenant`. The kernel validator also accepts a `str` subclass (it uses `isinstance`) and the domain does not; the parity test compares plain strings. The use case that creates a tenant must call `check_tenant_id`, not the kernel validator alone.
15. **Test correction after the red commit.** The first red test listed `a_b` as a refused reference; the prompt's allowed list includes `_`, so the test was wrong and now accepts it. Nothing in the source changed for it.
16. **Hazards.** H-T14, H-T15 and H-T21 are added to the hazard log with this task's tests under "Verified by" (control only: the use cases that enforce them are TEN-026, TEN-028 and TEN-065).

## DEC-156 Round 2 of TEN-004: a test name hides no statement, and a scenario must observe something and hold its keys (refines DEC-150 items 3, 4 and 6)
Status: accepted · Affects: `backend/tests/support/query_probes.py`, `adapters/outbound/graph_store/scenario.py` (`Scenario.new`, `_check_new`), the starter scenarios (`credential_store.py`, `audit_neo4j/outbox.py`, `idempotency_store.py`: a `new=` each), `backend/tests/integration/graph_store/test_tenant_isolation.py`, `test_query_probes.py`, `test_scenario.py`. Decided by the TEN-004 round-2 builder under DEC-133 and DEC-141, to close two blocking findings of round 1. Numbered DEC-156: DEC-155 is the TEN-006 branch's.

1. **A `Q-TEST-` id is for a private registry only (B1).** `production_statements` left out every id that starts with `Q-TEST-`, and nothing forbade such an id in the production registry, so a leaky production statement named `Q-TEST-SNEAK` passed every test. The universe now leaves a `Q-TEST-` statement out only for a registry that is not the module `REGISTRY` (the private registries the harness builds for its corpus). `registry_problems(registry)` fails for any `Q-TEST-` id, and any id outside `Q-[A-Z0-9]+(-[A-Z0-9]+)*` (the form the source scan in `test_queries.py` already relies on), in the production registry; `universe_problems(registry, statements)` fails for a registered tenant statement or template that the universe does not hold. Both are tested in `test_query_probes.py` against the real registry and asserted at the head of the generated test.
2. **A scenario must observe something (B2a).** `run_isolation` adds the finding `vacuous-scenario` when the baseline run (the scenario's own tenant, the other absent) returns no rows and changes nothing in the tenant's data. This covers an empty `Expect` with a no-op write and a read that never matches.
3. **A key must be held by the seed, or declared new (B2b).** `Scenario.new` names the parameters that are new data (a value to write, a limit, a time, the key of a node the statement creates). Every other parameter, `lookup` or not, must hold only values the seed holds (text, numbers, `Marked`, `Unique`, looking inside maps and lists; booleans and `None` are not keys). `validate_scenario` refuses the rest, a `new` name that is not a parameter, a lookup declared `new`, and a parameter declared `new` that the seed wholly holds. Before, `lookup=()` accepted any parameter, so a MERGE that stamps only on CREATE passed with a fresh id.
4. **Residual, stated.** A key declared `new` proves creation only: a statement that merges by it and leaks on a match is not run on a match (`known-issues.md`). The declaration is visible in the scenario for the reviewer, which is what DEC-142 item 9 relies on.

## DEC-157 The outbox relay: one tenant per round, the lock, failure and ordering, and the choices the prompt leaves open (I03)
Status: accepted (decided by the builder under DEC-133) · Affects: `adapters/inbound/workers/outbox_relay.py`, `bootstrap/outbox_relay_main.py`, I04, I05, TEN-007, TEN-016, TEN-C06 (Redis tenant prefix), O04, hazards H-26 and H-36. Numbered DEC-157: main holds DEC-155 and task/TEN-004 holds DEC-156; recheck before merging.

1. **The relay binds one tenant per round, from a directory (DEC-134 item 9, DEC-146).** The prompt's amendment offered an allowlisted cross-tenant read of ids or a loop over the active tenants. The relay loops: `run_once` asks an `ActiveTenants` source for the tenant ids, and for each one in turn binds that scope (`bind_scope`, tenant scope only; a `net-` id or any text that is not a tenant id is skipped and logged once, never relayed), takes the lock, and opens every unit of work under that scope. No statement spans two tenants and no statement is added to the allowlist. Tenant A's relay reads only A's pending rows (`Q-OUTBOX-PENDING` carries `tenantId: $tenant` since TEN-004), so it can never publish B's. The tenant comes from the directory, which is the platform's own record, never from a row, a payload or a request. The relay is therefore a binder, and is listed in `BINDING_MODULES` of `tests/architecture/test_single_execution_point.py` beside the SMS webhook (a one-line change outside the task's file list, made as I07 made it).
2. **`ActiveTenants` is defined in the worker.** `async active_tenant_ids() -> Sequence[str]`. TEN-007 builds the real `TenantDirectory`; it satisfies this or an adapter wraps it, and nothing in the relay changes. `StaticTenants(ids)` is the directory for tests and for the process entry until then. This is a new name beside TEN-007's `application/ports/outbound/tenants.py`, listed in `contracts.md` Additions so TEN-007 reuses or replaces it.
3. **Constructor.** `OutboxRelay(uow_factory, bus, cache, clock, tenants, batch_size=100, *, poll_interval_s=1.0, backoff_start_s=1.0)`. The prompt's signature had no `tenants`. The two keyword options exist so a test can run `run_forever` in milliseconds; production uses the defaults. `RelayResult(published, failed, skipped_no_lock)` is as the prompt, summed over the tenants; `skipped_no_lock` is True when at least one tenant's round was left because another holder had its lock, or the lock was refused under item 6.
4. **Every message carries its tenant.** Every `BusMessage` the relay publishes or schedules sets `tenant_id` (the existing field; the adapter sends it as the `tenantId` application property, DEC-130 item 7) to the tenant bound for that row. `BusMessage.id` stays the outbox id, as the prompt says. The `<tenant>:<id>` message id of the audit (C 5.2) is not adopted: outbox ids come from `UuidGenerator`, and the one producer id that is tenant-local (`cancel-<work id>`) is never sent as a message, because a `CancelWork` row makes a `bus.cancel` call. Revisit if a producer sends a tenant-local id on the bus.
5. **The lock.** Name `outbox-relay`, TTL 30 s, taken per tenant and per batch while that tenant is bound, and released in a `finally`. The cache adapter puts `diucs:<tenant>:` in front of the name (DEC-130 item 7, TEN-C06), so the lock is one per tenant once that adapter change is made. Until then the name is not prefixed and the lock is one lock for the whole relay: safe (it only ever stops a second relay) and a little more contention. The prompt's amendment said the lock is global; per tenant is what the audit (C 5.5) and DEC-130 say, and it follows from item 1 with no extra code. `tests/support/relay_rig.py::TenantPrefixedCache` stands in for the adapter to prove the lock is asked for under the bound tenant.
6. **Fencing (hazard H-26), partly.** DEC-091 says the relay must not trust a lock whose token is below the last token recorded in Neo4j. Nothing in Neo4j records a token yet: the outbox port has no field for it and this task owns no statement. The relay keeps the highest token it has held per tenant in memory and refuses (releases, relays nothing, logs a fixed line, counts as skipped) a lock whose token is not above it. That closes a clock step back or a Redis loss while the process lives. It does not close one across a restart. The Neo4j-recorded mark stays open, with the owner of the outbox head node (the audit, C 4.3, puts the mark on the tenant's outbox head): known-issues and H-26 say so.
7. **A batch stops before the lock can run out.** At `LOCK_TTL - LOCK_MARGIN` (30 s less 5 s, [ASSUMED] margin) after the lock was taken, the batch stops and the rest waits for the next round. Past that point nothing shows the relay still holds the lock. A message already in flight is finished.
8. **Failure and waiting (prompt: back off 1 s doubling to 60 s).** The wait is per tenant, so one tenant's failures never slow another's round: after a round with a failure the tenant is skipped until `clock.now()` passes 1, 2, 4, ... 60 s (capped), and a clean round forgets the wait. A skipped tenant is not `skipped_no_lock`. `run_forever` also backs off (the same schedule, real time) after a round that fails as a whole (the directory cannot be read), pauses a poll interval after a round that published nothing, and goes straight on after one that published. It never exits on an error, so an outage of Neo4j (31 to 43 s, lessons L13, L14) is waited out and no row is lost: a row stays pending until it is marked.
9. **What counts as a failure.** A bus call that raises: `mark_failed(id, <class name of the error>)` in its own unit of work (attempts + 1; the tenth makes it dead), the batch goes on with the next message (the prompt says continue), `failed` + 1. Only the class name is kept and logged, because an error's text can hold the payload. A bus call that succeeded and a mark that raised (the prompt's test 4): no attempt is counted (the publish did not fail), `failed` + 1, the batch stops (the store is the likely cause, and going on would publish a storm of rows it cannot mark), the tenant waits, and the next round publishes the same id again. A failure to count an attempt stops the batch the same way. A message that becomes dead is logged once, by id, topic and tenant only, never the payload; alerting on dead rows is O04.
10. **Order.** Rows go oldest first (`Q-OUTBOX-PENDING`: `createdAt`, then id). Because a failed row is skipped and the rest go on, a retried row can reach the bus after newer ones. Consumers must not assume bus order across a retry: the board projector rebuilds from the store, and I04 consumers dedupe by id. Within a session the bus keeps the order it was sent in, not the order created.
11. **Scheduled work.** A row is a ScheduleWork or CancelWork when its topic is `scheduled.work` and its event type is `ScheduleWork` or `CancelWork` (the constants of `application/scheduling.py`); anything else is published. ScheduleWork: read the work (a missing row is a failure with an attempt, not a skip), `bus.schedule("scheduled.work", BusMessage(id=<outbox id>, topic="scheduled.work", event_type="ScheduledWorkDue", payload={work_id, kind, about_id}, tenant_id), enqueue_at)` with `enqueue_at` from the payload; if the work was already `cancelled`, `bus.cancel` at once; then in one unit of work mark the row published and save the sequence number on the work, read in that same unit (the number is stored for a cancelled work too, so a later CancelWork finds it). CancelWork: read the work (missing: failure); with a number, `bus.cancel`; without one, do nothing (the ScheduleWork cancels itself when it is relayed); then mark published. A topic the bus does not know (including `scheduled.work` with another event type) fails in the bus and counts attempts like any publish error.
12. **Known limits, recorded.** (a) A crash after `bus.schedule` and before the unit of work that stores the number leaves a scheduled message whose number is lost; the retry schedules a second one. The handler ignores work that is not `scheduled` and consumers dedupe by id (U03, I05), so the work runs once, but a cancel that comes later reaches only the second message. (b) `Neo4jScheduledWorkRepository.save` writes every field from the object it is given, so a `cancel_work` that commits between the relay's read and its save in the same unit is overwritten to `scheduled`; the CancelWork row still reaches the bus and cancels the message. A statement that sets only the sequence number would close this (L142 pattern); it belongs to the conversion of `Q-WORK-*` (known-issues). (c) While `Q-WORK-GET` and `Q-WORK-SAVE` match by id (they are on the ratchet), a ScheduleWork or CancelWork row of tenant A that named a work id of tenant B would read, and for a cancel act on, B's work. No use case writes such a row (the work id and the row are made in one unit of work under one tenant), and the relay cannot tell, so the two statements are listed as tenant-pending in the evidence and their conversion is the fix.
13. **The process entry is in bootstrap.** `python -m diucs.bootstrap.outbox_relay_main`. The layers contract forbids an inbound worker importing the container or an outbound adapter, so the entry that names Redis and Service Bus and the container cannot live in `outbox_relay.py`; it is a new bootstrap module that builds the connection and the units of work from the container, `RedisCache` and `ServiceBusEventBus` from settings, and hands them to the relay. The container has no cache, bus or directory field yet, and `bootstrap/container.py` and `settings.py` are TEN-007's files, so the entry builds those three itself and reads the tenant list from `DIUCS_RELAY_TENANTS` (comma-separated tenant ids; missing or malformed stops the process before any connection opens, with a fixed message that names the variable and never a value). TEN-007 replaces `configured_tenants` with the directory. The entry's wiring (`serve`) was not run end to end here (it needs a stack the tests do not start); its configuration checks are unit tests and the relay with the real bus is item 9 of the prompt's tests.
14. **Tenancy, transitional.** The relay adds no statement. It uses `Q-OUTBOX-ADD` and `Q-OUTBOX-PENDING` (converted by TEN-004: tenant stamped and scoped), `Q-OUTBOX-LOCK`, `Q-OUTBOX-PUBLISHED` and `Q-OUTBOX-FAILED` (match by id, unconverted until TEN-016) and `Q-WORK-GET` and `Q-WORK-SAVE` (match by id, unconverted). The two-tenant tests use different ids in the two tenants for exactly that reason. Cross-scope reads the task would need under DEC-132: none.
15. **Test corrections after the red commits.** `test_the_lock_is_released_after_a_round_so_the_next_round_can_take_it` was green on the stub and was given an assertion that the round ran before the red commit. In the entry's tests the refused-id case `a` was a substring of the fixed message, so the check "never repeats the value" could not hold for it; the id became `q` (that test was red on the stub for another reason and is green now).
16. **Hazards.** H-26 is updated (partial, item 6) and H-36 is added: a queued message is lost, repeated beyond what consumers absorb, or sent for the wrong tenant.

Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/I03.md`.

## DEC-158 Grouped prompts for a phone with several services: what is grouped, what is stored, and the choices the prompt leaves open (U04)
Status: accepted (builder build-U04, under DEC-133; no owner gate) · Affects: U04 (`application/use_cases/send_prompts.py`), U05 (reads the records stored here, DEC-148 item 9), the prompt-due job (I05 wiring: the key helper), D15 (C9 is not changed), P07 (one new delivery status), H-16 · Numbering: DEC-156 was the top number on `main` (a95fa3c), and `task/I03-outbox-relay` holds DEC-157 when this was written; checked again before the merge.

1. **Scope, and two small edits outside the one file.** The use case is `send_prompts.py`. Two one-line edits outside it were needed to store the prompt's "grouped" status: `application/ports/outbound/messaging.py` (`GROUPED_STATUS`, added to `DELIVERY_STATUSES`) and `adapters/outbound/graph_store/communication_repository.py` (`_FHIR_STATUS["grouped"] = "completed"`). Known-issues row U05 (replace `_stamp` with `format_stamp`) is done: `_stamp` and `_MONTHS` are gone, and the test that pinned the two equal (`test_london_time.py::test_format_stamp_is_the_wording_send_prompts_gives_a_figure`) is removed with them; the C1 date is still pinned by `test_c1_text_carries_the_dated_figure_the_deadline_and_a_one_time_link` and `test_a_figure_from_a_previous_day_shows_its_date`.
2. **What is grouped.** When a prompt is due for service S, each of S's reporter phones is looked up (`services_for_phone_hash`, through the unit of work, so only this tenant's services are seen). Another service X of that phone joins the group when it is a text service, is active, is not suspended, has a figure, is in hours at its due time, its reply window is open (the same `_skip_reason` as its own prompt) and its prompt for that due time has not already been handled (its idempotency key is absent). X's due time is its `current_due` or `next_due` nearest S's due time and less than `GROUP_WINDOW` from it; `group_due` (D28) then decides the groups (anchored on the earliest, strictly less than 10 minutes), and S's group is used. Only a text service is grouped, and only with another text service: C9 is a text, and a service on another channel keeps its own prompt (the prompt's "same channel" rule, narrowed to text because C9 has no email or push form; the W38 app notification is out of scope).
3. **A service with no figure is not grouped (the prompt's "?" is not built).** The prompt says a service with no figure yet shows "?" in C9. D15's C9 takes whole numbers only (`test_c9_malformed_items_are_refused` pins it), its reply hints add 1 to the figure, and "Reply Y if both right" has no meaning for a service with no figure (U05 refuses a Y with no figure to confirm). Changing the safety-critical C9 template is not this task's scope and it would invite a Y that confirms nothing. A service with no figure, as S or as X, is prompted on its own with C2 (the question it needs), exactly as before. If the owner wants "?" in C9, it is a D15 change first (known-issues).
4. **A service with an old figure is not grouped.** U05 refuses a Y when the figure was more than `STALE_WARNING_AFTER` (2 hours, D05) old when the prompt was sent (DEC-148 item 2), and C9 shows no "as of" time. So a service whose latest figure is more than 2 hours old at the prompt, as S or as X, is prompted on its own with C1, which shows the date and time of the figure (DEC-115 item 11). The test is `now - effective_at > STALE_WARNING_AFTER`, the same line as U05's `figure_is_out_of_date`, so a group never offers a Y that U05 will refuse. [ASSUMED] That this narrows grouping to the services the reply rules can confirm; a daily or twice-a-day service is therefore almost never grouped (U05's known issue says the same services can never confirm by Y).
5. **The idempotency key, and what is stored under it.** `prompt_key(service_id, due)` is `prompt:<service_id>:<due in UTC, isoformat>`, the key form the U03 tests already use. In the same unit of work as the prompt, each other grouped service's result is stored under its own `prompt_key(its id, its due)`, with the fingerprint of the command its own job will send (this command's actor, its service, its due time, DEC-104), so that job is a replay and writes nothing; a different actor on that key is a Conflict. The job that runs `prompt-due` (I05 wiring, not built yet) must build its command key with `prompt_key`. If it used another form the cost is a second prompt to a grouped service, never a missed one. The result stored for X has `communication_ids` of X's own records and `grouped_service_ids` of the group.
6. **A group that cannot be made is not made.** Two services of one phone with the same name (letters could not tell them apart; `group_due` refuses them), more than 26 services, or a C9 that cannot be worded (the privacy guard refuses a name that looks like patient data) leave every service to be prompted on its own, each by its own job. The prompt is never lost and no reply can land on the wrong service. A name that the guard refuses still fails that service's single prompt as it did before; nothing here changes that.
7. **What is stored, as U05 reads it (lesson L153).** One `Communication` for each service of the group, in letter order: `message_id` C9, the same `sent_at` (this command's time) and the same `payload_text` (the C9 text, which has no link, so there is no link token for a grouped prompt), `about_id` the service, and `recipient_role_id` the role this phone holds for that service (so U05's `latest_prompt_for_phone` finds the C9 through that service's roles). Letter A's is `queued` and the only one with a `SendMessage` outbox message (sealed under DEC-116 with letter A's role as the recipient); the others are `grouped`. The letters come from `group_due`'s items, which are name order. A phone that holds two posts for S is texted once (the first role); the single prompt it would have had for the second post is not sent. The reply-by is `PromptGroup.reply_by`, the earliest due time in the group plus 30 minutes. The seam is tested end to end: `test_send_prompts_grouped_reply.py` runs `HandleInboundText` on a stored group.
8. **The `grouped` delivery status.** A new member of `DELIVERY_STATUSES`. It never changes: the record has no provider message id, and a status callback matches by provider id, so no callback reaches it. Its FHIR `Communication.status` is `completed` (the text went out in letter A's message). It is not a status a provider can report; the I07 mapping is unchanged.
9. **The result.** `SendPromptResult.grouped_service_ids` is every service prompted in one C9 with this one, itself included, in letter order, and empty when it was prompted on its own. `communication_ids` stays the records written for this service.
10. **Another reporter of a grouped service is not lost.** A grouped service's own job will do nothing, so its reporters on other phones, and on phones that are not in this group, are prompted now, on their own, in the same unit of work, with their own link and ladder. Such a reporter is not grouped with the other services on their phone from here (no cascade): they get the single prompt, as before U04. This is a residual: that phone may then get a single text and a C9 close together.
11. **Ladders, next prompts and audit.** Each grouped service gets its own ladder (reminder +30, phone task +60, escalate +90 from its own due time) about its own Communication, and its own next `prompt-due`, written once. One audit entry covers the command: its entities are every message written, the service, and the other grouped services; the snapshot keys are unchanged and `prompts` is the number of messages written.
12. **Tenancy (DEC-130 to DEC-132, transitional rule).** No statement, tenant argument or key is added. The other services of the phone are found through the unit of work's person repository, which runs in the bound tenant. The statements reached are the repositories' (`Q-PHONE-SERVICES-HELD`, `Q-PHONE-SERVICES-AT`, `Q-COMM-ADD` and the service, flag, capacity, scheduled work, outbox, audit and idempotency statements) and are on the ratchet. The two-tenant test is not written: no node carries `tenantId` yet, so a second tenant's phone cannot be seeded apart (known-issues, trigger TEN-009 and TEN-011).
13. **Not here.** The "more than 3 services" flag (W43), grouped app notifications (W38), and lettered replies and C10 (U05) are other tasks. A phone with more than 3 services is grouped up to the 26 letters.

Tests: `backend/tests/integration/application/test_send_prompts_grouped.py`, `test_send_prompts_grouped_reply.py`, `backend/tests/unit/application/use_cases/test_send_prompts_key.py` and `backend/tests/integration/graph_store/test_communication_repository.py`. Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/U04.md`.

## DEC-163 The tenant directory, the global statement allowlist, secret references and the fake secret store as built (TEN-007)
Status: accepted (decided by the builder under DEC-133; the task prompt file `docs/plan/prompts/TEN-007.md` does not exist, so the task's scope is `tasks.json`, the tenancy plan (TEN-007 summary), DEC-131, DEC-132, DEC-134 items 9 and 14, DEC-144 item 4, DEC-155 and DEC-157 items 2 and 13) · Affects: `application/ports/outbound/tenants.py` (new), `adapters/outbound/graph_store/tenant_directory.py` (new), `queries.py` (`GLOBAL_QUERY_IDS`, `GLOBAL_STATEMENT_LABELS`, the global checks), `migrations_tenant.py` (0034, 0035), `connection.py` (`driver`), `adapters/testing/fixed_secrets.py`, `bootstrap/container.py`, `bootstrap/outbox_relay_main.py`, `tests/architecture/test_single_execution_point.py` (`TENANT_PARAMETER_MODULES`), TEN-013, TEN-016, TEN-017, TEN-018, TEN-023, TEN-025, TEN-026, TEN-028, TEN-040, TEN-041, TEN-051. Numbered DEC-163: main holds DEC-161, task/U10 holds DEC-160 and task/TEN-008 holds DEC-162; recheck before merging.

1. **The registry is two node kinds, and `REGISTRY_LABELS` stays six.** A `Tenant` node holds the properties of the domain `Tenant` (TEN-006: codes as text, times as datetimes, counts as integers, an unset field as no property; `tenant_properties` and `tenant_from_properties` are the two directions and are the only place the mapping lives). It also holds `host` and `entraDirectoryId`, each unique across tenants and absent until set: DEC-134 item 3 gives a tenant one host and item 14 one Entra directory, so a property with a unique constraint says "one tenant" in the schema, and no new registry label is needed (the six of TEN-005 are pinned by a test and by the seeder). A `TenantNumber` node `(:Tenant)-[:HAS_NUMBER]->(:TenantNumber {e164, accountSid, secretRef, status})` holds a number: `e164` is unique across the platform, `accountSid` is indexed, `status` is `active`, `retired` or `quarantined` (`NumberStatus`). The reference from number to tenant is a relationship, not an id string (DEC-001), and carries no `tenantId`: registry nodes carry none (DEC-132), so the cross-scope invariant query ignores them.
2. **Seven new global statements, read-only, on the allowlist, each pinned by digest (DEC-144 item 4, DEC-134 item 9; round 2 of verification replaced the first design).** `Q-TENANT-GET`, `-LIST-ACTIVE`, `-ACTIVE-IDS`, `-BY-NUMBER`, `-BY-ACCOUNT`, `-BY-HOST`, `-BY-ENTRA`. `GLOBAL_QUERY_IDS` is eight entries with a reason each. **The guarantee is the pin:** `GLOBAL_STATEMENTS` maps each id to the SHA-256 hex digest of its exact text, stripped of leading and trailing whitespace and nothing else (`statement_digest`). A global statement registers only when its id is in `GLOBAL_STATEMENTS` AND the digest of its text equals the pinned one: a changed byte, a trailing comment, a case change, an extra clause, or the text of another statement under this id is refused, and so is an id with no pin. Adding or changing a global statement is therefore a deliberate edit of that constant, reviewed like any security-relevant diff (with its reason on `GLOBAL_QUERY_IDS` and its text in `test_global_allowlist.py`). This follows DEC-142: the seam is a registry of fixed statements, never an analysis of arbitrary Cypher. Round 1 rested the claim on a regex over the text, and `MATCH(n:Resource)` (no space) got past it; the regex is not made complete, the pin carries the claim. **The structural checks stay as defence in depth, and are not a parser:** registration also refuses a global statement that (a) is not `read`, (b) contains a write or procedure word or a `;`, (c) has a `(` after a name that is neither an allowed function (`properties`, `count`, `coalesce`, `toLower`, `toUpper`, `size`, `labels`) nor a clause keyword (so `MATCH(n:X)` is read as the node pattern it is), (d) has a node pattern with no label, a label outside `GLOBAL_STATEMENT_LABELS` (`Tenant`, `TenantNumber`; link-token and network registry statements add their label when they are added, as a deliberate change) or a parenthesis that is neither node nor function call, (e) has a brace outside a node's property map; comments are replaced and string contents removed before these checks, and an unterminated string, quoted name or comment is refused. A `QueryTemplate` cannot be global (its insert would choose the label). The checks run before the pin, so each has tests that reach it with the text pinned (`test_the_structural_check_alone_refuses_every_bypass_even_when_the_text_is_pinned`, 22 bypass texts). The behavioural half is `test_no_global_statement_returns_anything_from_a_graph_that_also_holds_data` (two tenants' data nodes carry the same words as the registry keys and no row ever holds one) and an architecture test (`test_tenant_registry_access.py`): only `tenant_directory.py` and the tenant migrations name a registry label in Cypher, only the ping and the directory run a global statement, the directory binds nothing and runs no tenant statement, and only the container builds it. The generated two-tenant test does not cover a global statement (it has no tenant); that is why these tests exist. Parameter names are `$id`, `$number`, `$accountSid`, `$host`, `$directoryId`: never `$tenant`, which is only ever the bound tenant. `graph_store/tenant_directory.py` is in `TENANT_PARAMETER_MODULES` (it takes the id it is asked about and sends no tenant).
3. **The directory is read-only and uncached.** No write statement is on the allowlist: provisioning (TEN-028) decides how a tenant is created, and a global write would be the widest door in the system. There is no cache: a suspension must take effect on the next call (audit B 4.1 allowed a short cache with invalidation; nothing here needs the speed). The tests seed registry nodes with raw Cypher.
4. **What a lookup returns.** `get`, `by_host`, `by_entra_directory` return the `Tenant` whatever its status: the caller decides what a suspended or offboarding tenant may do (TEN-040, TEN-041). `by_inbound_number(to, account_sid)` needs the number and the account to match one `active` number (a retired or quarantined number resolves to nobody, DEC-136 item 10) and returns `TenantLine(tenant, number, account_sid, secret_ref)`; `by_account_sid` (delivery receipts) takes the active numbers of the account. `list_active` and `active_tenant_ids` return the tenants whose status is `active`, in id order. `active_tenant_ids` returns ids only, from a statement that reads no other field, so one tenant's damaged record cannot stop the others' outbox. A key that is not the shape it names is not looked up and sends nothing: a tenant id (`check_tenant_id`, so `platform` and `net-` are refused), E.164 (`+` and 7 to 15 digits, no leading 0), an account SID (`AC` and 32 lower-case hex digits), a host (ASCII, lower-cased, one final dot removed, labels of 1 to 63 letters, digits and hyphens, no port or scheme or underscore, 253 at most) and a GUID (lower-cased). ASCII is checked before lower-casing, so no other character folds into a letter.
5. **Fail closed on a bad record: `TenantRecordInvalid`.** A node the domain refuses (missing or mistyped property, status that disagrees with its times, a time held as text, a naive time, an enum object where a code is stored), a number whose `secretRef` is not that tenant's own, an account held by two tenants or with two references, a second row for a unique key: each raises `TenantRecordInvalid` with one fixed message that names no id, number, host or reference, never a guess and never "unknown number" (an unknown number is a quiet 200; a configuration fault must be seen). Callers map it to a 500 and an alert (audit C, the inbound SMS steps). Nothing in the directory is logged.
6. **Secrets are references (DEC-131 item 4).** A reference is `<kind>/<tenant id>/<name>` (lower case; kind `[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,31}`, name `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]{0,63}`). `secret_ref(kind, tenant_id, name)` builds one, `twilio_token_ref(tenant_id)` is `twilio/<tenant>/auth-token` (audit C 8.2.5), and `check_secret_ref(value, tenant_id)` accepts only the tenant's own, so a record cannot send a number's signature check to another tenant's token. A refusal holds no value. Platform secrets (`link-token`, `phone-hash`, the BFF secret) keep their plain names and are not tenant references. The graph, git, logs and evidence hold references only: a test seeds two tenants, reads their secrets through the fake store and shows no token text in any node or relationship property.
7. **The fake secret store is a recording fake.** `FixedSecretStore` keeps its signature and adds `requested` (every name asked for, in order, hits and misses), `read_count(name)` and `names`; it records names and never values, its `repr` and its `vars()` show no value (the values sit behind a wrapper with a fixed repr), and it refuses a value that is not `bytes` and a name that is not a non-empty text at construction. Its values in tests are plainly fake (`test-only-...`). **Needs a real value at deploy:** a real secret store (a vault adapter needs a credential and does not exist), each tenant's Twilio subaccount and auth token, and the per-tenant references in the registry.
8. **The container carries `tenants` and `secrets`.** `Container.tenants: TenantDirectory` (`Neo4jTenantDirectory` over the one connection) and `Container.secrets: SecretStore`. `build(settings, *, secrets=None)`: the process that serves passes its store; with none, `NoSecrets` answers every name with `NotFound` (fail closed, no fake in a production build). `build_for_tests(..., secrets=None)` gives a `FixedSecretStore({})`. `Settings` is unchanged: no setting selects a secret backend because only one backend (none) exists, the directory has no cache to size, and the existing `twilio_*` and `smtp_*` settings are left to the adapters that move to per-tenant credentials (audit C 8.2 item 4).
9. **Start-up applies the migrations and the audit head.** `Container.startup()` opens the connection, runs `apply_migrations` on the global path, then `ensure_audit_head` once, as the audit head's own docstring says (after the migrations, once at start-up). The head statement is still unconverted, so it makes the one chain that is the default tenant's (DEC-134 item 10); TEN-017 makes it per scope and the start-up will then bind each active tenant in turn (TEN-028 creates a new tenant's head). A failure after the connection opened closes it and re-raises, so the process neither starts nor leaves a connection open. Running two processes' start-ups together is safe: every migration step is idempotent and the record is a `MERGE`. `Neo4jConnection.driver` is a new read-only property for these two calls (it raises before `open()` and after `close()`).
10. **Migrations `0034_tenant_registry` and `0035_default_tenant_record`.** 0034 creates `tenant_id`, `tenant_host`, `tenant_entra_directory` (unique), `tenant_number_e164` (unique) and the index `tenant_number_account`, `IF NOT EXISTS`, one statement each, with a manual `ROLLBACK_0034`. 0035 creates the default tenant record (`id` `default`, `Default tenant`, type `other`, `active`, `hashKeyVersion` 0 for the legacy global keys, no number, host or directory) `ON CREATE` only and only when some node carries `tenantId = 'default'`, so a fresh installation gets no tenant it did not ask for, a record changed since is never written over, and the development data that 0032 stamped belongs to a tenant the relay serves, as the environment list did. It names no organisation. The ids were checked against every ref and worktree (none used 0034 or above). Both live in `migrations_tenant.py`'s `TENANT_MIGRATIONS` (now five), so three TEN-005 tests that unpacked three entries now slice them.
11. **The outbox relay reads its tenants from the directory (DEC-157 items 2 and 13 closed).** `Neo4jTenantDirectory.active_tenant_ids()` satisfies `ActiveTenants` structurally (no import between an adapter and a worker). `outbox_relay_main.py` passes `container.tenants`; `configured_tenants`, `TENANTS_VARIABLE` and `DIUCS_RELAY_TENANTS` are gone (a test pins that); `serve(settings, stop)` loses its `tenants` argument and closes the container if start-up fails. `StaticTenants` stays, for tests. A directory with no active tenant relays nothing and says nothing: the process is healthy and idle until a tenant is activated. The relay's own validation still skips an id that is not a tenant id (DEC-157 item 1).
12. **Not built here.** The `InboundLineDirectory` adapter over the directory (TEN-041: `for_number` is `by_inbound_number` plus the reference, one small class), `TenantChannel`, `TenantContact`, config versions and settings storage (TEN-026), tenant creation and number assignment (TEN-028), the Entra role mapping and binding verification (the sign-in tasks), the `network` lookups (`networks_of`, TEN-051), a Redis namespace for the directory, and any change to a use case, route or repository.
13. **Tenancy.** The directory is a cross-scope reader by design (DEC-134 item 9): it returns configuration of the registry nodes and nothing else, whatever scope is bound. The cross-scope reads this task would need: none beyond itself. Tenant-pending statements: none added (all seven are global; no tenant statement was added). Hazards: H-T02 control (the line directory exists; the route still uses the fake until TEN-041) and a new H-T22 for the global statement allowlist.

Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/TEN-007.md`.

## DEC-161 Report flags: raised in the unit of work that stores the report, and a Y never answers a pending out-of-range figure (U07)
Status: accepted (builder build-U07, under DEC-133) · Affects: U07 (`application/use_cases/report_flags.py`, `report_capacity.py`), U02 (`ConfirmCapacity` raises flags through `record_report`), U05 (`handle_inbound_text.py`: the `_report` fallback is removed and the Y rule changes), `domain/events.py` (`ReportFlagged`), W42 and U39 (read and resolve the flags), hazards H-03, H-14 and the new H-35. Numbered DEC-161: checked on 2026-10-29 against `main` (top DEC-158) and the task branches (DEC-157 I03, DEC-159 TEN-005, DEC-160 U10); re-check before merging.

1. **Where flags are raised.** `raise_report_flags(uow, ids, *, profile, report, extra, now) -> list[str]` (`report_flags.py`) is called by `record_report` after the report is added, in the unit of work and under the service lock that both `ReportCapacity` and `ConfirmCapacity` already take before they read. So a number and a confirmation (which counts towards `many-changes`) are judged by the same rules, a flag commits or rolls back with its report, and two racing reports for one service cannot both raise the same open flag. `ReportCapacityResult` gets `flag_ids: tuple[str, ...] = ()`; the audit entry's `entity_ids` are `(report, service, *flags)`. `ConfirmCapacityResult` is unchanged (its flags are stored and announced, not returned).
2. **The look-back runs from the report's effective time, not from the clock.** The prompt read `reports_since(service, now - PHONED_WINDOW)`. A report sent at 15:00 for 14:00 (an explicit effective time, DEC-140) would then miss a figure phoned at 13:30, which D12 counts (30 minutes before the report). The read is `report.effective_at - LOOKBACK`, where `LOOKBACK` is the longer of D12's two windows. D12 then ignores anything later than the report. Test: `test_a_back_dated_report_is_judged_against_the_hour_before_its_own_time`.
3. **Reasons.** The rule reasons come from `flags_for_report` unchanged (D12, DEC-025: inclusive edges, any phoned figure in the window). The command's `flag` (the reply policy's `out-of-range-twice`) is added after them, once. The windows are D12's constants; the per-tenant settings of TEN-006 are not wired to the rules yet (TEN-006 pins the defaults equal).
4. **One open flag per service and reason.** A reason that already has an open flag for the service (`open_report_flags(service_id)`) raises nothing, and the report is stored all the same. A flag that is checked or accepted no longer counts, so the next breaking report raises a new one.
5. **A flag is never silent and never blocks.** The report is stored in every case. If the flag, its outbox message or the audit entry cannot be written, the whole unit of work rolls back and the error is raised: the report is not stored either (an unflagged report that broke a rule is the failure to avoid; the text path retries the message). Nothing is recorded for the idempotency key, so the same command can be tried again. Test: `test_when_the_flag_cannot_be_written_neither_the_report_nor_the_flag_is_stored`.
6. **`ReportFlagged`** (`domain/events.py`, topic `capacity.events`, Service Bus session id the service id, as `CapacityReported`): payload `{flag_id, service_id, report_id, reason, at}`, no figure, name or phone. `reason` must be a `ReportFlagReason` (a name, a postcode or a phone-shaped value is refused when the event is built or decoded, lesson L53); `at` is UTC. Added to the `DomainEvent` union, the topic map and the decoder map.
7. **`ReportCapacity` no longer refuses a flag.** It refuses, before any lock, a `flag` that is not a `ReportFlagReason` (`ValidationFailed("flag")`). The U01 test that pinned the refusal until U07 is replaced.
8. **The U05 seam.** `HandleInboundText._report` and its test are removed: the figure goes to `ReportCapacity` once, with the flag, and the flag is stored. U05's acceptance tests are unchanged except where they named the old fallback.
9. **U05's Y rule changes (the known-issues row "after an out-of-range recheck a following Y still confirms the old figure").** While a figure the reporter was asked to send again is pending for a service (the `recheck` key), a plain Y for that service is **not** a confirmation. It records nothing, sends the same C7 again ("if it is right, send 40 again"), and leaves the pending figure and its expiry as they were. Why: the reporter has just been told the new figure looks wrong and asked a direct question. Y answers neither "send 40 again" nor "keep the old figure", so it is ambiguous, and recording either reading puts a figure on the board the reporter may not mean: the old one when the true figure is lower than the usual range (overstated capacity, the unsafe direction), or a figure they said is wrong. The principle is the standard's first (when unsure, ask again). The cost is one more text. In a grouped reply a plain Y confirms the services with nothing pending and asks again about the others. After the pending figure expires a Y is an ordinary Y (H-31 and H-32 still apply). The old test `test_a_y_clears_the_pending_out_of_range_figure` is replaced by `test_a_y_does_not_confirm_the_old_figure_while_an_out_of_range_figure_is_pending` and two more. The change is in `HandleInboundText._item`, not in the reply policy (D11), whose `PromptState.pending_out_of_range` is unchanged.
10. **Tenancy (DEC-130 to DEC-134).** No Cypher statement is added: flags, reports and the outbox are read and written through the existing repositories, whose statements run in the bound scope; the command and the event carry no tenant. The flag statements (`Q-FLAG-*`) and `Q-CAP-SINCE` are still on the ratchet, so a two-tenant test cannot yet seed two tenants apart (no node carries `tenantId` until TEN-009 and the conversion bundles). Listed in the evidence as tenant-pending.
11. **Not here.** Resolving flags (U39), the "Reports to check" screen (W42), suspending a service (U36). D12 flags a text that differs from an earlier phoned figure; it does not flag a phoned figure that differs from an earlier text. That is D12's rule and is left (known-issues).

Tests: `backend/tests/integration/application/test_report_flags.py`, `backend/tests/unit/application/use_cases/test_report_flags_guards.py`, `backend/tests/unit/domain/test_events.py`, and in `test_handle_inbound_text.py` the three Y tests and `test_the_out_of_range_flag_is_passed_once_and_recorded`. Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/U07.md`.
## DEC-159 The expand step as built: composite keys beside the old ones, the isolation test on a contracted schema, and the stamp (TEN-005, refines DEC-134 items 10, 11 and 13 and DEC-150 items 2, 3, 14 and 15)
Status: accepted · Affects: `adapters/outbound/graph_store/migrations.py`, `migration_types.py`, `migration_loop.py`, `migrations_tenant.py`, `migrations_network.py` (all new but the first), `case_repository.py`, `scenario.py`, `credential_store.py`, `audit_neo4j/outbox.py`, `idempotency_store.py`, `backend/tests/support/tenant_seed.py`, `backend/tests/architecture/test_single_execution_point.py`, `backend/tests/integration/graph_store/test_tenant_isolation.py`, `test_tenant_migrations.py`, `backend/tests/unit/adapters/outbound/graph_store/test_tenant_ratchet.py` and `test_scenario.py`. Decided by the TEN-005 builder under DEC-133 where the prompt (written before TEN-004 was built as DEC-150) and the code on `main` differed. Numbered DEC-159: DEC-157 (I03) and DEC-158 (U04) are on their branches.

1. **Expand only, as the prompt says.** Migrations `0031_tenant_composites` (one global constraint `migration_id`, twenty composite constraints, six composite indexes), `0032_backfill_default_tenant` and `0033_case_open_reference` are added; nothing is dropped. The eighteen superseded constraints and six superseded indexes stay in the production schema until TEN-032 (the names are `SUPERSEDED_CONSTRAINTS` and `SUPERSEDED_INDEXES` in `migrations_tenant.py`, equal to the literals in `tests/support/tenant_seed.py`). `link_token_hash` stays globally unique on purpose (DEC-131 item 5: a hash lookup yields the tenant) and `location_position` is a point index. The ids `0031` to `0033` were free on every branch and worktree.
2. **The isolation test runs on a contracted schema, because the production schema cannot yet hold one id for two tenants.** DEC-150 item 2 ran the generated test on the shared harness schema and made `Unique` values for the keys a single-property constraint forbade sharing, "until TEN-005". But TEN-005 is expand-only, so the harness schema still has the old constraints until TEN-032, and the `contracted_container()` the TEN-004 prompt described was not built. TEN-005 builds it (`tests/support/tenant_seed.py`: a private throwaway container with every migration applied and the superseded keys dropped) and `test_tenant_isolation.py` now runs on it, in a module-scoped fixture. Every production scenario therefore shares every key between the two tenants: `Unique` is gone from the credential, outbox and processed-marker scenarios, `CONSTRAINTS_LEANED_ON` is empty and `COLLISION_BLOCKED` stays empty. `SINGLE_PROPERTY_UNIQUE` is not empty: it holds `link_token_hash` (a `LinkToken`'s `tokenHash`), the one key that stays unique across tenants. The `Unique` mechanism and the foreign-id run stay for it (TEN-013 will need them) and are tested against a table of their own in `test_scenario.py`; the corpus of leaky twins in the generated test uses a `LinkToken` hash where it used a `Resource` id. The integration test compares the table with the single-property keys of the contracted container, leaving out the registry label `Migration`.
3. **The migration runner.** `Migration(id, statements, loops, params, batch)` (`migration_types.py`, `batch` at least 1, `params` read-only). `apply_migrations(driver, migrations=None)`: each schema statement in its own write transaction, each loop repetition in its own session and transaction until it returns one row with an integer `changed` of 0, then the record in a transaction of its own.
