A failure raises `MigrationFailed("migration <id> failed at statement <n> | loop <n> | record: <ExceptionClass>")`, chained, with no Cypher and no value; steps before it stay applied and nothing after it runs. `MIGRATIONS` is assembled by `assemble_migrations(core, tenant, network)`, sorted by id; a duplicate id raises `ValueError` at import. `migration_loop.py` (an addition to the plan's file list, with `migration_types.py`) holds the batch loop so the runner and `backfill_default_tenant` share it; it runs raw `tx.run`, so the architecture test now names the three files that may run Cypher (`migrations.py`, `migration_loop.py`, `migrations_tenant.py`) instead of the glob `migrations*.py` (the known issue of TEN-001). A loop that does not return one integer `changed` is a `MigrationFailed`, whether it returns no row, two rows, a text, a boolean or a float. The loop cap is `MAX_LOOP_ITERATIONS` (10,000 repetitions).
4. **No Cypher is built from pieces.** The twenty-six statements of 0031 are written out in full in `migrations_tenant.py` (the architecture rule of DEC-144 item 7 forbids an f-string with a Cypher word anywhere in `src`), and a unit test reads each back and compares it with its row in `COMPOSITE_CONSTRAINTS` or `COMPOSITE_INDEXES`. `ROLLBACK_0031` is built from the rows with `DROP ... IF EXISTS` (no Cypher word) and is tested to drop exactly what 0031 creates; the runner never runs it.
5. **The stamp.** `STAMP_DEFAULT_TENANT` is label-less, selects `n.tenantId IS NULL` outside `REGISTRY_LABELS` (`Migration`, `Tenant`, `TenantNumber`, `Network`, `NetworkMembership`, `Grant`), and names its own parameter `$defaultTenant` (the default tenant id is `DEFAULT_TENANT_ID`, `"default"`), never `$tenant`. `backfill_default_tenant(driver, batch=10_000)` is the same loop as a function and is the repair if old code erased a stamp (`SET n = props`). `invariant_violations(driver)` returns `{"unstamped_nodes": [...], "cross_scope_relationships": [...]}`, both read in one transaction; TEN-031 runs it at start-up. An unstamped node is not caught by a composite constraint (Community has no existence constraint), which is why this query exists.
6. **The open reference.** `ServiceRequest.openReference` is set to the reference while the case is open and removed when it closes; the composite constraint `tenant_case_open_reference (tenantId, openReference)` is unique on it. `Q-CASE-SET-REFERENCE` sets both properties and stays unconverted (TEN-010 stamps it); the old `case_reference_id` still refuses a reused reference until TEN-032. `_rebuilt_request` in `case_repository.py` strips `openReference` with `reference`, because the stored request is read back into a FHIR resource and an unknown property is refused by the mapper.
7. **What this does not do.** No writer stamps a node until TEN-009, so the composite keys guard nothing on today's writes (a node without a tenant is ignored by them) and every statement except the eight converted ones is still unscoped. Old and new code must not write at once on a database that has data until TEN-030 (an old writer creates unstamped nodes; run `backfill_default_tenant` after). The statements that create a node under a key declared `new` (`Q-PROCESSED-MARK`, `Q-OUTBOX-ADD`, and the device id of `Q-CRED-SET`) still prove the creation only, not a match on a held key (DEC-156 item 4); every other lookup of a starter statement is by a key both tenants hold.


## DEC-160 Wall screen pairing: tenant from the edge, refusals, limits, the device record, and the choices the prompt leaves open (U10)
Status: accepted (builder build-U10, under DEC-133; no owner gate) · Numbered DEC-160: main's top was DEC-156, and DEC-157 (I03), DEC-158 (U04), DEC-159 (TEN-005) and DEC-161 (U07) are on branches; checked before the merge. Affects: U10 (`application/use_cases/pair_wall_screen.py`, `domain/model/device.py`, `DeviceRegistry`, `Neo4jDeviceRegistry`), R15 (pairing endpoints), W08 (pairing pages), R02 and R05 (the device token), U37 (revoking a screen). Refines DEC-131 item 5 and DEC-134 item 3, and overrides the prompt's "Task-specific change (from the tenancy audit)".

1. **The tenant is bound by the edge before the unit of work opens, for all three steps, and the use case only reads it from the unit of work.** The prompt's audit note says StartPairing creates the record unassigned and the lead's confirmation assigns the lead's tenant. DEC-134 item 3 is later and says the tenant of any unauthenticated request, pairing included, comes from the host, and that pairing codes are unique within a tenant and entered on the tenant's host. That is followed. A pairing that was unassigned until some lead typed its code would let any tenant's lead take any screen, and would need global statements. `StartPairing` and `ClaimPairing` run in the tenant of the host (R15 binds it with `bind_tenant` from the sub-domain); `ConfirmPairing` runs in the tenant of the lead's signed token. A unit of work with no tenant, or a network scope, is refused with `TenantNotBound` (a screen is paired to one tenant, DEC-132 item 5). No command carries a tenant.
2. **Codes and secrets are hashed with the tenant.** The stored hash is `hash_token("<tenant>-<code or secret>", link-token secret)`. The same six digits in two tenants are two different records, a lookup in tenant B cannot find tenant A's code or secret, and the global unique `tokenHash` cannot make one tenant's code collide with another's (a collision would otherwise tell a caller that another tenant holds that code). The code and the secret hold no hyphen, so the last hyphen splits `<tenant>-<text>` one way only. The link-token statements (`Q-LINK-*`) are still unconverted, so the two `LinkToken` nodes carry no `tenantId`: the keyed hash is the isolation until the link-token bundle converts them (tenant-pending, listed in the evidence). The replay statements (`Q-IDEM-*`) are unconverted too and key a record by the key alone, so the command key handed to the replay store and to the link-token burn is `pairing:<tenant>:<key>` (`_command_key`): two tenants using one key are two commands (found by the review of this task: tenant B's first confirmation with tenant A's key was refused as a Conflict; test `test_the_same_command_key_in_two_tenants_is_two_commands`).
3. **Every refusal of a code or a secret is one error with one message.** A wrong, malformed, expired, used, or another tenant's code, and a secret that is wrong, malformed, another pairing's or another tenant's, all raise `NotAllowed("This code or screen is not valid.")`. Another tenant's code or screen is therefore indistinguishable from a wrong one, which is what "reads as NotFound" needs for an answer that must not say whether the thing exists; `errors.py` already answers `NotAllowed` as 404 `not-found`. The prompt's test 6 names `NotAllowed` and "same message", so that is the type. Only a lead may confirm: any other role, or a lead with no practitioner id, gets a different `NotAllowed` ("Only a shift lead can connect a screen.") before the code is looked at, so the role check cannot be used to probe codes. A bad screen name or place is `ValidationFailed` and is checked before the code is burned, so the lead can fix it and use the same code.
4. **Rate limits, kept in the cache (nothing of record), taken atomically before the lookup.** A tenant may start `START_LIMIT` (20) pairings in `LIMIT_WINDOW` (10 minutes, the life of a code): this bounds the codes alive at once, so a guessed code has few to hit, and bounds the nodes an unauthenticated caller can make. A lead may have `CONFIRM_FAILURE_LIMIT` (5) failed confirmations in the window; a further one is refused with `PairingRateLimited`, whatever code is typed, until the oldest failure leaves the window. Both counts are per tenant (the lead's per tenant and practitioner), made of slots `<name>#<n>` written with `set_if_absent`, each living one window, so the window runs from each event. `AttemptLimiter.take` takes the first free slot in one atomic step per slot and returns it (or None), so parallel callers can never hold more than the limit between them. ConfirmPairing takes its slot BEFORE the code is looked up (round 1 of verification found the first version read the count, looked the code up, and counted afterwards, so 200 parallel wrong codes gave 9 verdicts instead of 5): a refusal keeps its slot, and a success gives it back with `release`, so a good confirmation costs nothing. A replay of a stored confirmation is answered before the slot is taken. A cache that cannot be reached raises, and the step is refused with nothing written: pairing fails closed (tests: `test_a_flood_of_wrong_codes_from_one_lead_reaches_the_code_lookup_at_most_five_times`, `test_twenty_five_racing_starts_from_one_tenant_give_exactly_twenty`, `test_with_the_cache_down_start_and_confirm_refuse_and_write_nothing`; the literal values 5, 20 and ten minutes are pinned by `test_pair_wall_screen_limits.py` and by literal counts in the integration tests). The guess odds: at most 20 live codes and 5 tries per lead per window is at most 1 in 10,000 per lead per window. `PairingRateLimited` is a new `DomainError` for R15 to answer 429. Not limited here: a wrong secret at claim (the secret is 128 bits and a guess is hopeless), and the starts of an attacker who exhausts the 20 (they can delay a new pairing by ten minutes, never touch a paired screen). R15 should add a per-address throttle in front.
5. **The device record.** A wall screen is a `:Device` node with `id` (the pairing id), `tenantId`, `deviceType` `wall-display`, `status` (`paired-unclaimed`, `active`, `revoked`), `deviceName`, `locationName` (absent when none), `boardScope` (`tenant` or `network`, DEC-132 item 5; a reader treats anything but `network` as tenant) and `tokenHash` (absent until claimed), `updatedAt`. Deviations from the prompt: it is not a `:Resource` node (it holds a token hash and a pairing status that are not FHIR elements, and nothing reads it through the resource store or the mapper), and its type is a property, not a `TYPE` link to a `Concept` node: a Concept written here would collide with the global `concept_key` constraint or create an unstamped shared node, and DEC-134 item 1 puts Concepts under tenancy in the resource-store bundle (TEN-009), which can then replace the property. `DeviceRegistry` gains `get`, `save` and `find_by_token_hash` (contracts.md already lists `get` and `save`), implemented on the existing `Neo4jDeviceRegistry`; the `Device` record is in `domain/model/device.py` and checks its name and place (1 to 60 printable characters, no postcode, NHS number or titled name: the D17 guard), a 64-hex hash, and that an unclaimed screen has no hash and an active one has. A practitioner's device has no `deviceType`, so the two kinds never match each other's statements and a save over a practitioner's device id is a Conflict.
6. **The device token and who can find it.** 128 bits as 23 letters (`new_link_token`), shown once to the claim that burns the secret, hashed with its own secret (`device-token`, `DEVICE_TOKEN_SECRET` in `identity.py`) so a link token and a device token never share a key. `find_by_token_hash` is tenant-scoped and returns only an `active` screen: a revoked or unclaimed screen, or another tenant's, is not found, and two matches is a Conflict. No global device statement is built. The edge (R02, R05, R15) binds the tenant from the host (item 1) and the hash match inside that tenant authenticates the screen; a token shown on another tenant's host finds nothing. If a later design serves screens from one shared host, a tenant-resolving statement must be added to the global allowlist by its own decision. The claim's result is never put in the idempotency store, an HTTP cache or a log (it holds the token), so a claim whose reply is lost leaves a screen with no token: the lead pairs it again.
7. **Claim states.** `waiting` (no lead has confirmed; nothing is written), `paired` (the token is given once; any later claim, in any time, gets `paired` and no token: used takes precedence over expired), `expired` (the ten minutes ran out unused; contracts.md already allows this status, so the screen asks for a new code). A screen confirmed but never collected stays `paired-unclaimed` and is never activated after the expiry; a device that is no longer `paired-unclaimed` while its secret is unused (revoked first) is refused. Claims for one pairing run one after the other (the screen is read first and takes its write lock, L85) and the secret is burned before the device is saved, so sixteen racing claims give one token (test).
8. **Audit.** Start writes none: it changes no device, an unauthenticated caller can make it, and every audit entry takes the one chain head's lock, so an entry per start would let that caller stall every audit write. Confirm writes one (`use_case` `PairWallScreen`, action `wall-screen-paired`, the lead as actor, snapshot: name, place, board scope). Claim writes one only when it issues the token (action `wall-screen-claimed`, actor role `wall` with the device id, no practitioner, channel `screen`); a poll writes none. No audit entry, replay record or node holds a code, a secret or a token.
9. **Shapes the prompt leaves open.** `StartPairing.execute()` takes no command (it has no input). `ConfirmPairingResult(device_id, screen_name, replayed)`. A code may be typed "482 913", "482-913" or "482913"; anything else is a wrong code. `rng` is a `random.Random`: production must pass `secrets.SystemRandom()` (a seeded `Random` is for tests). A code already held in the tenant, live, used or expired, is drawn again up to `CODE_ATTEMPTS` (10), then Conflict. The pairing id is the device id, so the code record, the secret record and the device are joined by one id. Replay of a confirmation uses `uow.idempotency` with a fingerprint of the lead, name, place, board scope and the keyed code hash (never the code); the same key for a different confirmation is a Conflict.
10. **Scenarios share the keys.** TEN-005 made the keys composite (`tenant_device_id`), so the three screen statements' scenarios (`Q-SCREEN-GET`, `Q-SCREEN-BY-TOKEN`, `Q-SCREEN-SAVE`) hold the same id, type and token hash in both tenants and lean on no single-property constraint (`CONSTRAINTS_LEANED_ON` stays empty). The save scenario replaces an existing screen with a colliding id. Until TEN-032 drops the single-property `device_id`, a test that wants one id in two tenants outside the generated test cannot (the shared schema refuses it): the tests of this task give each tenant its own ids (`SequentialIds.scoped`). Built first against the single-property schema with `Unique` ids and a create-only save scenario; changed at the rebase onto TEN-005.
11. **Not built, and where it goes.** Revoking a screen (U37), reading the board with the token (R02, R05), the endpoints (R15) and the pages (W08). No retention sweep removes expired link tokens or `paired-unclaimed` screens that were never collected (known-issues). The network board is stored, not read.

Tests: `backend/tests/integration/application/test_pair_wall_screen.py`, `backend/tests/integration/graph_store/test_wall_screen_registry.py`, `backend/tests/unit/domain/model/test_device.py`, `backend/tests/unit/application/use_cases/test_pair_wall_screen_helpers.py`, and the generated `test_tenant_isolation.py`. Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/U10.md`.

## DEC-162 The mapper reserves the tenant names and stays pure; Concept, CodeSystem and Identifier are stamped by the writer (TEN-008, refines DEC-130 item 1 and DEC-134 item 1)
Status: accepted · Affects: `adapters/outbound/graph_store/mapper.py`, `extensions.py`, `backend/tests/unit/adapters/outbound/graph_store/test_mapper.py`, `test_mapper_coded_values.py`, `test_mapper_backbone.py`, `test_extensions.py`, `docs/spec/fhir-data-model.md` (R5, R6), `docs/spec/contracts.md`. Decided by the TEN-008 builder under DEC-133. The task prompt `docs/plan/prompts/TEN-008.md` does not exist; the scope is the TEN-008 entry of `tenancy-plan.md` and section 5 of `tenancy-audit/A-graph-store.md`.

1. **The mapper stays pure and knows no tenant.** `to_graph` takes no tenant and gives the same fragment for every tenant. The writer stamps `tenantId: $tenant` on every node it writes (TEN-009), so the audit's recommendation (A-graph-store 5.1: "stamping lands in the queries, not in the mapper") is followed. A tenant argument on the mapper would be a second source of the tenant beside the bound scope, and a way to write a node under a tenant other than the one the unit of work is bound to.
2. **`tenantId` and `sourceTenantId` are the seam's names, not the resource's.** `TENANT_OWNED_NAMES` (in `extensions.py`, which the mapper already imports) holds both. `to_graph` refuses an element of either name at any depth (the root, a backbone child, a child of a child) with `ValidationFailed(<name>, "reserved: the tenancy seam stamps this name")`, whatever the value's shape (text, number, list, reference, concept, period, quantity, a map holding the name). A name inside `meta`, or as a key of a coding or identifier, was already refused as an unsupported element and still is. The same name as a value (a coding whose code is `tenantId`) is not refused: the reservation is by property name only.
3. **A last check on what leaves the mapper.** `to_graph` refuses a fragment in which any node or relationship property is one of the two names. The element names are refused as they are read, so this check cannot fire on any current path; it is there for the next path someone adds (a copied filter code, a flattened name), and a test drives it through a configured copy and a forged relationship property. The writer's own stamp is therefore the only `tenantId` that can reach a node, and a `SET n = row.props` or `SET n += row.props` of mapper properties cannot overwrite it.
4. **An extension cannot stand in for the stamp.** `ExtensionDef` refuses a short name in `TENANT_OWNED_NAMES` (`ValueError`), so no `ext_tenantId` can be registered. A resource carrying an extension whose URL ends `tenantId` or `sourceTenantId` is refused as an unknown extension, as before, and the test pins it for both names and three value types.
5. **`from_graph` drops both names from every node it rebuilds from** (the root and each backbone child), so a stamped node read from the store is never rebuilt as a `tenantId` element (A-graph-store 5.2). It does not change the fragment it is given. The resource store strips the same names from what it loads (TEN-009: `RESERVED_PROPERTIES`), so there are two strips; the mapper's is the one a test of the mapper alone can prove.
6. **Concept, CodeSystem and Identifier are tenant-scoped, and the mapper carries no "shared" flag.** The mapper has always described them as plain nodes keyed `Concept|system|code`, `CodeSystem|system`, `Identifier|system|value` with nothing but their data; `NodeSpec` has the fields `key`, `labels`, `properties` and a test pins that, and that a shared node holds no tenant and no marker. What makes them tenant-scoped is the writer: TEN-009 stamps them, reads and fills them only inside the bound tenant, and merges them by the composite key `(tenantId, key)` that migration 0031 created. Two tenants therefore hold their own `Concept` for one code, and `display` and `version` never cross (A-graph-store section 4). No behaviour of the mapper changes for them.
7. **What this does not do.** No statement is converted and no id leaves the ratchet: the mapper has no Cypher. The stamp itself, the typed anchors, the shared-node fill and the version nodes are TEN-009. Until that lands a stored node carries no `tenantId`, so nothing about isolation changes on `main` with this task.

## DEC-165 The inbound queue consumers (I08): the tenant is bound from the message, the reply processor holds no unit of work around its use case, a delivery status only moves forward, and the choices the prompt leaves open
Status: accepted (builder build-I08, under DEC-133) · Affects: `adapters/inbound/workers/reply_processor.py`, `delivery_tracker.py`, `tenant_binding.py` (new), `consumer.py` (`MessageConsumer`), `servicebus_runner.py` (reads `tenantId`, `serve`), `bootstrap/workers_main.py` (new), `application/ports/outbound/idempotency.py` (`ProcessedMessages.seen`), `messaging.py` (`statuses_before`), `graph_store/idempotency_store.py` (`Q-PROCESSED-SEEN`), `graph_store/communication_repository.py` (`Q-COMM-SET-STATUS`), `graph_store/connection.py` (`Neo4jConnection.driver`), the `BINDING_MODULES` pin, I04 and I05 (their consumers), I10 (it sets `sent` and `failed`), TEN-007, TEN-011 and TEN-015. Numbered DEC-165 on 2026-10-29: `main` tops at DEC-161, the TEN-007, TEN-008 and TEN-009 branches hold DEC-163, DEC-162 and DEC-164.

1. **The I04 framework does not bind a tenant, so the consumers do (lesson L153 item 4, DEC-149 item 7).** `IdempotentConsumer` opened its unit of work with nothing bound, and `to_bus_message` dropped the `tenantId` application property the bus adapter sends (DEC-130 item 7), so no consumer could know its tenant. Now `to_bus_message` copies the property into `BusMessage.tenant_id` (text or bytes key; a value that is not text is no tenant), and a new `TenantBoundConsumer(inner, tenants, observe)` wraps a consumer: it dead-letters, with one fixed reason each and no tenant id in it, a message with no tenant (`message has no tenant`), a tenant that is not a valid tenant id (a network id `net-...` included: `message tenant is not valid`) and a tenant the directory does not list as active (`message tenant is unknown or not active`; a suspended tenant is not listed, so it reads the same). The inner consumer then never runs and no unit of work opens, scoped or not. A directory that cannot answer is a retry (class name only): it says nothing about the message. For a listed tenant it binds that tenant (`bind_scope`) for the whole handling of the message and restores the binding on every exit. `consumer.py` gains a `MessageConsumer` Protocol (`async handle(message) -> Outcome`) that the runner now accepts, so `IdempotentConsumer` and the wrapper are interchangeable. Every later consumer (I05, U09 and the rest) must be wrapped in `TenantBoundConsumer`; this is a known issue for I04's other users (a lesson is published). The tenant is read only from the message property, which a producer sets from a verified source (I07 from the number and account after the signature check; the outbox relay from the tenant it read the row under); a message payload field is never a tenant.
2. **The directory is `ActiveTenants`, a stand-in until TEN-007.** The wrapper asks `ActiveTenants.active_tenant_ids()` for each message (a list scan: cheap for a few tenants, to be cached when the list is long) and the entry point uses `StaticTenants` from `DIUCS_WORKER_TENANTS`. TEN-007's `container.tenants` satisfies the same Protocol, so `main` swaps `configured_tenants(os.environ)` for `container.tenants` and nothing else changes.
3. **The reply processor is not an `IdempotentConsumer`, because the use case opens its own units of work.** I04 holds one unit of work around its handler and marks the message inside it. `HandleInboundText` opens its own (a read pass, one per write, a last one for its own record), and the graph store refuses a second unit inside an open one (DEC-146 item 4, `UnitOfWorkAlreadyOpen`), so the prompt's "ReplyProcessor as an I04 handler" cannot work. `ReplyProcessor.handle(message)` holds none while the use case runs and keeps the promise in three steps: ask `processed.seen(message_id, "reply-processor")` (a duplicate if true), run the use case, then mark in its own unit of work. A crash or store failure between the run and the mark leaves no marker, the bus delivers again, and the use case answers the second run as a replay by its idempotency key and writes nothing. Two deliveries at once both run the use case (a replay, or a Conflict that is retried) and the second to mark is told the marker is held (a duplicate). Marking after the work, not before, is deliberate: a marker written first and a crash after it would lose a capacity report.
4. **`ProcessedMessages.seen(message_id, consumer) -> bool` and `Q-PROCESSED-SEEN`.** A new port method and one converted read statement (`MATCH (p:Processed {key: $key, tenantId: $tenant}) RETURN count(p) AS found`, scenario in `idempotency_store.py`, run by the generated two-tenant test). It writes nothing and uses the same length-prefixed key as `mark_if_new`, so no two pairs share a marker.
5. **The idempotency key is `sms-reply:<tenant>:<provider_message_id>`, not the bare provider id (a departure from the prompt, lesson L160).** The replay store (`Q-IDEM-*`) is unconverted, so a bare key shared by two tenants would collide and answer the second tenant a Conflict that proves the first tenant's key exists. The key is built in one function (`reply_key`) from the bound tenant, and no tenant bound is an error. TEN-011 converts the store, and the key can then be the bare id. The command has no tenant field, and none is added.
6. **Failures.** A payload that cannot be read is a `PermanentError` with a fixed text that names the field and never the value: not an `SmsReceived` message, no usable `phone_hash`, `text`, `received_at` or `provider_message_id`, a `received_at` that is not an ISO instant with an offset (a wall time is refused). `text` may be any string, a blank one included: the use case answers it. A `ValidationFailed` from the use case (a phone hash that is not a hash, for one) is permanent (`the use case refused the text as invalid`, its own text is not repeated). A `Conflict` is a retry, not a dead letter, because it can be a race with another writer that the next delivery will not meet, and the bus dead-letters after its delivery limit. Every other failure is a retry by class name. A text the use case drops (a phone that is nobody's reporter) is finished, not retried. Logs hold the message id, the outcome word and the class name only: never the text, the phone hash or the provider id.
7. **The delivery tracker is an `IdempotentConsumer` inside the wrapper**, so the bus id (`<sid>:<status>`) is marked in the same unit of work as the change and a redelivery does nothing. Its handler checks the payload (`SmsDeliveryReceived`, a provider id, a status in `queued sent delivered failed`, an error code that is absent or text; otherwise a `PermanentError` with a fixed text, so a poison receipt is dead-lettered and does not hold up the queue) and calls `set_delivery_status`. A receipt for a provider id no Communication holds is completed and dropped, not retried (a receipt can arrive before the send is recorded; another tenant's id reads the same once TEN-015 lands): the log line holds the provider id and the status. The provider's error code is logged with them for staff and is **not stored**: the Communication has no property for it (known issue; a graph-native field would be a model change).
8. **The status order lives in the store, and it is atomic.** `statuses_before(status)` (in `messaging.py`) is queued < sent < delivered and failed, which are final and neither follows the other; `received` and `grouped` are reached from nothing and never move. `Q-COMM-SET-STATUS` now takes the record's write lock first (`SET c.id = c.id`, lessons L73 and L142: the id never changes), reads the status after the lock, and writes only `WHERE c.deliveryStatus IN $movesFrom`, setting the FHIR `status` property with it (it was left `in-progress` after a delivery). `set_delivery_status` therefore returns True only when the record moved, and False for an unknown id or a refused move. A read-then-write in the tracker would have regressed under the runner's four concurrent receipts; the test holds an uncommitted `delivered` and shows a `sent` that waited does not overwrite it. I10 (which sets `sent` and `failed` after the send) gets the same rule for free: a late `sent` cannot overwrite a `delivered`.
9. **Tenant-pending, and the test that flips (TEN-015).** `Q-COMM-BY-PROVIDER` and `Q-COMM-SET-STATUS` match by provider id alone: no Communication node carries `tenantId` yet (`Q-COMM-ADD` does not stamp it, and a converted reader finds nothing the unconverted writer made, lesson L155), so until TEN-015 a receipt that arrives for tenant A can still change tenant B's Communication with the same provider id. Twilio message ids are unique across accounts, so only a forged callback signed with a tenant's own token could do it. I08 does not convert them (the whole repository is TEN-015's, with its placeholders and Concepts under TEN-008 and TEN-009). The test `test_another_tenants_message_id_reads_as_not_found_and_is_dropped` is written and marked `xfail(strict=True)`: it fails today for the reason above, and when TEN-015 converts the statements it passes, strict makes the suite fail, and TEN-015 removes the marker. Likewise the two-tenant reply test cannot seed tenant B's people and services apart (their statements are unconverted too, DEC-149 item 10): it proves what I08 owns, that each text reaches the use case under its own tenant, key and unit-of-work scope, with its own marker.
10. **The same id in two tenants.** The shared harness schema still holds the single-property marker key (until TEN-032), so the tests that put one bus id in both tenants run on `contracted_container()` (a private container with the superseded keys dropped, as the generated two-tenant test does, lesson L157); the others give each tenant its own id.
11. **The entry point.** `bootstrap/workers_main.py`: `python -m diucs.bootstrap.workers_main`. `build_runners(client, handle_inbound_text, uow_factory, tenants)` returns one `ServiceBusRunner` for each of `inbound.replies` and `inbound.receipts`, each over a `TenantBoundConsumer`; `serve(settings, tenants, stop)` opens Neo4j (`container.startup()`), Redis and Service Bus, wires `HandleInboundText` with `ReportCapacity`, `ConfirmCapacity` and a `DriverIdempotencyStore`, and runs them until stopped. The tenants come from `DIUCS_WORKER_TENANTS` (a stand-in, item 2); a missing or invalid list, or no `DIUCS_SERVICEBUS_CONNECTION`, stops the process before any connection opens with a fixed message that never repeats a value. `Neo4jConnection.driver` is added as a read-only property, byte for byte the one TEN-007 adds (so the merge is clean). `ServiceBusRunner.serve` is an alias of `run`: the architecture scan for Cypher execution reads any attribute named `run` outside the graph store, and the entry would have been refused. `serve` was not run end to end (it needs Redis, Service Bus and Neo4j together; its parts are tested), as for the relay (known issue).
12. **Trace context hook (O01).** `TenantBoundConsumer` takes `observe(message) -> context manager`, entered after the tenant is bound and left after the message is handled; the default does nothing. `BusMessage` carries no application properties yet, so O01 adds the `traceparent` property to the message and the runner, and supplies an `observe` that opens a span; no consumer changes.
13. **Binding module.** `diucs.adapters.inbound.workers.tenant_binding` is added to `BINDING_MODULES` (an edge that binds a verified tenant).
14. **Not built.** Alerting on dead-lettered inbound messages (O04); storing a receipt's error code; a retry of a receipt for an id not yet recorded (it is dropped; the send that records the id comes first in practice); the tenant directory itself (TEN-007); converting the Communication, person and idempotency statements (TEN-015, TEN-010, TEN-011).

Tests: `backend/tests/unit/adapters/inbound/workers/test_tenant_binding.py`, `test_reply_processor.py`, `test_delivery_tracker.py`, `test_servicebus_runner.py`, `backend/tests/unit/application/ports/outbound/test_delivery_order.py`, `backend/tests/unit/bootstrap/test_workers_main.py`, `backend/tests/integration/adapters/inbound/workers/test_reply_processor.py`, `test_delivery_tracker.py`, `test_inbound_two_tenants.py`, `backend/tests/integration/graph_store/test_communication_repository.py` and `test_idempotency_store.py`, and the generated `test_tenant_isolation.py`. Evidence: `docs/delivery/evidence/I08.md`.
